# 11 — Decision log, Builder decisions (living, dated)

> The one place where Builders record the small, reversible choices a milestone file leaves open (DL-341). Architect decisions stay in `spec/11-decision-log*.md` parts 1–4; Builders never edit those. One row per decision, newest last, ids `BD-001` onward in run order. A row never changes a contract shape: a contract change is a proposal in the milestone report. When this file nears the 6,000-word cap (`tools/ci/spec-files.ts`), the Architect moves the oldest rows to `spec/11-decision-log-builder-2.md` and leaves a pointer here.

> **Earlier rows:** Builder decisions for M00 … M01f, recorded on `claude/build` before this file existed, are in `spec/11-decision-log-builder-2.md` (M00, M00b, M01a1, M00c, M01a2) and `spec/11-decision-log-builder-3.md` (M01d, M01b1, M01c, M01f), ids `BD-M<id>-NN`. When a Builder merges `claude/architect-spec` into a code branch and `spec/11-decision-log.md` conflicts, take the spec side: the code branch's Builder section is already in those two files. New rows go in the table below only.

## Builder decisions

| ID | Milestone | Decision | Why | Reversible by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BD-001 | M01e | Playwright WebKit's default context has no OPFS (`navigator.storage.getDirectory()` throws `UnknownError`; observed on webkit-2370, Linux). The `@pack-*` specs run in a persistent context on a throwaway profile directory (`launchPersistentContext`); the app itself falls back to the 48 MB memory store there. The M00a/M01c `@telemetry` test expects `app.start.store` `opfs` on Chromium and `memory` on `webkit-webgl2`. | AC-5 needs real OPFS on WebKit. A persistent context has it (probed: root ok, `createWritable` present; `createSyncAccessHandle` exists in workers only). The ephemeral result is the case R-PLAT-01 rule 6 describes (no OPFS), so it is also covered. | Give the WebKit project a persistent context in `playwright.config.ts` and drop the per-project expectation. |
| BD-002 | M01e | QA builds read the catalog from the same-origin mirror: pointer `/media-local/asset-catalog.json` (written by `--pointer-out apps/web/media-local`), media base `${origin}/media-local`, and prefer it to `hosts.media` when present. Production builds use `hosts.media` and `/asset-catalog.json`, and are `disabled` while `hosts.media` is empty. The string `/media-local` reaches the client only through `InitParams.localMedia`, set under the compile-time `VITE_DV_QA === '1'` literal, so `verify:dist` can reject it. | `HOST_MEDIA` in `wrangler.jsonc` holds placeholder hosts (`tha-bay-media.workers.dev`), not empty strings as the milestone assumed, so "use `hosts.media` when non-empty" would fetch a host that does not exist. The committed `apps/web/public/asset-catalog.json` is also stale against the M01f lock (its git stamp differs), which the mirror's own pointer avoids. | Delete the `localMedia` branch in `catalog-client.ts` once `media.<domain>` exists (Q-15). |
| BD-003 | M01e | The catalog loads on first use (an `AssetRef` `get`, `has`, `resolve`, `gc`, `downloadAll`, or the bridge's `catalogState()`), not at boot. `catalogState` takes the values `idle`, `loading`, `ready`, `invalid`, `disabled`, `unavailable`. | A production boot would otherwise request the unprovisioned media host every time. `invalid` is kept for a failed pointer or SHA check as specified; `unavailable` is a fetch failure; `disabled` is no media base. | Call `loadCatalog()` from `createPlatform`. |
| BD-004 | M01e | `@dv/shared` gets a subpath export `./assets` (C07 `c07.ts`). The barrel does not export C07. | The engine needs `resolveVariant` and the catalog types. Adding the export to the barrel risks name clashes with other milestones' edits to it. No contract shape changed. | Re-export from the barrel and change the imports. |
| BD-005 | M01e | `pin(hashes)` adds to a per-session pin set and sets the row flag; `open()` clears persisted pin flags; `unpinAll()` is an extra method on the class and the client. | The interface has no `unpin`, and a pin left from an old manifest would block eviction forever. The loader declares the boot set again on every boot. | Persist pins and add `unpin`. |
| BD-006 | M01e | The store's clock is strictly increasing (`max(now, last + 1)`), and LRU order is `lastUsedAt`, then `createdAt`, then hash. A commit failure (quota with nothing left to evict, or an IO error) does not fail `get`: the verified bytes are returned uncached and a `commit-failed` event is raised. | Two packs touched in one millisecond would otherwise tie. A cache that cannot be written is not a failed read. | Throw `PackUnavailable('quota')` from `get`. |
| BD-007 | M01e | Tabs: `hello` with `(startedAt, id)`, an `owner` answer from a writer (or from an older tab that is still probing), a 200 ms probe window. A read-only tab stays read-only until it reloads. A read-only tab still fetches and verifies packs it lacks, and returns them uncached. | A dying writer cannot hand over without a lock service; reload is enough for v1. The notice is non-blocking. Cross-tab handle behaviour stays UNVERIFIED (U-PLT-16). | Add a `bye` message so a read-only tab can retry the lock. |
| BD-008 | M01e | The shared fetch for a hash ignores any one caller's `AbortSignal`: an aborting caller stops waiting (`aborted`) and the request still completes and caches the pack. Joined callers each get their own copy of the bytes. | One caller's abort must not fail the others, and the worker transfers (detaches) what it returns. | Pass the signal of the first caller to the shared fetch (and accept that it fails the joined callers). `FetchRetry` already aborts the network request when no waiter is left. |
| BD-009 | M01e | `window.__dvBridge.assets` is installed without a `Window` type augmentation (a cast in `bridge.ts`). `get` returns `{byteLength, sha256}`, not the bytes. The e2e specs import the `AssetsBridge` type directly. | `Window.__dvBridge` is declared in `platform/probe.ts`; changing that declaration collides with the parallel M00d and M01b2 edits. `evaluate` cannot return an `ArrayBuffer`. | Extend the `Window` declaration in `probe.ts`. |
| BD-010 | M01e | The QA build step is `apps/web/scripts/qa-media.mjs` (run by the root `build` script as `pnpm --filter @dv/web run media:local`); it does nothing unless `VITE_DV_QA=1`. `closeBundle` of the QA-only Vite plugin copies `apps/web/media-local/` to `dist/media-local/`, and `vite dev` serves it through middleware. The service worker precache ignores `media-local/**`. | `assets:publish` needs `--env`; `staging` is used because `dir:` ignores it. | Move the step into the Vite plugin's `buildStart`. |
| BD-011 | M01e | The `app.start` event waits for the asset worker's `open()` with a 5 s cap. On timeout or failure the worker is dropped, `Platform.assets` is null and the event says `store: 'memory'`. | A hung IndexedDB must not stop the game from starting. | Remove the race. |
| BD-012 | M01e | `storage.persist` is requested by `createPlatform` on the first standalone launch (marker `dv-storage-persist` in `localStorage`); `requestPersist('story')` exists for M05a1. | The milestone puts the call in the store layer but the only caller in v1 is the platform boot. | Move the call into `asset.worker`. |
| BD-M01b2-01 | Default world and `?world=` | The page boots the `civic-graybox` scenario (the M01f fixture) unless a QA build passes `?world=<id>` (`walk-drop`, `physics-drop`); `QaFlags.world` and `QaFlags.touch` (`?touch=1`) are new. The title at "/" stays mounted over the live world (translucent, canvas behind it) and Start hides it; `?debug=1` skips the title. The four M01b1/M01a2 e2e specs that need the cube course now pass `world=walk-drop`. | The milestone says the home page opens on the block and the owner wants it visible; the older specs measure the walking course. | Drop the `world` flag and point the specs at the gray-box |
| BD-M01b2-02 | `quantAxis` rounding | Sign-symmetric: `round(abs(v) * 127)` with the sign restored. `Math.round` alone gives -63 and +64 for the half-radius touch stick, and AC-2 asks for 64 either way. Only exact .5 products differ. | AC-2 (29 px of a 58 px radius is 64). | Revert `quantize.ts` |
| BD-M01b2-03 | Touch controls in C03 terms | The stick writes four analog half-axes `touch:btn:moveRight/Left/Down/Up` plus `touch:btn:sprint` (inside the `touch:btn:${string}` member of `ControlId`); the router treats `touch:` axis bindings as analog like `pad:` ones. Look drag goes into `RawInputState.lookDelta` in pixels; `applyLook` reads it through the `touch:dragR` bindings (3.0 x 0.0025 rad per px x `lookSensitivity.touch`) when touch is the last active device, and through the mouse bindings otherwise. No contract change. | C03 has one `touch:stick:L` id and no way to carry two axes. | Move the half-axes to a C03 minor bump |
| BD-M01b2-04 | Touch details left open | A button reads as held for at least 120 ms (the press edge is still latched once); sprint switches on beyond 1.35 x radius and off below 1.25 x (hysteresis); `TouchLayout.zone` is a rectangle in viewport fractions (S10 leaves `Rect` open, note for M04b2); the layout's buttons are Jump, Camera and Pause only. Pointer Events are used; TouchEvents are the fallback and are ignored once a touch-typed pointer event was seen. Playwright WebKit keeps `pointerType` on synthetic events, so the fallback did not run in CI. | C03 rule 10 numbers; Risks. | Edit `touch.ts` and `default-right.json` |
| BD-M01b2-05 | When the layer shows | Only in play, and when the last active device is touch, `(pointer: coarse)` matches or `?touch=1` is set. The binder listens on the layer, not on `window`, so a hybrid laptop does not switch to touch by itself. Keyboard input is ignored until play starts, so Space and Tab keep working on the title. | Title buttons and links must stay usable. | Attach the binder to `window` |
| BD-M01b2-06 | Vault and footfalls | `registerControlSystems` adds a `vault` resource whose hash writes nothing while no vault runs; `walk-drop` opts out so its goldens (`determinism.walk-drop.json`) are unchanged. With a vault store the character does not auto-step in the air, so a 1.0 m jump plus the 0.4 m step cannot clear a 1.4 m box. The footfall cue is `audio.cue` / `foot.step` from `Anim.sinceTick` modulo 30, 18 and 14 ticks (walk, run, sprint): no new state, and events are not hashed. | AC-6 and the golden rule. | Remove the option and regenerate the goldens |
| BD-M01b2-07 | F3 and F4 | F3 is `client.DebugToggle` in `defaults.kbm.json` (on foot and in the `debug` context); F4 is a QA-only key listener, because C03 1.1 has one debug `ClientAction`. Proposal for the Architect: add `ClientAction.FreeCamToggle`. The free cam pushes the `debug` context, which binds no axis, so the sim sees neutral input. On a touch device `?debug=1` shows the panel from the start. | The contract has no second action; no keyboard on a phone. | Add the action and bind F4 |
| BD-M01b2-08 | Pack delivery | `apps/web` imports the two fixture packs with Vite `?url` (hashed; the 736-byte `l0` pack is inlined as a data URL by Vite's size rule), the page fetches them, and the `p0` bytes are cloned into `BootPayload.config.packs` (53 KB, not transferred, because a crash restart sends the payload again). The service worker precaches `*.pack`. The provenance record of the `p0` pack also lists the built path `assets/p0-64-64-<vite hash>.pack`, which `provenance:check` needs (the name changes with the bytes, as the record does). M01e replaces this with the pack store. | The milestone says "hashed static files". | Point `grayboxBootConfig` at the store |
| BD-M01b2-09 | IWER and three r186 | IWER 2.6.0 returns `null` for `XRWebGLLayer.framebuffer`, and three's WebGL backend uses it as a WeakMap key on every XR frame (TypeError). The QA-only `installIwer` gives each layer an offscreen framebuffer with colour and depth renderbuffers. A real headset passes its own framebuffer. Not yet run on a real Quest. | AC-9 needs the emulated session to render. | Drop the shim when IWER returns a framebuffer |
| BD-M01b2-10 | Audio API details | `duck(source, db, attackMs, releaseMs)` ramps the buses `config/audio.json` lists for `source` (voice: music and radio) and `unduck(source)` ramps them back over the release time. `unlock()` resumes the context once; visibility adds one `suspend` on hidden and one `resume` on visible (so a full cycle after the unlock is 2 resumes in all). Voices are capped by the tier's `audioVoices`. | AC-8 wording. | Edit `audio-service.ts` |
| BD-M01b2-11 | Gray-box look | One shared double-sided TSL node material with vertex colours; terrain is one coloured quad per 4 m cell (tinted by ground class, faint checker), roads are ribbons with lane lines, buildings are merged extrusions with a hue per record, trees and lamps are merged columns: 3 to 5 objects per chunk. Sky colour and fog (180 to 640 m) hide the block edge. While the title is up the world renders at 0.6 x and turns slowly (not under reduced motion). | Visible progress for the owner; at most 6 objects per chunk. | Edit `mesher.ts` and `createView` |
| BD-M01b2-12 | Camera polish | The mesh fades from 0 at 0.6 m to 1 at 1.0 m from the head; the shoulder blend is a smoothstep over 0.25 s; `shake` is a presentation wobble of 0.05 m per unit of `amp`, decaying by `decayPerSec`, zero under reduced motion and in XR; near = max(near, far / 20000) with `depthPolicy` `'standard'` (assumed when unreported); far is the tier's `buildingDrawDistanceM`. | The spec gives the endpoints only. | Edit `rig.ts` |
| BD-M01b2-13 | Anim graph | Ids 0 to 3 (idle, walk, run, sprint) share one `blend1d` object and count as one layer, so a speed change is not a crossfade; jump, fall, land and vault are single clips; crossfade weights rise linearly over the new state's `fadeInSec` (0.2 s). `prop/graybox.capsule` now draws the procedural mannequin (`Pose.animP0` was added to the interpolated pose). Foot IK is not built (`footIk` is in the tier config for M11). | The spec names the blend points and the fade time. | Edit `graph.ts` |
| BD-M01b2-14 | Pause, title and headless | Pause toggles the pause and shows the title over the world again; Start resumes. The page boots the engine with a view under `?xr-emulate=quest3` (it was headless there); only `?probe=gpu` stays headless. `verify-dist` also fails a production build that carries the sim panel, the free cam or the XR smoke button. | The XR smoke needs the view; QA tools stay out of production. | Revert `main.ts` |
| BD-M00d-01 | M00d | Package-local `test` and `test:*` scripts run `tsx <rel>/tools/ci/run.ts vitest --pkg packages/<pkg> [<spec stem>]`. `run.ts` maps `--pkg <dir>` to the root Vitest config (so root-relative include globs still apply) and turns each positional name into the path filter `<dir>/test/<name>`, or `<dir>/test/` when none is given; `--dir` was rejected because it re-roots the include globs and silently drops every `*.spec.ts`. `pnpm --filter @dv/shared test -- detmath` now runs only `detmath.vectors.spec.ts` (19 tests) and `pnpm --filter @dv/engine test -- headless` only `headless.noDom.spec.ts` | CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md promise that the filter works; a plain `vitest run` ran inside the package without the root config and ignored it | Replace the package scripts; `mapPkgFilter` is exported and unit-tested in `tools/ci/test/deploy-sweep.test.ts` |

Real files still need `# <id> — <slug>` with the id matching the file name | The AC-3 fixtures are named `good-ready.md` and so on | Tighten the regex in `parseStatusTable` |

| BD-M00d-04 | M00d | A command proof's oracle is the explicit `[oracle: x]` suffix, else the first inferred oracle that is not `static-gate` (all `static-gate` when none is), because `CommandProof` holds one oracle and chains such as `pnpm lint && pnpm check:unit` mix kinds. The per-command list stays available as `ParsedAc.oracles` | C15 1.1 has one `oracle` per proof | `inferOracle` and `parseAc` in `tools/ci/md-parse.ts` |

The registry for file X is 08 section 6, the 06 section 5 Scripts bullets and the Deliverables of every milestone at or before X in `_PLAN.md` order; `check:agents` accepts the Deliverables of every milestone (so `pnpm missions:build` passes) | 08 section 6 alone misses scripts that only a milestone declares; all 119 READY files pass L1-L7 with this reading and the four bad fixtures each fail with exactly their rule | Narrow `declaredScripts` |

| BD-M05b1-14 | Deploy path on push | The staging job (push to main) failed at `wrangler versions upload` after PR #3 because only a PR label selected the `wrangler deploy` path. `deploy:prod -- --auto-lifecycle` (`tools/ci/deploy-decide.ts`, wired in `deploy.ts`) now runs the `check:do-migrations` evaluation (`collectChanges` + `evaluate`) over `<base>..HEAD` and takes the plain deploy path when `lifecycle` is true, even if the range also holds runtime changes (versions cannot carry a lifecycle change, and `ok` only matters for PRs). Base order: `--base`/`DV_DEPLOY_BASE`, `github.event.before` (`DV_EVENT_BEFORE`) when it is a real commit, the previous `v*` tag (tag and manual runs), `HEAD^`; a shallow checkout with none of these is an error. `deploy.yml` fetches full history and passes `--auto-lifecycle` in both jobs; `workflow_dispatch` gets a `base` input. `--do-lifecycle` and `DV_DO_LIFECYCLE=1` (the PR label path) are unchanged. Limit: the base is the previous push, not the last successful deploy, so after a failed deploy re-run that run (same `before`) or dispatch with `base` set to the last deployed commit. | A push has no label; the range of commits is the only signal. | Drop `--auto-lifecycle` from `deploy.yml`. |

A record schema above the build's trips it with `NEWER_SCHEMA`. Writes inside one service are serialised in a queue. `createProfile` does not activate. | The interface has no session argument. Incoming tombstones are copied only by `asNew`. Deleting a profile keeps its tombstones. A new write after `deleteSlot` is a new life of the slot at a generation above the retired one. | The interface returns only `SaveResult`. | Change `lastImport` into a richer return in a contract minor. |

Tombstones are purged 180 days after `deletedAt` only when `deletedAt <= markTombstonesSynced(id, upTo)`. | C05 says "since a cursor" without a unit. Stamps are unique per device, so `>` loses nothing. | Replace with a counter store when M12a1 needs cross-device order. |

Ring vertices on the box perimeter move 4 m outward so the last post row and column stay land. | BD-CI-01 | CI repair (M00b, M01e) | **`provenance:check` and the QA mirror.** The dist walk skips `media-local/**` (`QA_MIRROR_PREFIX`), as DL-667 (part 2) specifies: it is the QA-only same-origin copy of the M01d mirror (production builds never ship it, M01e AC-10), its blobs are the lock's variants (joined by the lock walk) and its catalog, manifests and pointer are derived files checked by `assets:verify` and `check:assets`. Outside `media-local/`, a dist file whose basename is a C07 object key (`<variant>.<sha256[0:12]>.<ext>`) whose hash fragment equals its content hash matches its record by hash, so a record that lists the source path no longer reports `path-not-recorded`; a file that only imitates a key (wrong fragment) still does, and a manifest copied to any other dist path still reports `no-record`. New cases in `tools/ci/test/gates.test.ts`. | The ip job failed with 13 findings (11 `path-not-recorded`, 2 `no-record`) on every QA build since M01e merged, because the gate wanted the record's `paths` to name the published key. The e2e job on CI serves the downloaded dist, so the same spec now works in both setups. The describe block also gets `timeout: 120_000`: each test loads the app twice, and on the GPU-less sandbox a load takes about 20 s, so the 30 s default expired (all five pass in 24 to 50 s each). | `expectedFor` threw ENOENT at three tests on CI. | Revert to a file read if the mirror ever moves out of the served dist. |
| BD-CI-03 | CI repair (M01b2, M01b1) | **Boot specs: tick rate by the sim's own accounting, in the small viewport.** Cause: the sim is paid by the page's frames and C02 caps a frame at 9 ticks (0.15 s). On the GPU-less runner the software WebGPU adapter renders the default 1280x720 view at about 9 fps (5 to 11 in a 1 s window), so slow frames hit the clamp and ticks fell to 36 to 62 a second (`droppedSec` counted the rest); the sim itself was fine, and at 480x270 the same adapter gives 30 to 47 fps and 59 to 66 ticks a second. `boot.spec.ts` now sets `SMALL_VIEWPORT` (as `graybox.spec.ts` and `xr-smoke.spec.ts` already do) and replaces `b.tick - a.tick >= 50` over 1 s with `measureTicks`/`fixedRateViolations` in `engine-helpers.ts`: a window that starts and ends on a stats report, then: ticks > 0; ticks / 60 plus the dropped time the sim reports is within 70% to 130% (+0.5 s) of wall time (a first run with 85% to 115% failed once on a loaded runner, where the report is seen a frame or two after it arrives); every clamped frame ran its 9 ticks; and with nothing dropped, at least 45 ticks a second (the old count was 50 over a wall-clock second; a webkit run measured 47.3 because the report is seen a frame late, while undisturbed runs read 58.8 to 63.5). At the default viewport (6 fps) the accounting still held (21.5 ticks/s plus 1.38 s dropped) while the stall check's frame count did not, which is why the small viewport stays. The tests are not skipped or quarantined. | Keeps the AC (the sim advances at the fixed rate and survives a stall) without a wall-clock guess that depends on GPU speed. | Revert both files to restore the fixed `>= 50` count. |

A named main span (Golden Gate 67 m, Bay Bridge 58 m, `config/world/bridges.json`) reaches its deck height only if the span is long enough for the 6 % ramps to climb from the shore (a 3 km test span does; a short one peaks lower, because the ramp limit wins over the named height). | The spec gives the limits, not how to meet them at pinned ends. | Edit `s7-grading.ts`. |

| BD-M02b1-01 | One ChunkStore for sim and page | `ChunkStore<P>` is a generic synchronous state machine (no clock, no IO; the host injects `now`, `request`, `exists`). The sim never meshes and the page never reads colliders. | 02-world.md section 3 names one `ChunkStore` but the p0 statics must stay inside the deterministic sim worker while meshing is main-thread work. | Split the class per side; the tests exercise the shared core. |

Requests are `{ seq, ref, priority, refetch? }`, replies `{ seq, bytes }` or `{ seq, error: { reason } }`; the sim decodes on receipt and applies replies at the start of the Streaming stage in `seq` order (DL-678). | A restarted sim worker needs a fresh channel; replies must not change tick results by arriving mid-tick. | Route requests through the main thread (one extra hop). |

M02b1 Scope says "Preview builds stay without a world" and AC-10 deploys a production build to a Preview; this changes the QA Preview on the owner's instruction. The real `sf` release to staging stays with M02c2 (DL-670), which ignores `release.json` for `deploy:preview` and every CI build. | Delete the cache, bake and `DV_WORLD` lines of `preview.yml`; the Preview returns to the gray-box scene. |
| BD-M02b1-23 | xr-quest3 platform spec on the world build | The `@xr` spec in `platform.spec.ts` (enter immersive-vr under IWER, 10 XR frames, `webgl2`) keeps every assertion and gets `test.setTimeout(120_000)` with a 90 s poll. On the `DV_WORLD=synth` build the page boots the streamed world beside the emulated session; on the software renderer that took 20 to 41 s against the 30 s default, so the first attempt timed out (it passed on the retry, and failed outright under load). The other xr-quest3 failure of the same leg, `@xr-smoke` waiting for the gray-box `world.chunks === 4`, was already fixed on `claude/build` (d15f225: it asserts the baked world's reveal); with that spec world-aware it stays in the world half and `GRAYBOX_GREP` is unchanged. | The `xr-quest3` job is advisory but must be green; the XR criterion is unchanged, only the budget for the larger boot. | Drop the `setTimeout` line and the longer poll. |
| BD-SFBAKE-01 | OSM mirrors (DL-703) | `sources.json` source `osm` keeps Geofabrik NorCal as `url` and gains `mirrors` (`{url, coverage: play|california, note?}`, BBBike SF then openstreetmap.fr California). `tools/map/src/sources/osm.ts` `ensureOsmPbf` tries the primary and then each mirror on any failure (reset, HTTP status, truncated body), downloads to `osm-source.osm.pbf.part`, renames, and writes `osm-source.json` (url, sha256, bytes, route index, retrievedAt) beside it; a PBF with its sidecar is reused. The raw lock entry for `osm` takes `url` and `retrievedAt` from the serving route, keeps `sha256` as the hash of the exported GeoJSON lines the bake reads, and adds an optional `extract {sha256, bytes, route}` for the PBF bytes. In the real run Geofabrik answered, so the mirror path is covered by `tools/map/test/fetch.mirrors.spec.ts` (injected downloader) and by a manual run of the BBBike file through the same osmium commands. DataSF already used `data.sf.gov` (`data.sfgov.org` answers 301 to it), so no host change. | The lock must say which URL served the bytes; hashing the export keeps `sourceHashes` (bakeId) a function of the data the bake reads. | Drop `mirrors` and `ensureOsmPbf`. |
| BD-SFBAKE-02 | S5 cuts ways at the area grid | `clipWayToBox` (`s5-roads.ts`) cuts OSM ways at the chunk-aligned grid box, 0.5 m inside the edge (S5 quantises node positions to 0.25 m, so a 1 mm inset still rounded into the next chunk), with `cut:` vertex keys. The code comment already promised the cut; only synth and the excerpt had run, and the real extract (sf bbox reaches past the `play` box, plus complete_ways) left 32,707 road records in chunks without terrain and S13 threw. TOOL_VERSION not bumped: no byte of the synth or excerpt bakes changes. | Needed to bake the real city at all. | Remove the `clipWayToBox` call; S13 then fails on any real extract. |
| BD-SFBAKE-03 | S2 bridge chunk sampling | The `extra` chunks that get a terrain grid for bridge decks are sampled every 8 m along each bridge way (was 64 m). A deck clipping a chunk corner by less than 64 m had no sample inside, so 237 slabs and hoods on the real city fell outside the terrain (`s7.deck-chunks`); 0 after the change. | Same defect class as BD-SFBAKE-02, found by the same run. | Restore the 64 m step. |

Terrain Tiles was accessed on DATE from https://registry.opendata.aws/terrain-tiles" to the Terrarium text (DATE = UTC date of its `retrievedAt`); without the lock the text is unchanged. This was M02a1 AC-9's last open item. `config/attribution.world.json` regenerated. | The acceptance text asks for the citation with the committed lock's date; the generator did not read the lock. | Revert `terrariumAccessDate` and regenerate. |

M02a2 gates fail at design values and were not lowered (DL-669): largest SCC 93.77 % (98 %), max grade 45 % (40 %), slab step 0.20 m (0.16 m), portal routes 72 of 77, hero edges Bay Bridge west span and Yerba Buena tunnel outside the main component, cable-car chains, GG bridge arrival pose, crissy-field 225 m and candlestick-point 369 m from their OSM feature, 35 POI seed findings. | BD-SFBAKE-06 | Not done | R2 publish (`map:pack -- --target r2:...`, `--archive`) and `assets/world/sf.lock.json` skipped for lack of R2 keys; `map:pack -- --target dir:` and `DV_WORLD=sf VITE_DV_QA=1 pnpm build` ran (404 `world_pack` files, 75,770,903 B; with the graph, index and transit data 407 files, 81.66 MB). `.github/workflows/preview.yml` untouched. | Task scope. | n/a |
| BD-M02b2-01 | Where the clock is installed | `installClock` runs in `SimRuntime.boot` (the page-hosted sim) and not in `createPhysicsSim`, so the headless sim, the determinism suite and its goldens are unchanged. It registers the resource `clock`, the system `engine.clock.step` (Rules, order 900) and sets `services.clock`; `SimRuntimeEnv.clock: false` leaves it out. The clock asks for the `weather` stream at each draw instead of keeping it, because a checkpoint restore replaces the stream objects. `EnvState` goes in the snapshot side block under `env`, a module's `side()` entries after it. | The spec says the clock is a hashed sim resource and part of `snapshot()` and names no host; installing it in the factory would have moved every golden hash. | Call `installClock` from `createPhysicsSim` and regenerate the goldens (needs a DL entry, `golden-guard`). |
| BD-M02b2-02 | Clock rules the spec leaves open | `lock` freezes `t` (and with it the weather schedule), a second owner is ignored while a lock stands, `setHour` and `forceWeather` are ignored while locked, and an empty owner name is refused. `setHour` keeps the day and shifts `changeAtT` and the blend start by the same amount, so setting the hour never changes the weather. `forceWeather(w, 0)` switches at once, also on a paused clock; a longer blend runs on the clock. The weather change draws the interval first (20 to 40 game minutes, `config/clock.json`) and then the preset by integer weight with `current` excluded; Karl and golden carry an hour bias. A change at `changeAtT` makes `next` current and starts the following interval from `changeAtT`, not from the tick that took it. The default blend is the last 8 game minutes of the interval. `night` and `dark` are the tables in `config/clock.json`. | The contract gives the shapes and the 20 to 40 minute interval only. | Edit `clock.ts` and `config/clock.json`. |
| BD-M02b2-03 | Night half of the sun | `sunDirection` follows the spec for 06:30 to 19:30 (`a = pi (h - 6.5) / 13`, elevation `52 deg * sin a`) and carries on from `a = pi` at 19:30 to `a = 2 pi` at 06:30, so the direction is continuous at midnight (the spec formula alone jumps there) and the sun is below the horizon at night; the moon is its opposite at 0.4. | `sky.continuity.spec` (AC-4) found a 0.16 normalized jump at midnight with the day formula alone. | Replace the night branch in `sky-keys.ts`. |
| BD-M02b2-04 | Window threshold without a new attribute | The per-building window threshold (0.15 to 0.75) is a hash of the baked wall colour in the shader. The mesher tints each building by `jitter(seed)`, so the colour is a function of the record `seed`; no vertex attribute is added, so the upload byte caps and the mesh buffer sizes of M02b1 stay as they were. The lit fraction is `max(smoothstep(0.1, 0.9, uNight), uWindowLit)` against the threshold, so a dark rain lights some windows by day; the blackout takes the glow to 0.15. | A 4-byte attribute on every vertex would have moved the budgets the M02b1 tests hold. | Add an `aLit` attribute in the mesher and read it in `windowEmissive`. |
| BD-M02b2-05 | Debug and bridge surface | New `DebugCommand`s `debug.hour {h, pause?}`, `debug.weather {w, blendSec}`, `debug.clock.pause {on}` and `debug.blackout {on}` (QA builds). `SimModule` gains `side()` and `onDebug()`; the world module answers `debug.blackout` with the owner `debug` and publishes `bo: 1` while it holds. The page reads `env` and `bo` into `SnapshotView` and `WorldFrame` (`env`, `blackout`). `__dvBridge.world` gains `setHour(h, freeze = true)`, `setWeather`, `blackout`, `freeze`, `rain`, `sky()` and `luma(pitch, yaw, {passes, pixels})`; the sky controls (hour, weather, blackout, rain, freeze) sit in the debug panel and show with `?debug=1`. `?sky=0` (QA) draws without the sky. `Rain` is a client-side override of the streak intensity (Karl fog in the rain for the device checklist). | The spec names the bridge calls and the panel controls and not the plumbing; none of it exists in a production build. | Remove the commands; the bridge calls then need another path. |
| BD-M02b2-06 | Fog | The world pass uses `scene.fogNode` (three 0.186.1 `Fog.js`, checked in the installed source): linear between the keyed near and far on the mobile tiers (Quest also caps the far end at 1,500 m), and on the desktop tiers and Vision Pro that times a height factor (everyday haze scale 700 m; in a Karl preset full below the marine layer and 60 m above it). `bankWeight(x)` multiplies the fog by the preset's Karl share `uBank = fogBankM / 240`, so only Karl fog is heavier in the west. The backdrop pass uses the same colour with the distances stretched 6 and 5 times so the far ring reads in clear weather and disappears in Karl fog. | The spec gives the weights and the tier kinds, not the distance model; one factor per tier kind keeps the nodes small. | Change `fog.ts` and the rows in `sky-keys.json`. |
| BD-M02b2-07 | Probe | The sky probe re-bakes the hemisphere lights' colours (the sky-derived ambient) only on the probe-grid tiers (mobile, mobile-low, Vision Pro, Quest); the desktop tiers follow every frame. A change of the weather pair (`weather`, `next`) also forces the next bake, still no sooner than 200 ms after the last, because a forced preset at a frozen clock moves neither the sun nor the darkness. | A QA preset change with the clock frozen would otherwise leave the ambient stale. | Remove `invalidate()` in `SkyView.update`. |
| BD-M02b2-08 | Street lamps | S9 writes `l0` LAMP records every `lampPitchM` (30 m, `config/world/density.json`) on both sides of each Arterial and Collector segment, 1.5 m outside the carriageway edge, centred between 10 m clear zones at the ends of the segment, none on bridges or tunnels (the LAMP record has no height; the renderer puts it on the terrain) and none on water; ordered by x then z. The mesher returns them as `UnitMesh.lamps` (local x, y, z) and `NightLights` draws the nearest 1,400 (desktop tiers), 700 or 350 within 700 or 380 m as three render objects in all (instanced poles with emissive heads, additive halo sprites, pool-of-light decals up to the tier cap). | The spec fixes pitch and classes; one shared set keeps the render-object count flat whatever the chunk count. | Edit `placeLamps` and `night.ts`; re-bake. |
| BD-M02b2-09 | Sky structure | The dome is a back-faced sphere in the backdrop pass (the pass the host draws first; it is not drawn in XR, so the XR sky arrives with M11b1), the rain streaks, the overlay and the night lights are in the world pass. `SkyUniforms` gains `uStars`, `uSunSize`, `uWindowLit` and `uCloud` beyond the spec's twelve. Clouds are a painted fbm layer where the tier has one; `volumetric` (desktop-ultra) and `world.atmosphere` are interfaces only (`AtmosphereAdapter`, M10d2). Splashes are in the matrix as data and not drawn. TSL is used through a loose typing layer (`sky/tsl.ts`) because three's static node types reject the mixed shapes the shader code uses. | Keeps the first sky small and removable. | Replace the dome or add the adapter. |
| BD-M02b2-10 | Luminance gates | `luma()` renders into a cached 128 x 72 byte target (not tone mapped: linear light) from the camera position, and the gates read the mean and the variance of 0.2126 R + 0.7152 G + 0.0722 B. "Sky luminance" is the backdrop pass alone looking 40 degrees up; "city" is both passes from 150 m above the camera, looking west and 8 degrees down over the roofs (a view along the street left the near dark walls to dominate the variance; the east is lightly fogged by `bankWeight`, so the westward view is the one the Karl bank shapes). Karl fog is thick by design: linear fog, near 0 m, far 80 m by day. The keys were tuned against these. | The spec says "a 128 px readback" without naming the target or the view. | Change the views in `world-sky.spec.ts`; re-tune `sky-keys.json`. |
| BD-M02b2-11 | `run.ts` filter check | `run.ts` exits 1 with `run.ts: no spec under <dir>/test matches <name>` for each name filter that no spec path contains; `pkgSelection`, `listSpecs` and `unmatchedNames` are exported and tested in `deploy-sweep.test.ts`. M00d's merged `--pkg` form was as the milestone describes it, so no other change. | DL-674 part 2, AC-10. | Revert the check in `main()`. |
| BD-SFFIX-01 | S5 one-way rule (DL-705) | A matched row gives a one-way only when the OSM piece is two-way, has a `name`, and the row's `layer` is exactly `STREETS` (not `STREETS_YBI`, `STREETS_TI`, `FREEWAYS`); an OSM one-way is never reversed. The orientation scoring is unchanged (all matched pieces with an OSM one-way, unnamed ones included: 7,007 pairs, 97.0 % F-along). `roads.oneway` gains `applied` (370), `osmKept` (6,224), `osmKeptAgainstDsf` (108), `unnamedSkipped` (1,206) and `otherLayerSkipped` (37); the `s5.oneway` gate row prints them. | The spec text left the pair set open; keeping it unchanged keeps the measured U-W15 ratio comparable with the first run. Undo: relax the three conditions in the adoption loop of `runRoads`. |
| BD-SFFIX-02 | S6 box-clip turnarounds (DL-705) | S5 marks a node made by a `cut:` vertex with the box side (`NodeB.clip` 1 west, 2 east, 3 north, 4 south). S6 pairs each sink (clip node with in-edges only) with the nearest source (out-edges only) on the same side within 150 m, then each source that no sink chose with its nearest sink (+2 edges in the largest SCC). A pair is one `DeadEnd` junction whose movements run from the sink's in-edge to the source's out-edge as `uturn`; such a movement joins two member nodes, which no other junction does (C08 clarification proposal: `Movement.inEdge.to` need not equal `outEdge.from` inside a `DeadEnd` junction). 8 pairs on `sf`, none unpaired. | The spec says "nearest clip start on the same box side within 150 m" for each sink only; pairing the leftover sources too gives the traffic of a one-way carriageway a way in. Undo: drop the second loop in `buildJunctions`. |

Dropped fragments leave `nodes` and `segs`; every other fragment stays, gets `SegB.island = true` (tool-side only, not in the packs) and is listed in `roads.islands.list` (status, rule, edges, up to 12 `osmWay`/`cnn` ids, centroid, touchesClip). Islands get no traffic or parked slots (S9 spawn), no ped slots (S8 `sidewalkM`), no county-line portal and no XR teleport node within 14 m (S9). `roadclass.json` `islands` is two booleans. | Measured on `sf`: 192 fragments (905 edges) dropped, 29 (135 edges) kept. Undo: set a rule to `false`. |
| BD-SFFIX-04 | `content/world/road-fixes.json` (DL-706) | New file, schema `tools/map/schemas/road-fixes.schema.json`, part of the `content-world` stamp. `keepWays` survive all three S5 drops (service, access, excluded layer; a layer-dropped way loses the row's `cnn`); a `connectors` entry is a polyline whose end points are OSM vertex coordinates (7 decimals), so it shares their nodes. Twelve ways: w8919473 alone does not reconnect McDowell Rd SW (the other eight Van Ness Avenue ways of Fort Mason sit behind the same PRIVATE row cnn 15101000: w419755791, w467982369, w775576585, w24007947, w26987989, w254759961, w254759962, w680783336), w1222505189 plus the connector `ti.avenue-n` (Avenue N is `highway=construction`) reconnect Clipper Cove Way and Avenue G, and w8919110 (The Embarcadero lane at Pier 45) and w23798318 (Medical Center Way) are public streets lost to PRIVATE rows. | Without the last two the largest SCC is 97.99 % (53,190 of 54,282); with them 98.00 % (53,210 of 54,294, one edge over the line). They are the Architect's to keep or drop. Undo: delete the entries. |
| BD-SFFIX-05 | S7 integer grade closure (DL-707) | `closeNodeHeights` is a multi-source Dijkstra on decimetres that only lowers node heights; the allowance of a segment is the sum of `floor(limit x step x 10)` over its polyline steps, not `floor(limit x L x 10)`: with the Architect's allowance, segment 410 (74.3 m, 4.4 m of rise at 6 %) could not be clamped: its per-step floors add up to a few decimetres less than the floor of the whole. `clampPolylineDm` runs forward and backward passes with pinned ends. The limit is 6 % on a bridge segment (profile clamp, relaxation, closure, pinned clamp) and 40 % elsewhere; the 39 % pre-clamp stays for surface roads. Result: max grade 40 %, 0 edges over, 353 bridge segments all at 6 % or less, 82 nodes lowered (not 5: the bridge limit lowers decks and their abutments). | The 6 % rule was never enforced before (288 segments over 6.5 %). Undo: `limitOf`/`slopeOf` in `runGrading`. |

| BD-SFFIX-07 | Landmark arrival (DL-709) | `PlacesInputs.farArrival` is true on `sf` only: the nearest valid pedestrian node within 120 m, else the nearest valid one at any distance (distance in `places.arrivalFar`, gate `s10.arrival-far` warns). Teleports to the Golden Gate therefore land in Marin County. The `sf` raw lock changes: osm entry sha256 ddc5aeeb2c06..., 561,193 rows (was 561,182); the other entries are unchanged. | The tag filter dropped 9 of the 35 ids. Undo: remove the two commands. |

New points are pedestrian nodes of the baked ped graph. `poi.mission.dolores-park` is now `poi.castro-upper-market.dolores-park` in `content/npcs/poi.stub.json`, `ro-batista/soul.md` (`soulVersion` 2, lint L14) and `npc-roster.json` (`data/npc-roster` rev 2, `assets.lock.json` approved by `m02a2-sf-fix-builder`); tests that pinned ro-batista at version 1 were updated. Spec files that quote the old id as an example (`04-npc-contracts.md`, `research/`) are left to the Architect. | Rule (6) of 02-world-bake.md section 9. |
| BD-SFFIX-10 | Cable-car legs and the 6 m check (DL-710) | The leg finder walks the street segments of the leg's street plus the OSM cable rails (`railway=tram`, `cable_tram=cable`, no sidings, no tunnels) joined to street nodes within 6 m (the rail edge is split at the projection) and to the turntable centre through rail vertices within 20 m (the track ends 10 to 12 m short of the ring at Hyde/Beach). At the first leg's start and the last leg's end the turntable within 250 m of both streets is preferred to the street node carrying both names, so the real track ends are reached: w197974389 (Powell/Market), w667569434 (Taylor/Bay: the street node carrying both names lies 21 m short of the Taylor block that holds the turntable, and no street or rail joins them), w197979144 (Hyde/Beach).
