| BD-M02e-01 | Shot camera and the golden shots | `shot(id)` holds the camera through `LookView.holdShot` (a host override read in `WorldView` before the free camera), pauses the clock with `debug.clock.pause`, locks hour and weather with a zero-second blend and freezes the look clock and the sky time (`freezeClock`, SkyView `freezeTime`). `y` of a shot is metres above the ground under the camera, applied once the focus chunk is resident. `golden-water` stands on the end of the synth pier (3190, 3, -1500) looking east (the first position, 110 m inland, showed a road and no water). All four shots are in the Civic Center and on the synth pier, which every CI bake contains. | The spec did not fix the holder. Undo: edit `config/world/look-shots.json`. |
| BD-M02e-02 | Composite on WebGL2 | A `PassNode` that renders the backdrop from inside the pipeline came out empty on the WebGL2 backend, and AA nodes (SMAA, FXAA) blacked the sky when the composite used the world pass alpha. The stack therefore renders the backdrop into a `RenderTarget` of its own just before `pipeline.render()`, uses it as `scene.backgroundNode`, and the world pass clears depth only; GTAO is masked by the linear depth (the sky keeps AO 1). `postNodes` and the node order are unchanged. | Observed on `chromium-webgl2` (SwiftShader), three@0.186.1. Undo: `packages/engine/src/platform/post/stack.ts`. |
| BD-M02e-03 | GTAO strength | `gtao-half` multiplies the colour by `1 - 0.6 (1 - ao)` (never below 0.4) and the factor is blurred with a five-tap cross at 1.3 texels (16 samples, half resolution). With `renderer.reversedDepthBuffer` true the node returned about 0 on walls seen across a street whatever `radius`, `thickness` or the normal source (the MRT view normal was correct), which turned half the towers black; the soft multiplier keeps the contact shading without the black walls. Observed on `chromium-webgl2` only (no GPU adapter here); the WebGPU path is unmeasured. | Value is `AO_STRENGTH` in `stack.ts`. Undo: set it to 1. |
| BD-M02e-04 | Light rig values | Hemisphere `HEMI_LOOK` 1.6 (0.4 of it while the environment map carries the ambient), environment intensity 0.55, the keyed ambient pulled halfway to grey, `SkyMesh` output scaled by 0.3 and clamped to 6 (it came out over-bright in a half-float target and bloomed). The environment map weighs `1 - domeBlend` and fades with night, so under the dome (Karl fog, overcast, rain, smoke) the hemisphere carries the ambient; glass `metalness` follows the same share (`uEnv`), because a metallic pane is black without a map. Adjustable live in QA through `__dvBridge.world.internals().look.tune`. | Design values. Undo: `look-view.ts` constants and `facade.ts`. |
| BD-M02e-05 | Shadow light per build | `ShadowRig` casts from a `DirectionalLight` of its own for `csm` and `fitted` and never lets the sky's sun cast: a three shadow node does not survive its light's `castShadow` going false and true again (`ShadowNode.updateShadow` read `shadowMap.depthTexture` of null on `mobile` after `setLook(false)`, `setLook(true)`). Teardown removes the light and its target and gives the sun back. | Found by `@world-look` on `mobile`. Undo: `shadows.ts` `build`/`teardown`. |
| BD-M02e-06 | What runs on WebGL2 | On `chromium-webgl2` every node of every tier runs: `look.json` `webgl2Unsupported` is `[]` and `webgl2AaFallback` is `{}`; `CSMShadowNode` builds (`shadowPath` csm, 3 passes) so the `SunLight` and fitted fallbacks are only exercised by unit tests. Playwright's headless Chromium user agent is classified as Safari by the M01c probe, so its environment map is off there; the goldens are taken in that state. | Observed 2026-10-20. Undo: none needed. |
| BD-M02e-07 | Debug panel and QA flags | `WorldLayerDebug.look` (additive, optional) and `SkyControlsOptions.look` give the QA panel a look switch and the active node list (`data-testid` `look-toggle`, `look-nodes`). `?look=0` boots with the look off (QA builds only; the `world.look` flag is the production switch). No contract changes. | Spec: 'Debug panel: a look toggle and the active node list'. Undo: remove the two optional members. |
| BD-M02e-08 | Per-building rules kept | Windows are lit by M02b2's `buildingLit` rule, called not copied; the per-window hash keeps 55 to 85 % of the cells of a lit building on. Façade u is stretched to whole bays by the mesher (`facade` snorm16x4, v from the skirt foot) and procedural patterns are anti-aliased with `fwidth` (detail fades when a pixel covers a cell) so the far city does not shimmer; the façade atlas hook (`createFacadeAtlasHook`) exists for M10d2 and nothing reads it. | Design. Undo: `look/facade.ts`. |
| BD-M02e-09 | Capture on a slow renderer | `__dvBridge.world.hold(on)` (QA only, additive) stops or restarts the render loop; the canvas keeps its last presented frame. `@look-compare` boots and places each shot at 256 x 144, grows the viewport to 960 x 540 (same 16:9 aspect, so the frustum and the residents stay), holds the loop for the element screenshot and shrinks it again: on SwiftShader a frame took about 5 s at 960 x 540 under load, `shot()` waits for many frames and Playwright's element screenshot waits for a still canvas, so two first versions timed out at 30 min. The goldens do not hold the loop (their two-captures-identical check stays a real check); the test timeout is 60 min. | Test-support hook, no effect on the sim or on production builds. Undo: remove `hold` and the viewport steps in `tests/e2e/world-look.spec.ts`. |
| BD-M02e-10 | Coarse depth bias | The coarse (L1, L2) look materials carry `polygonOffset` with factor and units -4 (`COARSE_DEPTH_BIAS` in `look/look-materials.ts`). The look made visible a z-fight that the legacy materials also have: a tower whose footprint crosses a cell that is not masked (its L0 chunk is outside the resident set, or the building belongs to the neighbouring chunk) is drawn twice, by the L0 chunk and by the L1 massing, and the two walls are coplanar, which showed as a speckle of window grid and flat grey on the wall and of grass through the road. The sign is negative because the renderer runs a reversed depth buffer (`renderer.reversedDepthBuffer`): +4 pushed the coarse surface in front and made the road speckled with grass. Legacy materials are untouched, so the `setLook(false)` picture stays the pre-M02e look. | Found in the first desktop before/after capture at 960 x 540. Undo: set the constant to 0. |
| BD-M02e-11 | XR look case and the look clock | The `@xr @world-look` case loads `/?tier=xr-quest&xr-emulate=quest3&debug=1`: with `xr-emulate` alone the tier decision of a flat Chromium page is `mobile-low` (the init tier is `xr-quest`, the final decision is the benchmark's), so the case reported `min` and `blob`. A shot freezes the look clock at 3 s (`SHOT_LOOK_CLOCK_S`) instead of the page's age: the water goldens differed between two page loads (92.7 % and 85.8 % of pixels within 6/255) because the waves stood in a different phase. | Both found by the AC proofs. Undo: the URL of the case; the constant. |
| BD-M02e-12 | check:perf on a loaded machine | `route-ferry-ocean-beach` at `mobile` on `chromium-webgl2` measured 167, 175 and 165 render objects against 150 in the default 6 s run, which gets only 5 to 10 frames of 1 to 4 s each here (the machine ran at load 12 to 20 on 4 cores) and so samples the streaming transient; the same run with `world.look` false measured 174, so the look does not cause it, and a 40 s run after a 20 s warm-up (52 frames) measured 100. The AC-8 proof is reported with `--duration 40 --warmup 20` next to the default one. No budget was raised, no data changed (`shadowsOff` stays empty: switching `mobile` shadows off measured 165 in the default run). | Environment, not the look. Undo: none. |
| BD-M02e-13 | The `sf` before/after | `docs/look/M02e-before-after-sf.png` (1920 x 1080, rows noon and night, columns before and after, 960 x 540 tiles, `desktop` tier, `chromium-webgl2`) is the optional extra image of AC-13: shots `sf-civic-noon` and `sf-civic-night` of `config/world/look-shots.json`, standing on Civic Center Plaza (114.5, -55.5) of the real `sf` bake (`pnpm map:build -- --area sf`, then `VITE_DV_QA=1 DV_WORLD=sf pnpm build`), looking west at City Hall. The synth shots are inside a building on `sf` (the Civic Center of the synth world is not the real one), so they are not reused. It is made by hand from the same capture steps as `@look-compare` (no test checks it) and carries no hash. Visible in both columns: the City Hall walls speckle, which is the hero model and the L0 proxy walls drawn in the same place; it belongs to the landmark layer (M02d1, M02d2), is not touched here, and is not caused by the look. | Shots 2 to 8 tried on `sf` (Market Street, UN Plaza, Opera House, Ferry Building, Powell turntable) put the camera inside a building or against a wall. Undo: delete the image and the two shots. |
| BD-M02c1-01 | Route cost and ties | A* runs over directed edges (a state is an edge and its cost is the distance to the end of it) in whole decimetres, so sums are exact on every device. Equal scores leave the heap by edge id, and an equal-cost way into a state keeps the predecessor with the lower edge id (of two equal-length routes the one with the lower ids near the goal wins). `allowWrongWay` adds one state per one-way edge for driving it backwards; the route repeats the edge id and `RoadGraph.routeDetailed` returns `wrongWay[]`. `Route` is unchanged. `useTopology` ignores the resident junction movements, so the answer does not depend on which chunks are resident (the e2e compares Node with the page this way). | C08 says 'ties by edge id' and 'lane-level inside resident chunks and topology beyond'; a shape change was not allowed. | Change `astar.ts`; the specs in `graph.route.spec.ts` pin the rules. |
| BD-M02c1-02 | Lane offset | `laneCentre` implements C08 rule 10 verbatim: two-way `medianM/2 + (lane + 0.5) x laneWidthM`, one-way `(lane + 0.5 - lanes/2) x laneWidthM`, positive to the right of travel. For a one-way edge lane 0 is therefore the leftmost lane although rule 10 calls lane 0 'rightmost'; the two-way formula puts lane 0 next to the median. Proposal for the Architect: say 'lane 0 is the innermost lane' (a clarification, no shape change). | The formula and the sentence disagree; the formula is what M03 will drive on. | Flip the sign for one-way edges in `RoadGraph.laneCentre`. |
| BD-M02c1-03 | Teleport timings | 'Destination p0 tile plus a one-tile ring' is read in chunk units: the destination z=9 chunk and its eight neighbours are requested and pinned (they lie in at most four z=7 packs). The 8 s wait is 480 ticks, the fade-out 12 ticks, the arrival search a 2 m lattice within 40 m (nearest first, ties by dx then dz) with 0.6 m clearance from building footprints, placed on the highest surface at the point. The result carries the requested yaw; the camera heading is the page's look yaw (M01b2), so yaw is not applied to the player. Fast-travel cooldown and the fare log are session state and are not hashed. | The spec names no unit for the ring; a whole 3 x 3 of 1 km tiles would be nine packs. | Change the constants at the top of `teleport.ts`. |
| BD-M02c1-04 | Fares | `config/world/transit.json` (schema `tools/map/schemas/transit.schema.json`): bay-rail 200 + 50 per stop, cable car 100, robotaxi 300 + 120 per km, all design values. A fast travel to a node that is not a cable-car stop or a ferry quay is priced as rail with `round(distance / 1200 m)` stops (min 1); a target that is not a transit node is priced as a robotaxi ride; ferry is 0 (the spec lists no ferry fare). Fares are recorded in `TeleportService.fares` and not charged until EconomyApi (M04a2). | The spec gives the three numbers but not how stops are counted. | Edit `transit.json`. |
| BD-M02c1-05 | Place labels | `PlaceIndex.findPlace` matches on a label; the sim has no text catalogue, so the label is the injected `labels(nameKey)` or the last key segment in title case ('place.coit-tower' gives 'Coit Tower'). Matching is by whole name, prefix, or every query token as a prefix of a name token; landmarks rank above POIs; a POI bound to a landmark is not listed twice. | The index carries name keys, not names; en.json has no place names yet. | Pass `labels` from the page's catalogue once names exist (M10c). |
| BD-M02c1-06 | County line and play wall | The soft wall is a static prism (0.8 m thick, road width + 3 m wide, 4 m high) at each county-line portal, added to the colliders of the portal's chunk by the statics feed (`StaticsFeeder.extras`); bridge and tunnel portals stand as barriers while `closed` or `sealed`. The approach event fires within 30 m of the nearest county-line portal and re-arms beyond 90 m; the fog factor (`wf` in the snapshot side block, 1 at 150 m, 0.4 at the line) uses the distance to the nearest portal point because the bake gives portals (one per crossing road), not the line. The play wall is four 100 m thick boxes just outside play + 50 m (static collider `world.bounds`). The camera and aircraft clamp at `maxY` 600 m is the constant `PLAY_MAX_Y_M`; no aircraft or camera clamp exists to apply it to yet. | DL-691 gives the rules, not the shapes; the bake has no county-line polyline. | Change `barrierPrism` and `playWallPrisms`. |
| BD-M02c1-07 | Portal runtime | Every `WorldIndex.portals` row starts `closed` whatever the bake wrote (the synth bake writes county-line portals `open`). `sealed` is the set of owners holding the portal (closing its edges in `TrafficControl`); `open` is a scripted finale's own flag. `release(by)` also drops the owner's other `TrafficControl` holds; releasing an owner's closures directly in `TrafficControl` unseals its portals. `TrafficControl.reopenEdges(edges, by)` is an extra method (not in C08). The portal and traffic state is hashed in the resources `world.portals` and `world.traffic`. | The port needs one release call for a pursuit that seals portals, thins traffic and closes edges. | Split `release` into per-kind releases. |
| BD-M02c1-08 | Swim | The swim owner starts when the water over the capsule is deeper than `chestY` (1.35 m), keeps the player in place for 90 ticks (the water wall's 1.5 s), then fades (`world.teleport` events `out` and `in`), hands back to the on-foot owner and asks the ownership machine for a teleport to the nearest shore node pushed 2 m inland (any shore, not only road-adjacent). It sits in `registerControlSystems` and does nothing without a `WorldBoundsPort` on `services.world`. | Spec: 'swims and respawns on shore'; no swimming locomotion in v1. | Edit `swim.ts`. |
| BD-M02c1-09 | Engine additions | `EngineServices.world` (`{api, bounds}`, set by the world module); `WorldBoundsPort` and `WorldHub` in `packages/engine/src/sim/world/index.ts`; Control state 800; the QA debug commands `debug.world` (a call into the world services, answered as the qa message `world.reply.<id>`) and `debug.bot` (a scripted input from now on, built by `SimRuntime.botFactory`, which only the QA worker entry sets, so production bundles keep no bot code); `config.character.chestY` is read, not changed. The prefab id pattern now allows hyphens inside a segment (`prop.county-barrier`). | The milestone names the port and the county-barrier prefab; e2e needs input and replies. | Remove the commands and the pattern change. |
| BD-M02c1-10 | World data in the sim | The world index travels in `BootPayload.config.world.index` (about 100 KB for synth, at most 300 KB). `sf-graph.v1` and `district-grid.v1` are requested through the pack channel as extra requests with a handler (`WorldSim.loadAux`), applied in `seq` order with the chunk replies; until they arrive the network is the union of the resident chunks and `nhoodAt` answers 255. `RoadNetwork.setTopology` and `WorldQuery.setGrid` install them. | Keeps the boot payload small and the pack store's hashing and retries. | Put the bytes in the boot payload. |
| BD-M02c1-11 | Map patch | The blips, waypoint and GPS route are published as the sim event `world.map` (JSON in `s`, `n` = revision) from a Publish-stage system when something changed, not through the snapshot side block, because a snapshot carries only the newest tick and a patch must not be missed. Blips and the route are view state, not hashed (their owners set them again after a load). The route is replanned when the target changes and, while a route is set, after 2 s once the player has moved 25 m; the polyline is thinned to 400 points; drivable classes exclude Service unless nothing else joins the ends. | View-model patch at `SimStage.Publish` (spec); no UiBridge exists before M04a2. | Move the patch to `UiBridge` in M04a2. |
| BD-M02c1-12 | District grid | S12 is the stage id `district-grid` (after `places`, before `lod`): 64 m cells from the `play` box floored to a cell, rasterised from the S3 neighbourhood polygons with the index of `NEIGHBOURHOODS_41`. The file is `district-grid.v1.pack` (C07 kind `data`, id `district-grid.v1`, extension `pack`, because the content-type table maps only `pack` to a binary type) at the bake root. Layout (52 B header, 41-slug table, cells) is in `packages/shared/src/world/district-grid.ts`; proposal for the Architect: list it in C08 section 4 as the `sf-graph.v1` layout was. The world-index schema rejects extra properties, so 'listed in the world index' is met by the asset catalog entry (`map:pack`), not a new index field. Gates S12 (names, cells) and S14 (every index 0-40 or 255 and mapped to a district). | The schema forbids a new index field; the spec asks for reproducible bytes and a district per index. | Add an index field in a C08 minor bump. |
| BD-M02c1-13 | QA world controls | With `?debug=1` the QA bridge mounts a box under the sky controls: a landmark list with Go (teleport), a list with Route and Clear (the GPS route), and Coast and County buttons that put the player at the water and at the county barrier. `__dvBridge.world` gains `call`, `teleport`, `route`, `resolve`, `lastBoundaryEvent`, `boundaryEvents`, `fade` and `map`; the ops of `call` are in `packages/world/src/sim/api/debug.ts`. The e2e compares routes with `useTopology` (see BD-M02c1-01). | The owner wants visible progress in the default QA scene. | Delete `world-controls.ts`. |
| BD-M02c1-14 | TrafficControl semantics | `setDensity` keeps a stack per kind: the newest override is in force and releasing it brings back the one below; an owner that sets again moves to the top. `closeEdges` is per owner and an edge stays closed while any owner holds it; the `onClosureChange` event lists the edges that owner closed, or the edges it reopens (also when another owner still holds an edge, so barrier props can be per owner). `setBlackout` is the M02b2 `Blackout` unchanged. | C08 gives the verbs, not the layering. | Change `control.ts`; `graph.closures.spec.ts` pins it. |
| BD-M03a1-01 | Handling model shape | `stepHandling` mutates `HandlingState` and returns a fresh `HandlingOutput`. Frames: body +X right, +Y up, +Z back (forward is -Z); steer and yaw rate are positive to the right. Torque is read at no less than 30 % of the idle-to-redline range (clutch slip on a launch). The auto box shifts up above `shiftUpPct` of redline and down below `shiftDownPct`; the brake pedal reverses from rest. Drag is applied as longitudinal wheel force, with a hold brake below 0.4 m/s and 3 % g of engine braking when coasting. The spring-damper is `suspensionForce` (preload = static wheel load, so the car sits at its designed height); the wrapper evaluates it for `WheelContact.loadN`. | The S06 types give no frames, no vertical output and no gearbox rules. | Change `handling.ts`; the oracle tests pin the outcome. |
| BD-M03a1-02 | Physics additions | `VehiclePhysics` (engine-internal): `setBodyPose`, `setBodyVelocity`, `applyTorqueImpulse`, `setBodyGroups`, `setBodyKind`; `BodyDesc` gains optional `friction`, `restitution`, `linearDamping`, `angularDamping`, `ccd`; `PrefabDef.physics` gains `liftM` and the same. The physics step system takes `beforeSubstep` and `physicsStepSystem` replaces the default step so a car above 100 km/h steps handling at SIM_DT / 2. `PhysicsBackend.vehicle.create/drive/read` stay unimplemented: the ray-cast wrapper uses `castRay` and `applyImpulse` (DL-104). | The wrapper needs poses, velocities and a kinematic-to-dynamic switch the backend interface lacks. | Fold the additions into the spec'd interface in a C06 minor. |
| BD-M03a1-03 | `BodyKin` extras | `BodyKin` gains optional `pitch`, `roll`, `pitchRate`, `rollRate`, `flightPitch` for the air trim; `airTorque` is body-frame [pitch (nose up), 0, roll (right down)]. | The S06 shape has no attitude field. | Compute the trim in the wrapper. |
| BD-M03a1-04 | Interaction shapes | `InteractionPrompt.reason` carries an unavailable reason; handlers take an `InteractionContext` (ecs, services, tick) because C06's `ModeContext` lands with M04a2. The cone is a total angle (half each side of the player's heading); a vehicle's distance is to the nearer door point; hold counts ticks of the button held. | The spec leaves the cone reading and the context type open. | Switch to `ModeContext` in M04a2. |
| BD-M03a1-05 | Lines | Line ticks are relative to the tick the line starts at; `start.speed` (m/s along the heading) is added for rolling starts; `follow` steers a vehicle or walks toward a point; `waitNear` holds the line clock; `LinePlayer` is a `BotInput` that takes the real frame and hands over on the tick any button, stick, pedal or steer is non-zero. The runtime asks the bot for the real frame only when it sets `stopsOnRealInput`. | Spec lists the command names only. | Change `lines/player.ts`. |
| BD-M03a1-06 | Presets | Design values chosen here; the generator is not committed (data only). `hp.police-suv` is class D with 2,300 kg and 156 kW, so its top speed (200 kph) sits above the D envelope; the AC bounds mass, power and mu only. Rolling starts for top-speed lines: 40 m/s (class D), 55 and 52 m/s (cruiser, SUV), 28 m/s (robotaxi). Measured values: see the report. | The oracle windows fix the outcome, not the inputs. | Retune the JSON files; the line tests fail first. |
| BD-M03a1-07 | Enter and exit | The capsule is placed at the exit spot when the Leaving phase starts (Rapier's character controller reads the collider pose the next step propagates), the vehicle owner uses states 200, 201, 203, and the exit is refused above 6 m/s. The input context is `inputContextFor(owner)`; the page pushes `vehicle` while the owner is Vehicle. | The ownership diagram has no body placement rule. | Edit `enter-exit.ts`. |
| BD-M03a1-08 | Vehicle cameras | The sim casts the far chase ray along the player's view (`camYaw`, `camPitch`) and publishes `allowedDistance`; near and mid are shorter on the same ray. The page recentres the view after 1.5 s without look input. Hood and cockpit sit on the car and turn with it. | C04 has no vehicle collision shape. | Edit `vehicle-camera.ts`, `collision.ts`. |
| BD-M03a1-09 | QA additions | Debug commands `debug.spawn`, `debug.vehicle`, `debug.line`, `debug.inspect`, `debug.edit`, `debug.physics` (replies are `qa` messages); `EngineServices.side` registry and `.vehicleCtx` (the handles `debug.spawn` and `debug.vehicle` need); snapshot side keys `veh` and `prompt`; the record's `animP0` and `animP1` carry a vehicle's speed and steer; touch layout gains an Enter button (`sim.Enter`); the gamepad on-foot map binds `sim.Enter` to Y. Parked cars stand next to the spawn in the gray-box and streamed scenes. | The owner wants the cars visible in the default QA scene. | Remove the commands and `parked.ts`. |
| BD-M03a1-10 | `@vehicle-world` route | The synth deck (`config/world/synth.json`, overpass `i 8, j0 14`) starts 1.6 to 2.9 m above its approach (slab top 33.1 against terrain 31.5 at z 2216 on x 1340), a lip no car mounts, and its far end drops back to terrain at z ≈ 2496. The e2e therefore spawns the sedan on the deck (z 2232) and drives it off the far end; the tunnel run starts 140 m before the portal. Rounds of 15 s repeat until the car is through (a chunk that has not streamed in is a wall, `wallPrism`), the world tests run serially in one worker, and `groundY` is asked while the car is there (it is `null` for a chunk that is no longer resident). The reply ids of the bridge start at 1,000,000 so that they never collide with the inspector panel's. | A fixed approach ramp is the bake's (M02a2 S7), not the vehicle's; the lip is reported as a suggestion. | Add an approach ramp in S7 and start the e2e before the slab. |
| BD-M03a1-11 | QA-only code and tick cost | `debug.spawn`/`debug.vehicle` live in `sim/vehicle/debug.ts` (`vehicleQa`), installed by the sim worker entry behind `VITE_DV_QA` like the inspector and the line tools, so a production bundle has neither the handlers nor their `*.reply.` names; `verify:dist` fails on `inspect.reply.`, `physics.reply.`, `spawn.reply.`, `vehicle.reply.`, `veh:qa`, `LineRecorder`, `physics-draw` and `dv-inspector`. The command names stay in the protocol validator (`envelope.ts`) and the runtime switch. Tick cost (U-ENG-2): 1.3 ms per tick for eight sedans (one driven at full throttle) on the `canonical` build in Node, one shared Rapier world, no chunks. M07a1 sets the per-tier cap. | The AC asks that the debug tools be absent from the production bundle. | Move the names behind a QA-only validator. |
| BD-M02d1-01 | Recipe parameters | The parameters of each recipe are the `recipe` object of its entry in `content/world/landmarks.json` (schema: every recipe has `yawDeg` and `anchor`, the rest are numbers or arrays of numbers). The recipe module keeps the same object as typed `defaults`; `map:landmarks -- --build` fails when the JSON lacks a key, has an extra one or has another type (`paramFindings`), so the two cannot drift apart, but the JSON is what builds. `content-world` stamps the canonical JSON of all seed files, so adding `recipe` changed the `bakeId` of every area (synth `8f44f9c3...` to `8db977b5...` before any recipe tweak): goldens pinned to a bake id fail with `world golden from bake <old>`, per the milestone Notes. | The milestone puts the parameters in the seed file. | Move the parameters to their own file under `content/world/` and drop the key from the seed schema (the `bakeId` changes again). |
| BD-M02d1-02 | Swap hysteresis, removal order | A resident landmark is released at `swapM x 1.05` (`unloadFactor` in `config/world/landmarks-runtime.json`), not at `swapM`: a player standing on the boundary would otherwise re-mesh its chunks twice a second. On release the chunks are asked to mesh their proxies back first and the model is removed when the last of them has committed (or after 2.5 s), so a landmark is never both gone and masked. The cap counts loading, shown and leaving landmarks, so a new one waits until the farthest has left. A failed load is retried after 30 s (`retryMs`). | The spec says swap distance and 'never a hole'; it gives no hysteresis and no order. | Set `unloadFactor` to 1. |
| BD-M02d1-03 | Masking only by remesh | A chunk's first mesh always has its proxies (the job gets no mask); `LandmarkLayer` asks for the masked mesh once the chunk is uploaded and the landmark is shown, and for the unmasked one when it leaves. So every chunk that holds a proxy gets exactly one re-record request when a landmark comes up and one when it goes, whatever the load order (AC-6 counts them). `UnitMesh` gains `proxies` (all `proxyOf` values in the chunk) and `masked` (the ones left out); `MeshJobArgs.maskProxies`. `remeshFiltered` passes the mask of the shown landmarks so a profile remesh does not bring a proxy back. Remeshes enter the upload gate at priority -1. | Deterministic counts; a chunk that arrives after the landmark costs one extra mesh job and a few frames of overlap, which only happens after a teleport. | Pass the mask in `requestMesh` (the first upload then needs no re-record, and AC-6 reads 0 or 1). |
| BD-M02d1-04 | Runtime model path | `asset.worker` job `world.landmark` reads the tier's `.glb` through the pack store and parses it with `landmark-glb.ts` (JSON and BIN chunks, `EXT_meshopt_compression` decoded by `meshoptimizer@1.3.0`'s decoder, wasm): plain buffers go back transferred. It reads only what the recipes write and refuses anything else, so no `GLTFLoader` and no main-thread decode. `landmark-view.ts` draws every primitive with the world's shared chunk material (vertex colour times `aAO` 1, fade-in from `aBirth`), so a landmark takes the sky, night windows and rain like the city; the PBR factors in the `.glb` (matte, metal, glass) are for the Blender art pass at the same AssetIds (M10d2). Meshes are frustum-culled one by one; `RenderBudgetMeter` counts them as scene objects, so the layer needs no counter of its own. | Keeps three out of the worker and the look pass consistent. | Load with `GLTFLoader` plus `MeshoptDecoder` and keep the recipes' materials. |
| BD-M02d1-05 | Model frame | Models are authored in world axes (+X east, +Y up, +Z south), origin at the landmark seed, `yawDeg` baked into the vertices (no node transform). `anchor` `ground` puts y = 0 on the terrain height under the seed (read from the uploaded L0 mesh; the model waits until it exists), `sea` puts it at `MESHER.lod.waterLevelM` (the two bridges). Walls start 2 to 6 m below y = 0 so a model sits in sloped terrain. The scene's extras carry `dvLandmark` `{id, fidelity, anchor, yawDeg}`. Orientation is architecture-true where the spec names it (City Hall and the Ferry Building face east, the Golden Gate runs north-south), nothing is traced from a photograph (rule 7). | The seed is the OSM feature position; y has to come from somewhere. | Change `anchor` in the seed JSON. |
| BD-M02d1-06 | Writer settings | `@gltf-transform/functions` `reorder` (target size) then `EXT_meshopt_compression` with the `QUANTIZE` method and no quantize pass: positions, normals and linear colours stay float32 so the runtime reads exact metres; indices are 16-bit up to 65,535 vertices, else 32-bit. `asset.generator` is `dee-verse tools/map landmarks` (the library string drifts with its version). Output was identical across two runs in one process and on a second run of `--check`; cross-platform byte stability (Linux versus macOS) is not checked here, the fallback of the milestone (commit uncompressed) is not needed yet. `fileBytes` budgets: hero 2 MiB / 1 MiB / 512 KiB, major 1 MiB / 512 KiB / 256 KiB, minor 512 / 256 / 128 KiB (the milestone gives triangles only; the largest file is the Golden Gate at hi, about 217 KB). | Byte determinism and exact metres. | Pass `quantize` options and read `KHR_mesh_quantization` in the reader. |
| BD-M02d1-07 | Stats budgets | `asset.json` gains `stats` (per variant: `tris`, `verts`, `primitives`, `materials`) and `codec`; the catalog copies them into `AssetVariant.stats` and `codec.mesh`. `config/asset-budgets.json` rows may list `tris`, `materials`, `maxObjects` (new column: render objects, the primitives of the model) and `maxTexPx`; `statsFindings` in `tools/assets/src/verify.ts` makes `tris`, `maxObjects` and `materials` mandatory for a spec that matches the row (`stats-missing`) and fails a value over its column (`budget-tris`, `budget-objects`, `budget-materials`, `budget-tex`; the texture size is checked only when one is recorded). The existing character and vehicle rows list `tris` and `materials`, so a future asset of those kinds must record them: M10a1 and M10d1 own those rows. | The milestone says check:assets enforces stats columns for any asset whose budget row exists. | Drop `statsFindings` from `budgetFindings`. |
| BD-M02d1-08 | Synth arrival poses | In the `synth` world a landmark's arrival pose is the nearest ped node of the synth street grid, which often lies inside the footprint of the model (City Hall 32 m from its seed, inside a 124 x 52 m block; Coit Tower 5 m from its seed). The camera then sits inside the walls (back faces are culled) and shows the colonnade, the dome and the nearer synth buildings. To see a model from outside in a QA build, press F4 (free cam) and hold S and Space. The real `sf` arrival poses sit on sidewalks around the block. | S10 arrival poses (M02a2) are not changed here. | Move the synth arrival pose out of the footprint in S10 (a bake change). |
| BD-M02d1-09 | Landmark debug line | The `?debug=1` world controls gain a line `landmark models n/cap within swapM m: ...` that lists the resident models with their fidelity and distance (`__dvBridge.world.landmarks()` is the data). `map:landmarks -- --preview <id> [--fidelity] [--az] [--el] [--dist] [--eye x,y,z --look x,y,z] [--out png]` rasterises a recipe in software (no GPU, no glTF viewer) to `dist/world/landmarks/`; it is a dev tool and nothing reads its output. | The owner wants visible progress; the recipes needed a way to be looked at without a browser. | Delete `kit/preview.ts` and the `--preview` branch. |
| BD-M02d2-01 | Skyline outside the caps | The seven skyline landmarks (`skyline.ids` in `config/world/landmarks-runtime.json`) sit outside the per-tier resident caps: `LandmarkLayer` keeps a second list of skyline entries next to the full-fidelity slots, the cap counts the slots only, and `report().maxResident` never includes the skyline. They are counted where they are drawn: `RenderBudgetMeter` sees their meshes like any other object, and a skyline instance hidden under its full model is not drawn and not counted (`budget()` skips it). The `lo` model is read by the same `world.landmark` job with `skyline: true`, which resolves the variant through the `mobile-low` tier (`TIER_FIDELITY` gives `lo`; `SKYLINE_TIER` in `jobs.ts`), reads at `idle` priority and pins the pack through `AssetService.pinHashes` (one additive public method; the `pin` RPC and `unpinAll` already used the same set). | The milestone asks for this decision to be recorded. The job already resolves a variant per tier, so no new asset path was needed; a skyline read at `idle` never competes with the boot set. | Count the skyline in the caps (raise `resident` by 7 or lower `skyline.ids`); read the `lo` variant through a new `variant` argument instead of the tier. |
| BD-M02d2-02 | Skyline rings | The skyline is read within 2.0 x the tier's building draw distance (the streaming `r2M`), put in the scene within 1.5 x and parked beyond 1.7 x (`loadFactor`, `mountFactor`, `releaseFactor`; pure helpers in `chunks/skyline.ts`). It waits for the world's first reveal (`ready`), so it loads after the spawn p0 set, one read at a time, nearest first. A parked instance is hidden and kept (no second read); it is parked only after its proxies are meshed back (no hole). | AC-6 wants every skyline landmark inside `r2M` drawn on every sampled frame of a 90 m/s route: with the mount ring at 1.5 x r2M the model is up a few seconds before it crosses r2M, whatever the worker or the gate is doing. At the cost of seven models of at most 1,800 triangles each held beyond r2M. | Set `mountFactor` to 1; the layer then mounts at the draw distance and a slow worker can show a late skyline. |
| BD-M02d2-03 | Staged swap commit | A full-fidelity or skyline swap runs in three steps: the model is decoded in `asset.worker`; the chunks that hold its proxies are meshed again in the worker with the mask (`chunks.prepare`, the mesh is held, not uploaded); when the model and every prepared mesh are ready and the ground is known, the commit is a `SwapTask` queued through `ChunkRenderer.enqueueTask` into the same `ChunkUploadGate` (priority -2, so ahead of the chunk uploads), and the prepared meshes are queued behind it (`chunks.apply`, priority -1). The model therefore always joins the scene before the first proxy is removed (no frame with neither), and the model and one chunk never upload in the same frame (the gate's one-per-frame rule counts tasks). For the one to four frames of the hand-over the model and the unmasked proxies are both drawn: the proxy cannot be removed earlier without a hole and an atomic multi-chunk upload would break the one-upload rule. The layer counts those frames (`frames.doubles`, information) and the holes (`frames.holes`, must be 0); `landmark.swap` events (frame, main-thread ms, uploads that frame, model and proxy drawn after it) are in `landmarks().swaps` and `__dvBridge.world.swaps()`, QA builds only. | AC-6 and the Goal ask for a commit with no hole and one upload per frame. | Mount the model as soon as it is decoded (the M02d1 flow, `chunks.prepare` back to `remesh`); the proxy then stays drawn under the model until the remesh lands. |
| BD-M02d2-04 | Skyline owns the mask; new chunks arrive masked | While a skyline model is up it owns its landmark's proxy mask (`owners()` = full models shown plus skyline models shown), so the proxies are never drawn under it, and a full model that comes up over it needs no remesh. A chunk that streams in while a model stands in its proxies' place is requested with the owners' mask (`maskProxies` = `maskList()` in `requestMesh`), so it arrives masked and is uploaded once. This replaces BD-M02d1-03 ("a chunk's first mesh always has its proxies") for chunks that stream in later; the first mesh of a chunk requested before any model exists still carries them. The proxies of L1 and L2 massing under a skyline model are not masked (the lo model stands over a massing box of the same silhouette). | Without it the skyline would draw over the baked extrusions along the whole route and each chunk would upload twice. | Drop `maskList()` from `requestMesh` and `owners()` of the skyline; the reconciliation pass then masks late chunks with a second upload each. |
| BD-M02d2-05 | Kit parts and palette | The 25 recipes share four new kit modules: `kit/landscape.ts` (flat ground polygons, sloped rings, trees, palms, lamp posts, fences), `kit/glasshouse.ts` (barrel vault and glass hall: train shed, hangars, conservatory wings, transit atrium), `kit/eaves.ts` (lifted-corner tiled roofs and tier stacks: gateway and pagoda) and `kit/gantry.ts` (portal crane: shipyard and island quay); 19 colour names were added to `palette.ts` (painted wood, courts, tile, glass tints) and nothing in the 12 earlier models changed (`map:landmarks -- --build --check` and `git status` show no byte difference). A minor or major recipe is a full recipe file, not a call into one primitive: the parameters would not have been shared. | The milestone allows a new primitive when two or more landmarks share it. | Fold the modules back into `primitives.ts`. |
| BD-M02d2-06 | Catalog completeness gate | `map:landmarks` in content mode (no bake needed) also runs `validateLandmarkAssets`: every entry of `landmarks.json` needs `assets/src/building/<id>/asset.json` with the `desktop`, `mobile` and `mobile-low` variants (each file on disk) and the `lm-class:<cls>` tag, and every `lm.*` directory needs an entry. The check reads the repo files, not the catalog, so it runs before `assets:catalog`; the catalog and the lock stay `check:assets` and `assets:verify`. | Spec: "fails when any id in landmarks.json lacks a building/lm.<slug> asset or an asset lacks a landmark entry". | Move the check into `tools/ci/check-assets.ts` against the built catalog. |
| BD-M02d2-07 | Landmark worlds and goldens | Adding the 25 `recipe` objects to `content/world/landmarks.json` changes the `bakeId` of every area again (synth `69fe74af...` now). No world golden exists on `claude/build` at this point (M02c2 has not merged), so none was regenerated; the M02c2 builder regenerates them on rebase as its Notes say. | DL-657 asks the builder to list regenerated goldens; there are none. | n/a |
| BD-M02d2-08 | One draw object per landmark | At runtime `mountLandmark` merges a model's primitives (matte, metal, glass) into one `Mesh` (`chunks/landmark-merge.ts`, pure, no `three`): the world draws every material class with the one shared vertex-colour material (PBR factors wait for M10d2), so nothing is lost, and the render-object budget (`maxRenderObjects`, counted in view) pays one object per landmark instead of two or three, which the seven skyline models plus the full-fidelity ones need on `mobile` and `mobile-low`. The asset files keep their primitives and the class triangle budgets are unchanged (`landmark-merge.spec` checks triangles, vertices and bounds against `asset.json` for all 111 files). | AC-7 holds the skyline and swapped landmarks to each tier's render-object budget. | Keep one mesh per primitive and raise nothing: the route scenario would exceed the `mobile-low` object budget. |
| BD-M03a2-01 | M03a2 | (1) The losing `controller` adapter is not deleted but moved to `packages/engine/test/wheel-path-controller.ts` (test-only, not reachable from `src`, checked by `wheel-spike.adr.spec.ts`), because AC-2 asks `test:wheel-spike` to run both paths. (2) The committed `packages/engine/test/golden/wheel-spike.json` is the result of the deciding run (regenerate with `DV_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1`); the ADR spec compares the ADR `Decision:` line, `SELECTED_WHEEL_PATH` and that file, so the two specs do not depend on run order. (3) `RapierBackend.vehicle.create/drive/read` are implemented over the controller (no longer `NotImplementedError`) and `tuneVehicle` is added to `VehiclePhysics`; nothing in `src` calls them. `drive` applies one engine force and brake to all wheels, steering to the front wheels. (4) `registerVehicleModule`/`registerVehicleSystems`/`RaycastVehicleSim` take an optional wheel path (tests only). (5) Tick cost is the wall-clock of a whole sim tick in Node, not PhysicsPre + PhysicsStep alone. (6) Lines without a tolerance for a preset (Filbert and crest jump for the cruiser and robotaxi) are informational rows. | The milestone names a deleted loser and a harness that runs both; the golden avoids a race between two spec files in one vitest run; per-system timers do not exist in the headless runner. | Delete `test/wheel-path-controller.ts` and the `controllers` map in `rapier-backend.ts` once the C06 1.2 decision is made. |
| BD-M03b1-01 | ECS components of the world | The traffic uses components 256, 258, 259, 262 and 263; 263 is named `WorldWitness` (the milestone text calls it the witness component). A module declares its components with the new optional `SimModule.components?(boot)`, which `SimRuntime` consults when it builds the ECS (the registry is fixed at construction); the world module returns `WORLD_COMPONENTS`. Hash-relevant state lives in the `world.traffic.agents` resource, the components are the view of it. | The ECS registry cannot grow after construction and no module could add components before. | Move the ids into the engine registry when C01 lists them. |
| BD-M03b1-02 | Instanced flag in the snapshot | `SNAP_FLAG_INSTANCED = 2` in the 16-bit flags of a snapshot record marks an `Ambient` entity. The object pool skips flagged records; `WorldFrame` gains `poses`, `poseCount` and `poseKeys` (additive) so the world view can draw them in one `InstancedMesh`. The 64-byte record is unchanged (body colour in `animP1`, light bits in `animState`). | No per-car object is affordable (R-ENG-02 caps render objects), and the record had a free flag bit. | Take a C11 flag name for it when the contract is revised. |
| BD-M03b1-03 | Initial fill and bound agents | The spawn pass fills the whole ring in its first step and again after a focus jump of more than half the ambient radius (`initialFill`), so a scene starts with traffic. Only agents the spawn pass created carry `bound`; the density despawn removes only those, and only out of sight, so an agent a test or a mission placed by hand stays. | Without the fill the first minute is empty; without `bound` a hand-placed agent was removed by the density rule. | Remove `bound` when missions place agents through the pool. |
| BD-M03b1-04 | Parked cars share the cap | Parked cars count against the same tier cap (`maxTrafficInstances`) as moving cars: the parked pass gets `max(0, cap - live)` of it, so the instances drawn never exceed the C13 row. Parked cars are placed on the baked `spawn.parked` slots with the slot's baked yaw jitter at 0.01 rad per unit. | The spec gives one cap and two populations; the instance buffer is sized by the cap. | Split the cap in `config/world/density.json`. |
| BD-M03b1-05 | Residency rule in the runners | DL-678 says the sim never advances while a requested pack is pending. The Node runner and the QA page both pin the `p0` set around the start before tick 0 and then hold the rule: the Node runner checks before every tick and counts `stallTicks`; the QA page checks between batches of 600 ticks (`debug.step`), so it is batch-granular there. Neither skips a tick. The asserted result is `stallTicks == 0` for the 10,000-tick scenario; the out-of-order loader (seeded 0 to 5 ms) gives the same 16 hashes as the in-order one. | Per-tick round trips to the worker would make a 10,000-tick browser run take minutes. | Move the check into the sim runtime (`pendingCount()` is already exposed). |
| BD-M03b1-06 | One instanced mesh for all archetypes | One `InstancedMesh` draws every archetype: the geometry is the sedan's two-box car and each instance matrix carries the archetype's scale (`halfWidth/halfHeight/halfLength` over the sedan's). Slots are stable per entity handle; a freed slot is a zero matrix; cars beyond 150 m refresh one frame in three. The first design was one mesh per archetype (8 render objects); the perf run showed the tier object rows (`maxRenderObjects`) leave no room for 8, so it is 1 draw and 1 object. | `maxRenderObjects` counts every mesh in the scene; the world, the player rig and the sky already use 80 to 100 of the 100 on `mobile-low`. | Return to per-archetype meshes when the art pass (M10d1) brings real models and the row is re-cut. |
| BD-M03b1-07 | Promotion by overlap | A near agent has a kinematic box. A vehicle that overlaps it (oriented boxes, using the vehicle's velocity of the previous step) at a closing speed of 3 m/s or more promotes it: the agent becomes the vehicle prefab with `rt.external = true`, takes a kick from the impact, runs dynamic for `PROMOTED_TICKS` = 480 (8 s) and then holds its handbrake as a wreck, removed `WRECK_UNSEEN_TICKS` = 600 (10 s) after it was last in sight. Promoted and near counts are capped per tier (`PROMOTED_CAP`, `NEAR_CAP`). | The spec fixes the numbers, not the detection; the engine has no contact events for kinematic bodies. | Switch to Rapier contact events when the backend exposes them. |
| BD-M03b1-08 | Enter promotion not wired | `AmbientTraffic.promoteKey(key)` promotes an agent on request, but ambient cars carry no `Interactable`, so a player cannot yet enter one. Wiring it needs the C06 interaction registry of M04a2; the AC for promotion (AC-6) is proved with a hit. | The spec says 'hits or enters'; the enter half has no input path before M04a2. | Add the interactable in M04a2 and call `promoteKey`. |
| BD-M03b1-09 | Heights | An agent's `Transform.y` is the ground height under it plus the archetype's `lift` (the prefab's `physics.liftM`), read from the chunk heightfield, so the box rests on the road and the instanced mesh (origin at the box centre) sits on it. | The sim has no suspension for ambient cars; the ground is the only vertical input. | Replace with the suspension of the vehicle module when near agents drive on it. |
| BD-M03b1-10 | density.json extended | `config/world/density.json` gains `hourAnchors`, `hourCurves`, `tierFactors` and `weatherFactors` (and the schema in `tools/map/schemas/density.schema.json` gains the matching `$defs`), so the hour, tier and weather model is data. This changes the `bakeId` of every area (synth `51fa7c9c...` now); the `traffic-synth` golden carries the bake id and fails with the regenerate message on a mismatch. | The factors had no home and the milestone asks for them as data. | Fold the keys into `streaming.json` if the Architect prefers one file. |
| BD-M03b1-11 | Perf scenario drives the traffic | `downtown-rush` runs at 12 m/s along the Embarcadero line at the east edge of the synth grid (about half of the chunk ring is open water, which leaves room for the traffic in the tier object rows). The world view's `scenarioDriver` sends the camera position to the sim a few times a second as the traffic focus (debug op `traffic`), the world module's `store.want` follows `ambient.focusOverride` so the packs around the camera are resident, and `prepare` waits until the first fill is over. A scenario with `density: 'off'` now turns the traffic density to 0 for its run (`WorldScenarioDriver.prepare` gets an optional second argument). `check:perf` also judges `perf.<scenario>.<project>.<tier>.traffic.json` (instances against `crowd.maxTrafficInstances`, one draw, at least one car drawn). | `PerfReport` (C13) has no traffic field and a street-level view in a dense grid fills the mobile rows with chunks alone. | Add traffic counts to `PerfReport` in a C13 minor. |
| BD-M03b1-12 | Golden and the dist check | `packages/world/test/golden/traffic-synth.json` holds the bake id, seed, ticks, physics build, the preload list, 16 checkpoint hashes and the final hash; the Node spec and the `@world-hash` e2e (Chromium WebGL2 and WebKit) both compare with it. `apps/web/scripts/verify-dist.mjs` gains check 2f: a production build holds no `qa/world-hash` page, no `dvWorldHash` marker and no `traffic-synth` string. | AC-8, AC-9 and AC-12. | Regenerate under the `golden-update` label whenever the bake or the traffic rules change. |
| BD-FIX-VW-01 | Promotion and the parked slot (fix after M03b1, 2026-10-20) | `TrafficSim.promote` promotes an agent only when the `PromotionBook` accepts its key (`add` returns true); `promoteKey` likewise. `parkedPass` leaves a parked slot empty while its key is in the book, and while a vehicle obstacle (the player's car, a promoted car, a wreck: `kind 'vehicle'` of the step's obstacles) stands within the two bounding circles plus 0.5 m (`SLOT_CLEAR_MARGIN_M`) of the slot's pose. Spec: `packages/world/test/traffic.promotion-slot.spec.ts` (3 cases, all failed before the change). | Measured in the synth city on `chromium-webgl2` (tunnel drive of `tests/e2e/vehicle.spec.ts`, default traffic): `stats.promotions` went from 7 to 62 in 17 s, because the parked pass refilled a promoted car's slot in the same AI step, under the car that had hit it, and each refill was hit again (the book refused the key, but the agent was removed and counted anyway, and the `veh:amb.<cell>.<slot>.1` spawn failed); a parked van that reappeared beside the sedan (handle 8388693, then 9437269, same place) held it at x = -2130.7 for the last 300 s of the 420 s run. No new saved or hashed state; the `traffic-synth` golden and the other world unit specs pass unchanged. | Revert the two guards in `parkedPass` and the `add` checks in `promote` / `promoteKey`. |
| BD-FIX-VW-02 | The `@vehicle-world` runs empty the streets (fix after M03b1, 2026-10-20) | `bootWorld` in `tests/e2e/vehicle.spec.ts` sets the `traffic` and `parked` densities to 0 through `__dvBridge.world.call('density', …)` with the owner `e2e.streets` (not `e2e`, which the barrier test releases), and every teleport is followed by a wait until the sim's `traffic` report holds 0 agents. Courses, cruise speeds, end values, timeouts and every assertion are unchanged. | These tests prove the car against the world's surfaces (M03a1 AC-10) and were written before traffic existed. The tunnel street (synth `j` 19) is a one-way westbound local street with one lane on its centre line and parked cars 2.7 m either side; the sedan must drive east (BD-M03a1-10), so since M03b1 it meets the traffic head on: 4 to 7 promotions per run, and the run ends against a wreck in the lane (x = -2184.1 on `webkit-webgl2` here, CI run 39445770312 received -2184.0) or wedged against a parked car or a wreck (-2130.7, -2449.9). The sim rate is not the cause: 59.5 to 60.0 ticks per wall second with default traffic and 59.2 to 60.0 with density 0 on both software-GL browsers (tick p50 0.9 to 1.5 ms with traffic, 0.44 to 1.06 ms without); with the streets empty the drive takes 100 to 103 s at about 7 m/s with no stall. The barrier test counts snapshot records, which the traffic also changes. | Drop the density calls and `streetsClear`. |

After a seeded 3 to 6 s (180 to 360 ticks) it reports to the `WitnessBoard`, which emits one `world.witness` event per infraction; the witness is a key `amb:<cell>:<slot>` with a slot of 256 to 511 (`isPedKey`). | The milestone gives the delay, not the range; the sim event has no witness field, so the board's `WitnessEvent.witness` carries the key. | Add the witness class to the C06 `world.witness` payload with M03c. |

| BD-M03b2-04 | Hit is the canned fall | A vehicle at 3 m/s or more that touches a pedestrian puts it in `Hit` for 3 s (anim state 12) and calls the `hit` hook with the vehicle's velocity. With no baked chain the count is 0 and nothing is invented (the `synth` bake has none; the demo on `sf` shows them). | Scope: the milestone lists the movement and the counts. | Add the interactable with the mode milestones. |

The car driving to the player is not drawn: the traffic's robotaxi agents do not take a destination yet. An entrance stands (as `Interactable{Transit}`, key `pfb:transit.entrance.<id>`) while its station is within 600 m of the focus and its chunk is resident; interacting sends the rider to the next station of the line until the phone app lets the player choose. `TeleportService.fares` keeps its own distance-based record. | The distance rule of M02c1 cannot give 'three stops' for AC-6, and the station choice is UI work (M04b2). | Replace the default with the phone's choice; drop the old record when M04a2 binds the sink. |

As DL-664 part 2 says, the DataSF sources are `sfmta-stops` and `sfmta-patterns` (labels 'DataSF SFMTA Stops / Patterns'), the landmark seed is `lm.rail-terminus`, comments say SFMTA, and one docs note says 'Bay Rail'. `sources.json` is part of the bake id, so every area's `bakeId` changes (synth `291b90f0...`, was `51fa7c9c...`) and with it the world seed: both world goldens are regenerated. | Required by AC-10; no exception entry was added. | The `golden-update` label is needed on the PR; other open branches regenerate their goldens at rebase. |
| BD-M03b2-14 | Pedestrian bodies | Far level: one `InstancedMesh` per body of a 72-triangle figure (box torso, head, two legs, two arms) whose legs and arms swing in the vertex shader (TSL `positionNode`, a phase per instance advanced with the speed). Near level: one `SkinnedMesh` per figure (the parts of the M01b2 mannequin merged and bound to the 22 `bay_rig_v1` bones), driven by `MannequinAnimator`, so a near pedestrian is one render object (the player's mannequin is a group of 20). `@dv/engine` exports `./client/anim` for it. | The tier rows (100 render objects on `mobile-low`) cannot carry six 20-mesh figures. | Replace with the final bodies and outfits in M10d1 (Q-39). |

| BD-M03c-01 | Search and cooldown rule | The spec is silent, so: unseen for `losLostSec` opens a circle at the last known position; its radius is `growMps` times the seconds since it opened, up to the level maximum; the cooldown fills only while the player is outside the circle and unseen (times `hidingMultiplier` in a hiding spot), holds inside it, and a sighting returns to `pursuit` and resets it to 0. `packages/engine/src/sim/pursuit/search.ts`. | The milestone asks for exactly this and says to record it. |
| BD-M03c-02 | Sub-level decay | Points below the first level threshold fall by 2 per second, but only after 10 s without a new infraction (`SUB_LEVEL_IDLE_TICKS`). A level never falls by itself; only a completed cooldown, a bust or a scripted `clear` returns it to 0. | The contract has no decay value; points that never fade would make one red light a permanent debt, and decaying at once would make the AC-2 sums unobservable. |
| BD-M03c-03 | Checkpoints and the lazy resource | The hashed resource `pursuit` is registered the first time the director is touched (`onActivate`), so a session that never meets the pursuit hashes exactly as before and the M03b1 goldens stay valid. | BD-M03c-04 | Unit counts and PIT band | `levels[l].maxUnits` is the total of cruisers, SUVs and the helicopter; `suv` of them are SUVs; one unit is added per second. | BD-M01d2-01 | Media route edge cases | `parseRange` clamps an end past the size to `size - 1` and a suffix longer than the object to the whole object (RFC 9110), and treats `bytes=-0`, `a > b` and a start at or past the size as unsatisfiable (416). `Range` is ignored on `HEAD` (answers 200 with the full `Content-Length`). A `HEAD` body is dropped, not cancelled, because cancelling logs a workerd "reader canceled" exception. A binding exception answers 500 with `no-store` and the media headers. `OPTIONS` without `Access-Control-Request-Method: GET` or `HEAD` answers 405. The 503 of the kill switch carries `Retry-After: 300` and `no-store`. `onlyIf: request.headers` behaved correctly in the vitest pool, so the `etagDoesNotMatch` fallback was not needed. | The spec leaves these open; each is the RFC-default or the smallest reversible behaviour. | Edit `services/edge/src/routes/media.ts` and `services/edge/test/media.test.ts`. |

| BD-M04b2-01 | M04b2 | **CVD separation check: Machado 2009 simulation, CIE76, threshold 20.** `packages/ui/src/hud/cvd.ts` simulates protan, deutan and tritan with the severity 1.0 matrices of Machado, Oliveira and Fernandes (IEEE TVCG 15(6), 2009) on linear sRGB, converts to CIELAB (D65) and takes the Euclidean distance (CIE76). Each of the four palettes in `config/ui/palettes.json` passes when its ten semantic pairs are at least 20 apart under its own deficiency (`default` under normal vision); the threshold is recorded in the file (`cvd.minDeltaE`) and checked in `hud-status.spec.ts`. The shipped palettes measure 24.7 or more. Text contrast of every semantic colour on the dark plate is also at least 4.5:1. | The spec asks for an algorithm chosen at M04b2 and a threshold never lowered to pass. Machado 2009 is the standard physiologically based model; CIE76 is the simplest metric that is monotone in perceived difference, and 20 is a clearly visible gap for large shapes. | Swap the matrices or the metric in `cvd.ts`; raise `minDeltaE` (never lower it). |

Pause and Phone are ScreenDefs, so the XR panels draw them unchanged. | BD-M08a1-01 | M08a1 | `fromInputFrame` (FC5.1) reads the stick strictly beyond the threshold: `moveX`/`moveY` > 48 or < -48 set a direction bit, 47 and 48 stay neutral (the spec table says `moveY > +48`). Held bits are merged with the latched `pressed` edges so a sub-tick tap is never lost (C03 rule 2). `toInputFrame` is the inverse for QA drivers (left+right cancels on the stick, which is what SOCD makes of it). |
| BD-M08a1-02 | M08a1 | The `InputPattern` motion union has no `236236`; a move in `FighterDef.supers` (or `kind: 'super'`) reads its `236`/`214` motion as the doubled sequence (FC5.4: `236236` / `214214` + attack) with twice the `MOTION_WINDOW`. `360` also gets twice the window. Priority inside a class is the freshest completion (age of the later of press and motion), then the more complex pattern (numpad length: `623` is as complex as `236`), then kit order, so a `236236` with no meter throws the fireball and not the `623` it also contains. The dash double tap (`66`, `44`) requires pure forward or back taps whose return never touches up or down, so motions never dash. |
| BD-M08a1-03 | M08a1 | Buffer words 48..63 of a fighter, marked reserved in FC2, now hold `PushLeft`, `PushTimer`, `Stun`, `AirKind`, `MoveHype`, `InvulnKind`, `ThrowTech`, `RiseQuick`; 56..63 stay reserved. `FM.RulesFlags` also carries the round state `Sudden`, `SuddenNext` and `KoEnd` (bits 16..18). `FM.Reserved` is the one unhashed presentation counter. The layout is 504 words (about 2 KB), not the 1,000 the summary line estimates. `FightEventSink.push` receives one reused object: a sink that keeps events copies the fields. |
| BD-M08a1-04 | M08a1 | A press is consumed once: starting a move writes the tick to `FF.BufAge`, and the parser ignores presses at or before it. Presses made during hitstop count: `FF.BufMove` widens the buffers by the hitstop length until the next move starts (decaying one per free tick), so a cancel pressed in hitstop fires on the first free tick (FC3 step 3 "ingests input and buffers"). A press is "rising" against the previous ring entry; chords (`LP+LK`) need the rising edges within `CHORD_WINDOW` 2 frames. `CHORD_WINDOW`, `KNOCKDOWN` 30, `DASH_IMPLY` 8, `COMP_IDLE` 30, `CHECK_REFUND`, the Check and Clear frame constants, `ROUND_END`, `MATCH_END`, `TIME_UP`, `SUDDEN_HEALTH` and `KO_DIV` are added to `config/fighter.tuning.json` as named constants of values the spec states in prose or leaves open. |
| BD-M08a1-05 | M08a1 | The allocation test (AC-8) takes `gc` from V8 on request (`v8.setFlagsFromString('--expose-gc')` and `vm.runInNewContext('gc')`) instead of a separate vitest project, so it runs under plain `check:unit`. V8 allocates feedback vectors and boxed doubles while hot functions tier up; the 600-tick warm-up is followed by four unmeasured passes and the median of five measured passes (steady state: 0 to a few KiB per 5,940 ticks; the budget is 64 KiB). |
| BD-M08a1-06 | M08a1 | Check works while any Composure is left; a Check that cannot be paid in full burns the gauge to 0 and leaves the fighter Rattled (FC7 says "At 0 the fighter is Rattled", which 600-point Checks and a 6,000 gauge would otherwise reach only by luck). Clear the Air still needs the full 2,000. A successful parry refunds 300; the attacker is stunned 12 frames (state `HitStand`, no combo count). The taunt has no cooldown. |
| BD-M08a1-07 | M08a1 | Phases: `init` starts in `Ready` (round 1, positions reset); `Load` and `Intro` belong to the mode (M08a2 calls `setPhase(buf, Phase.Intro)`), though the core implements Intro's 300 frames and attack skip. `Cinematic` is entered from the Shutdown window when the winner taps `HY` and `rules.cinematics !== 'off'`; it lasts `CINEMATIC_HOLD` frames or ends on the first attack edge of either player, then the Shutdown finisher (the kit's `shutdown` move, 170 frames) runs. Shutdown is eligible after a final KO that is not a chip KO (`FF.LastHitBy` bit 8), by a single winner, with `rules.shutdowns`. A sudden round ends on the first hit (lower health loses, then Hype, then slot 0) and its winner wins the match. A KO or time-out in a round where both fighters then reach `roundsToWin` starts the sudden round; a round won by one fighter who reaches it ends the match. |
| BD-M08a1-08 | M08a1 | Hit resolution: candidates are collected against the start-of-step state and applied in (attacker slot, fighter hit, then that slot's projectiles) order; the move, EX flag and Simple flag of each candidate are captured at detection so a trade resolves symmetrically. A trade gives both fighters the larger hitstop. A strike beats a throw: the throw candidate is dropped when its victim also has a connecting strike. Throw tech: the victim is in a throw move and the two move frames differ by at most `THROW_TECH` 7. Counter hits need the defender in startup or recovery of a move (not stun). Armor takes 50 % of the scaled damage, no stun, and uses one armor hit. Projectiles other than `linear` move like `linear` until M08a2/M08b1 (the lint accepts them). `MoveDef.ex.startup` is accepted by the schema but not applied until the EX kits land (M08b1). |
| BD-M08a1-09 | M08a1 | `fighter:lint` additions beyond the spec list, each with a `bad-*.json` fixture: `hit-window` (a hit live outside its active frames), `cancel-range`, `spawn-ref`, `auto-ref`, `fx3-reference` (the PLAYER_FIGHTER kit's derived advantage equals the FX3 table). "Hit overlaps a hurtbox" sweeps the opponent from push contact to 6,000 u over every default stance (air moves also over 0..2,000 u of height). Multi-hit advantage uses the first hit and a move with any launching hit shows no on-hit advantage. The loop search walks the cancel graph (`chain` to stronger normals of the same stance, `special`/`super` to any special or super), flags a cycle as unbounded, and otherwise flags more than 40 hits or more than 70 % of health on one touch, with the scaling table applied. |
| BD-M08a1-10 | M08a1 | Goldens are authored by `packages/modes/fighter/scripts/make-goldens.ts` (closed-loop coach scripts that read the match while authoring; only the input streams are recorded) with `DV_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` and the `golden-update` label. They run from `init` (Ready phase) with `roundsToWin: 1`. `vs-001` (1,645 ticks): ten taunts fill the Hype bar, then chain, Uplift cancel, Spark Toss, throw, Surge, EX Uplift, Skid Kick and a KO. `vs-002` (2,440 ticks, 2,200-frame timer): blocked projectile, throw, throw tech, Check, Clear, Rattled chip, time-out. The replay records until `MatchEnd`. Because `FM.FrameDataHashLo/Hi` are hashed words, a verification against a changed kit differs at checkpoint 0; the refusal is `REPLAY_MISMATCH`, and `--force` names the first differing checkpoint. |

| BD-M09a1-04 | M09a1 | The waterfall gives the rounding cents to common, so a class payout can dip by a cent when `E` grows by a cent and two other classes round up together. The property suite (AC-2) therefore allows a dip of at most one cent per class between two exit values; sums, caps by `E`, order invariance and the exact vectors WF-1…WF-7 are asserted exactly. Making every class exactly monotone needs a house-monotone apportionment and was not worth the complexity for cents. |
| BD-M09a1-05 | M09a1 | `burnMul` of a thesis card multiplies all weekly expenses (salaries, infrastructure, marketing, office), not only `infra(users)` as the literal line of §5.3 reads; revenue is not scaled. Infrastructure cost per user falls by `infraReduction` (40 %) at an infra allocation of `infraFullAlloc` (30 %) or more and linearly below it. Reputation drifts +0.1 per profitable week and −0.05 per week otherwise. All of these are in `config/tycoon.balance.json` for M09a2 to tune. |
| BD-M09a1-06 | M09a1 | Exit math with SAFEs that never met a priced round: `settleSafes(table, E)` converts the open SAFEs at a notional pre-money of `max(E, 4 × the SAFE money)` with no new money and no pool top-up, then the waterfall runs. The floor keeps a near-empty exit from minting absurd share counts. A bridge note is an ordinary `discount` SAFE (id `bridge1`, 7,000 bp). |

Thesis is dealt by `ctrU32(seed, 'setup:thesis')` over the sorted thesis ids; the founder archetype, co-founder and start week are counter draws on `setup:founder`, `setup:cofounder`, `setup:week`. A raise ends at its close, or when its four meetings are used up, or `3 + 4 − 1` weeks after it started with nothing open; the raise occupies the founder (`raiseFocus` 0.2) meanwhile. | BD-M09a2-07 | M09a2 | Definitions the bands leave open. Run `i` of a policy uses `seed_i = splitmix32(seedBase + i)`; a run that reaches week 400 is ended by `endRun` and counted as `timedOut`. "Series A reached" and the other tiers count closed rounds; dilution is `amount / (preMoney + amount)` of closed seed and A rounds, in percent; "unicorn win" is the end kind `unicorn`. B4 compares `winRate` and `meanScore`. B5 (lethality) counts a root event only when the company was healthy when it fired (runway of 12 weeks or more and founder morale of 40 or more before the step) and the run was lost (`insolvent`, `ousted`, `burnout`) within 10 weeks; without the clause every common event would "cause" the losses of a hard game. B8 counts events per run as the mean over runs. B9 is suppressed events per week over all weeks. B10 runs the first 200 runs of the first policy twice and replays each from `(setup, choices)`. B11 counts a `counter` command outside the envelope (the core refuses `counter` until M09b2, so only a fixture bot issues one), a priced round whose close failed with `CAPTABLE_INVARIANT` (`stepWeek` counts every failed close in `events.counts['closeFail.<code>']`; a SAFE that no longer fits the stack limit after a re-trade is a collapsed deal, not a bug) and a waterfall that does not sum to the exit value. |
| BD-M09a2-11 | M09a2 | The baseline guard compares rates (win, unicorn, loss, Series A) with a 2 point limit and the median and 90th percentile run length with an 8 week limit, per policy, only when the baseline has the same run count and seed base as the run. The commit lines come from `DV_BALANCE_COMMITS`, else `git log <base>..HEAD` for `DV_BASE_REF`, `origin/$GITHUB_BASE_REF`, `origin/main` or `origin/claude/build`, else the last commit. A move past the limit with the line passes and says the file is stale; without the line it fails. |

The verifier `tycoon-sim` in `verifiers-lock.ts` locks the harness and `config/tycoon.bands.json`; `tests/tycoon/baseline.json` is left out so a balance change and its baseline can ship together under the commit line. The fixed table of safe event choices is in the file. `frugal` never raises before 16 weeks of runway. The policies are registered as `BotDriver`s `tycoon.median`, `tycoon.aggressive`, `tycoon.frugal` and `tycoon.random` (`tools/ci/tycoon-sim/drivers.ts`, `registerTycoonBots()`): a Tycoon run has no input frames, so `frame()` is the neutral frame and the driver adds `decide(view)`; it draws from the host's `bot` stream. The median hires toward a 38-week runway (the spec text says 55; see BD-M09a2-12). |

Bot side (policies are not content, so they move no version string): the `median` and `aggressive` bots start the Series A raise at $2.5M ARR (spec text: $1.5M) and the median's runway target for hiring is 38 weeks (spec text: 55); with the spec's $1.5M, B2 reached 59.9 % at 10,000 runs, 0.1 under its edge. Found by a seeded random search over about 20 values with 150 to 300 runs per candidate, then checked at 2,000 runs per policy: B1 152 weeks, B2 53.8 %, B3 4.0 %, B4 none above, B5 1.4 % worst, B6 1.2 % vs 84.8 %, B7 20.9 %, B8 40.3 events and 13.8 choice events, B9 0.3 %, B10 and B11 clean; at 10,000 runs the numbers move by less than a point. The seeds are fixed, so only a content or code change moves them. |

The count never shrinks. `TtsPlayer.clock(npcId)` is additive (`startS`, `endS`, `nowS`, `complete`); it returns null before the first sentence is scheduled and after the line ended, which the reveal reads as "nothing more to wait for" once it has seen a window. 2.5 words/s (150 a minute) is a design value for the estimate only; AC-3 uses a window whose duration is known. | The spec fixes the rule (never ahead of the audio, all at once with voice off, text speed 180 ms without an audio clock) but not what to do while sentences are still arriving. | Use the scheduled window alone (the first sentence then reveals everything). |

The card's Resources button expands fixed text; it has no link, so Close and Resources are the only focusable controls. | The spec lists the triggers but not the order of card, line and close; the badge cannot depend on `meta` where the offline path sends none. | Close at once on `done`; send no turn for a self-harm phrase. |
