| BD-FIX-CI6-A01 | The renderer host skips three's untimed loop call (fix round 6 A, 2026-10-23) | three r186 `Animation.start()` runs the loop once, synchronously, with no timestamp; its own `requestAnimationFrame` follows. `XRManager` restarts the window loop that way when an XR session ends (`_currentAnimationLoop`, then `start()`), and on session start its own `onAnimationFrame` drops the untimed call (`frame === undefined`). Engineer H's diagnosis also named the loop start; that is not reached, because `Renderer.init()` calls `start()` before `setAnimationLoop` installs the loop. The untimed call at session end gave `bootEngine` a NaN `dtSec`, the camera rig integrated it, and the flat camera stayed at NaN for good (observed: `observe().camera.pos` `[NaN, NaN, NaN]` on every frame after `endXr`). `RendererHost.tick` now returns at once for a missing or non-finite time, as three's XR loop does: no frame, listener or render. H's zero-length-frame variant and its `bootEngine` dt guard are dropped (one place absorbs three's contract). Unit spec `renderer-host.spec.ts` fails without the guard (3 of 3 frames expected, 5 seen). This was not why @xr-panel timed out (see A02). | Revert `renderer-host.ts` and its spec. |
| BD-FIX-CI6-A02 | The QA XR emulator paces frames on a GPU fence (fix round 6 A, 2026-10-23) | Root cause of `xr-panel.spec.ts:82` timing out (CI run 39891630677, job 120212835797): after `endXr` the page's main thread was blocked for 17 to 60 s, so `observe()` never returned inside the 30 s poll. A CPU profile across `endXr` shows 57.6 of 58.9 s in native code, not JS; a `readPixels` issued in XR just before `endXr` took 61.6 s. The XR frames had queued a GPU backlog: BD-M01b2-09 gives each IWER layer an offscreen framebuffer that nothing presents, so the compositor's back-pressure never reaches the XR loop; IWER runs it on every window frame (about 36 a second against about 5 stereo world frames SwiftShader draws), and the backlog came due when the canvas was presented again. The backlog grows with time in XR, so @xr-smoke (about 2 s in XR) passed and @xr-panel (about 6 s) did not. `installIwer` now wraps IWER's `XRSession.requestAnimationFrame`: the first callback of a device frame checks the fence set after the previous XR frame and, while the GPU has not finished it, every callback of the frame moves to the next device frame (the caller's handle stays valid for `cancelAnimationFrame`). QA builds only; a real headset runs none of it. Proof: the same `readPixels` takes 173 ms, `observe()` answers 6 ms after `endXr`, and `check:e2e -- --project=xr-quest3 --grep-invert "$GRAYBOX_GREP"` on a `DV_WORLD=synth` build passes 4 of 4 (@xr-panel, @xr-smoke, platform, @world-look). | Revert `paceEmulatedFrames` in `packages/engine/src/platform/probe.ts`. |
| BD-FIX-CI6-A03 | The creator spec hides every QA-only panel (fix round 6 A, 2026-10-23) | `creator.spec.ts:37` on webkit-webgl2 (job 120212835810): the trace shows the click point (1198, 238.9) of `creator-card-voice-deep` (box x 1148, y 190.9, 100 x 96) under `modes-quit` of the QA modes panel (M04a2, fixed at the right edge, `top: clamp(5rem,28vh,11rem)`) on every retry until the 300 s timeout. The spec hid only `.dv-npcdbg` (BD-M06a4-16). Whether the card's centre falls on the button depends on font metrics: locally both engines lay the card out 80 px tall (centre y 231) and the quit button at x 1134 to 1176, y 235 to 266, so the click passes; on CI's WebKit the card is 96 px tall and Playwright's hit test at its centre found the quit button (the panel's own text widths follow the font too). Choice: the test hides the QA panels, not the panel avoiding the page. The panels are engine debug overlays that production builds do not contain (`verify:dist`), the shell specs test the shipped layout, and no placement of a fixed overlay stays clear of every page at every viewport and font; the menus, touch and world-look specs already hide them for the same reason. `creator.spec.ts` now calls the shared `hideQaPanels` (BD-FIX-CI5-G03) before `goto`; its steps and assertions are unchanged. | Revert `creator.spec.ts`. |
| BD-FIX-CI6-B01 | The @vehicle-world driver throttles for 20 ticks per round and samples beside the control loop (fix after the e2e gap 5b2a561..b070901, 2026-10-23) | `tests/e2e/vehicle.spec.ts` `drive()`: "came out of the tunnel" failed on chromium-webgl2 shard 2 (run 39891630677, x = -2422.8) and passed on its retry. Instrumented locally (two tunnel runs side by side on 2 workers, the CI worker count: 2 of 4 failed, then 0 of 4, then a stall in each of two more batches, stopped after their first dump): no traffic, parked car or pedestrian anywhere near (agents 0, near 0, peds 0), the page renders about 11 fps, and one control round took 1.7 to 2.4 s, because it awaited four world queries before it played the next line. Each line held full throttle for 90 ticks, so on the tunnel's 25 % fall the sedan reached 14 to 18 m/s against a cruise of 6, hit the hood (body damage 1.0 to 0.93, then 0.78, yaw turned 40 to 70 degrees) and stayed wedged with throttle on for the rest of the 420 s. In the failing CI run the test ran beside world-bounds, world-hash and world-landmarks, three synth worlds; the retry ran beside lighter tests. No product change since the last green run moves the car; the shard mix and the page's frame rate decide the round time. Now the sampling (ray up, three `groundY`) runs one at a time beside the control loop, each round plays 20 ticks of the controller's throttle or brake and then brakes (70) until the next round replaces the line, and the last sample is still the car past the end. Courses, cruise speeds, end values, the 420 s budget and every assertion are unchanged. Each drive logs its sample count and top speed: 7.8 to 8.2 m/s in the tunnel (cruise 6) and 8.8 to 9.3 m/s on the overpass (cruise 8) over 4 runs on 2 workers. | A control loop that does not wait on world queries, and a line that cannot keep the throttle open when the page stalls. | If a sim-side cruise control lands (a `LineCommand` that holds a speed), use it and drop the brake tail. |
| BD-FIX-CI6-B02 | The orphan sweep and the pointer move each run in one store transaction (fix to M01b save rules, found through @modes on webkit, 2026-10-23) | "a remapped Jump is saved with the settings and is back after a reload" failed on webkit-webgl2 about 1 run in 4 (3 of 8 here). The cause is in the save layer, not in the controls publish: the sim publishes `sys.controls` only after `loadSlices` resolves (`packages/engine/src/sim/modes/index.ts`, `onInit`), so the reload saw default controls because the settings record was gone. `SaveServiceImpl.open` sweeps orphan records of every profile (C05 rule 1), and a second `SaveService` on the main thread opens while the sim worker writes: the QA save bridge on its first `list()`, and in the product the shell's saves port (`apps/web/src/shell-ports.ts`) or a second tab. A record put at step (c) is an orphan until step (e) moves the pointer, so the sweep deleted it, (e) moved the pointer onto nothing, and the next load fell back to default settings and wrote `lastSave {slot: settings, ok: false, error: CHECKSUM}` (captured in both failure shapes). `SaveStore` gains `sweepOrphans(profileId, keep)` and `commitRecord(rec, expectedGen)`, each one transaction over `pointers` and `records` (IndexedDB and memory stores): the sweep decides and deletes atomically, and (e) moves the pointer only with the record present and intact in the same transaction, putting it back from memory when a sweep took it. A missing record at (d) is no longer a CHECKSUM failure; a present record that does not verify still is. New unit cases in `packages/engine/test/save/atomic.spec.ts` (another service opening after step (c) or (d), both stores) fail before and pass after. The e2e now also requires the settings save to be `ok`. The test's own wait is right as it is, so it keeps waiting for `sys.controls` and does not poll for the remapped value. | Atomic store operations instead of a time window on the sweep: a window would leave a killed write's orphan in place and the next write of that slot would hit CONFLICT. | None. |
| BD-FIX-CI6-B03 | The tycoon CLI self-test runs its five `tycoon:sim` processes at once, with 20 B10 replays (fix after M09a2, 2026-10-23) | `tools/ci/tycoon-sim/test/balance.selftest.spec.ts` "the CLI exits non-zero on each fixture and zero on a clean B5/B11 control" timed out on the PR run's unit job (run 39901038462: 35,165 ms against the 30 s default; 21,525 ms on the push run of the same commit, 16,615 ms here alone). It called `main()` four times in a row inside one Vitest worker, and each call replayed 120 runs twice for B10 (about 60 % of each call: easy-mode 1.5 s of runs and 2.3 s of B10). Now the test starts the four fixtures and the clean control its name promised but never ran as real processes together (`node --import tsx tools/ci/tycoon-sim/cli.ts`, 120 runs each), and checks each exit code (`[1, 1, 1, 1, 0]`), the band each fixture must fail (B3, B5, B4, B11) and that the control fails none. The new `--determinism-runs <n>` flag (default 200, capped at `--runs`) sets B10 to 20 replays here, the count the `runSuite` cases of the same file use; `determinism.spec.ts` owns B10. Measured after: 9,974 ms alone (tycoon-sim suite, load average 3.5 from other work on the machine), 7,362 ms inside `pnpm check:unit -- packages/world packages/engine tools/ci` (193 s, 1,994 tests). No timeout was raised. `tools/ci/verifiers.lock.json` carries the refreshed `tycoon-sim` hash; no subject of that verifier changed. | Concurrent real processes and less redundant B10 work, so the default timeout keeps its headroom. | None. |
| BD-FIX-CI6-C01 | The service worker registers even when `load` fired before `main.ts` reached it (fix C, PR #6, 2026-10-23) | Preview run 39901038461 (job `preview`) failed `check:coi` on both browsers with "navigator.serviceWorker.ready did not resolve". Registration still waits for `load` and still happens only in `import.meta.env.PROD` builds. Unit specs in `apps/web/test/pwa-update.test.ts`. | Revert `main.ts` and `pwa-update.ts`. |
| BD-FIX-CI6-C02 | The precache installs six entries at a time (fix C, PR #6, 2026-10-23) | With registration fixed, the install itself outlasted `check:coi`'s 20 s wait: Workbox's `PrecacheController.install` fetches one entry at a time, and the precache grew to 175 entries (11.5 MB) in a QA build and 71 in a production build (M04b1 page chunks, the shell group, the extra QA inputs; main's staging has 30). Against the live Preview a manual registration took 20.9 s (Chromium) and 34.5 s (WebKit); behind a local proxy adding 100 ms per request the QA build took 17.6 s and 37.3 s, and `check:coi` failed on WebKit. Warming the HTTP cache first helped Chromium only (WebKit's service-worker fetches did not reuse it). `apps/web/src/sw-precache.ts` runs the same per-entry work through the public `PrecacheController` API (`getURLsToCacheKeys`, `getIntegrityForCacheKey`, `strategy.handleAll`, the request Workbox would build: `cache: 'reload'` for revisioned entries) with at most `PRECACHE_CONCURRENCY = 6` in flight, and rejects the install on the first failed entry, as Workbox does. `sw.ts` builds what `precacheAndRoute` did from a `PrecacheController` (same default cache name, `PrecacheRoute` defaults, `activate` cleanup, `createHandlerBoundToURL('/index.html')` for the navigation route). Behind the same 100 ms proxy: 6.7 s and 7.4 s; every unique entry is cached (170 of 175 manifest rows, five icon and manifest URLs are listed twice) and an offline reload is served, controlled and isolated. `check:coi`'s wait is unchanged. | Revert `sw.ts` to `precacheAndRoute(self.__WB_MANIFEST)` and delete `sw-precache.ts` and its spec. |

| BD-FIX-CI7-D01 | The e2e harness blocks service workers; only the service-worker specs allow them (fix D, PR #6, 2026-10-23) | Since BD-FIX-CI6-C01 the worker registers in every `vite build`, the e2e QA build included, and `clientsClaim()` makes it control each page a second or two into the test. Playwright's `page.route()` "will not intercept requests intercepted by Service Worker", and the docs recommend `serviceWorkers: 'block'` wherever a test intercepts requests (spec/03 section 6, row of this fix, with URLs). A/B on the same QA synth build, webkit-webgl2, 2 workers, CI retries: with the worker allowed, `npc-dialogue.spec.ts` :45, :77, :135 got the real Worker's ink answer ("Noted. | BD-FIX-CI7-D02 | `cutscene-player.spec.ts` :75 (route 27 absent, answer within 2.5 s) is not a service-worker failure; no code change here (fix D finding, 2026-10-23) | It fails with the worker blocked: candidate sources, SW blocked, chromium-webgl2, 2 workers: 4 of 4 runs at 3,619 to 4,818 ms, and 2,540 to 11,332 ms over both browsers in an earlier run (CI candidate run 39918869187: 2,633 ms chromium, 3,642 and 5,315 ms webkit). Timeline (page `performance.now()`): the glue notes the request, then awaits the QA stand-in media unlock (`engine.media.trigger()`, 1.9 s on webkit where the three blank plays time out at 1.5 s, 2.9 s on chromium), and only then calls `player.request`, which answers 30 to 960 ms later; so the 2.5 s window of section D7 starts after the unlock. That change was reverted. The cause is the software-GL main thread at mission start, beside the QA unlock placed before the clock; making the beat's first frame cheap (precompiled fallback materials) or starting a QA `?mission=` after the world reveal changes engine or AC behaviour and is left to the Architect. Local timings were taken at load average 13 to 21 on 4 cores (three fixers). | Not applicable (no change). |

Proof: `platform-host.spec.ts:27` then `:39` in one worker, 10 times on chromium-webgpu (`--workers=1 --repeat-each=10`): 20 of 20 passed, :39 in 6.8 to 9.2 s. No test timeout changed. Unit case in `tools/ci/test/workflows.test.ts` keeps the list equal to wrangler.jsonc and checks the env. Change, in every job that installs with `--with-deps` (ci.yml e2e-shard and perf, nightly.yml perf, e2e-full and determinism, preview.yml): `actions/cache/restore` of `~/.cache/dv-pw-apt/*.deb` (key `pw-apt-<os>-<pnpm-lock hash>`, restore-keys `pw-apt-<os>-`), an apt.conf.d fragment that sets `Dir::Cache::archives` to that directory, the unchanged install, then `actions/cache/save` right after it on a miss (spec/03 section 6). A hit leaves apt only the packages the runner image updated since. The four shards of S01 stay. Proof: `workflows.test.ts` (each install is preceded by the restore and the apt fragment and followed at once by the save); the fragment simulated with apt-get on Ubuntu 24.04 (51.0 kB, then "Need to get 0 B"). Not provable here: a run on GitHub's runners (ci.yml runs on `claude/integ-**`, not on this branch); the first run after the merge still downloads on a miss. Old .deb files of updated packages stay in the cache until the lockfile key's restore falls back to a newer entry |
| BD-FIX-CI10-R01 | The flat world is not drawn while a cutscene hides it: the armed frame is held while the clip resolves, `poster.webp` covers the pre-roll, `last.webp` the mid-play stall (fix to M05b2 AC-8 and AC-9, Architect direction for round 10, PR #6, 2026-10-23) | `cutscene-player.spec.ts:131` and `:167` failed on both tries on chromium-webgl2 shard 1 of run 40017655149 (job 120634029149): no video within 60 s, the player fell back with `start_stall`. The failed try's trace (Resource Timing of the page) shows the start chain waiting on the main thread, not on the network: route 27 answered in 487 ms, the manifest was asked for 837 ms after that answer and arrived in 18 ms, the poster and `v768.mp4` were asked for 730 and 975 ms after the manifest, so the rendition request went out about 2.3 s into the 2.5 s window and the first frame could not arrive in time; between those hops SwiftShader drew the full synth world (100 to 600 ms a frame, BD-FIX-CI9-K03). Reproduced here on the committed integ-ci9 code as ci.yml runs the synth half (DV_WORLD=synth QA build, `--grep-invert "$GRAYBOX_GREP"`, CI=1: 2 workers, 1 retry, both spec files): `:131` and `:156` failed both tries, `:167` passed only on its retry. Spec: section D7 step 2 says that while the player resolves a ref `poster.webp` (or, if none, the armed in-engine frame) covers the screen, and 11-platform-pwa section 7 that the host stops the loop under the clip; the code stopped the loop only once the first frame was in (`DomCutsceneHost.show`) and showed no poster, and after a mid-play stall it drew the world behind the opaque `last.webp`. Change: `RendererHost.holdWorld(kind, armed?)` (an explicit, counted signal; release is idempotent) skips the backdrop, world and post passes of flat frames while the loop keeps running, so frame listeners and `frameHook` (the engine hand-off whose frame signals tick the sim) run every frame and the menu scene and XR frames are always drawn; `cover` holds from the next frame, `frame` draws until `armed()` is true (that frame included), then holds the canvas's last frame, and a resize draws it once more; `heldFrames` counts skipped frames, and a held frame submits nothing to the QA GpuPacer. `DomCutsceneHost` uses it through `LoopControl.holdWorld` (optional, so the QA media page's stand-in loop draws as before): `holdFrame()` (the page's `frameArmed`: the newest snapshot tick has reached the tick of the `cut.request`, whose snapshot carries the armed DirectorScript's first shot), `holdPoster(url)` (black layer with the poster, HUD hidden, sim not paused because it has not heard `video` yet), `holdStill` now holds a `cover` while it restarts the loop for the sim, `show` drops poster and holds and stops the loop and pauses the sim as before, `hide` and the cue-only fallback layer (`showCues`, translucent) draw the world again. `CutscenePlayerImpl` calls `holdFrame` when a single ref (or a sequence's first item) passes the checks without network (none for `?beats=fallback`, `setting_off`, `policy`, `offline` or an unknown ref) and `holdPoster` before the pre-roll of a clip that is not under another clip; every fallback answer releases the held frame in the same step (`showCues`), and a late clip shows no poster. Audio sync (`AudioSync`, `requestVideoFrameCallback`) and the sim's pause under the clip are unchanged; the sim keeps ticking behind the held frame, the poster and the still. QA bridge: `__dvBridge.cutscene.state()` adds `host.held` (`stopped`, `cover`, `frame` or null) and `heldFrames`; `cutscene-player.spec.ts` asserts `stopped` under the clip, `cover` under the still, null under the fallback's cues and at the end, and records `video-ms` (request to `video` answer) and `held-frames`; no wait, timeout or threshold changed. Unit cases: `renderer-host.spec.ts` (5), `dom-host.test.ts` (5, and the `holdStill` order now has `hold:cover`), `player-triggers.test.ts` (6). After the change, same reproduction on chromium-webgl2: the five proof runs and the three A runs of R02 (the committed code) passed 11 of 11 each with no retry (load 11 to 23 on 4 cores, a second agent's e2e running); every clip test answered `video`, in 443 to 1948 ms from the request (24 of 24), with 11 to 31 frames held per clip start, and `:94` (route 27 absent) answered in 322 to 666 ms; shard 1/4 of the synth half as ci.yml runs it (2 workers, retries 1, its neighbours): every player test passed on the first try. webkit-webgl2, 22 runs: all six player tests passed on their first try in 20, `?beats=video` passed on its retry in two, and `:140` failed both tries in one (try 1: the clip fell back within 0.5 s of the request, try 2: the boot mission never started after the gate released, "sim worker stalled"); the cutscene-spike tests were flaky twice (`:29`, `:104`). The integ-ci9 baseline at the same load: 8 webkit runs, `?beats=video` passed on its retry once and spike `:29` failed both tries once; so these WebKit results are not caused by this change. The failure log of one such `?beats=video` try (the new afterEach attachment) shows the clip answered `video` 627 ms after the request and was ended as skipped 300 ms after it showed, before any key press of the test: an unrequested skip, left as an open question. `cutscene-spike.spec.ts:115` (F9 on the game page, the M04c spike path, which does not use the CutscenePlayer or these holds) fails here under load on both builds: `--repeat-each 4`, two rounds each, first tries failed 2 of 8 on this build and 3 of 8 on integ-ci9 (each passed on its retry); in its trace the page takes 2.8 s to take the Escape key and the 3 s wait times out while the desktop-tier world frame drawn after the clip runs; it passed in CI run 40017655149. | Revert the `holdWorld` calls in `dom-host.ts` and `player.ts` (the RendererHost API can stay unused). |

B: 33 of 33 passed; answer 304, 508, 792 ms (mean 535); clip start 924, 562, 767, 635, 1263, 1269, 996, 1605, 1119 ms (mean 1016), 9 of 9 `video`. | BD-FIX-CI10-R03 | @ui-pause bounds the pause by page frames: the signal that stopped the sim went out no later than the frame in which Pause shows (review note W04 on BD-FIX-CI9-W04, round 10 R, 2026-10-23) | W04 made `hud.spec.ts:146` wait up to 30 s for the sim's report that it runs paused, so nothing bounded how long the pause took to reach the sim. M04b2 AC-6 says that when `client.Pause` is pressed, Pause opens and the sim tick stops; 10-ui-hud-xr.md section 2.10 rule 2 says Pause sets `FrameSignal.paused`, and C02 rule 3 that the sim pauses on `paused: true`. The bound is therefore the frame signal, not a wall time (a software GPU draws a frame in 0.1 to 2 s, so any fixed time would either be loose or flaky). Change: `SimRuntime` keeps `pausedSince`, the `FrameSignal.frame` of the first paused signal of the pause in force (`pump` coalesces a run of signals into a group that keeps its first signal's frame), and every `StatsReport` carries it (null while running; engine envelope, not a contract; `isFromWorkerMessage` checks it), so a page that misses the report of the turn still learns it; `EngineObservation.simPausedSince` is the newest report's value. The spec reads the observation when Pause is visible (`paused` must be true and `frames` is the number of signals sent), still waits for the report to travel back (the 30 s is now only that trip), then asserts `simPausedSince <= frames at Pause`, and keeps the 800 ms window (reported tick and snapshot tick move at most 1, still paused). New case in `transport.spec.ts` (a pump with one running and two paused signals reports `pausedSince` 2, every report of the pause repeats it, the first resumed report has null). Proof: `--grep @ui-pause` on chromium-webgl2 (DV_WORLD=synth QA build, CI=1), four runs, 2 of 2 passed each, none on a retry; the `pause-frames` annotations (signals sent when Pause showed / the signal that paused the sim / signals sent when its report was read): 31/22/37, 32/23/38, 30/22/36, 33/24/39. | Revert `pausedSince` in `runtime.ts`, `envelope.ts` and `boot-engine.ts` and the frame check in `hud.spec.ts`. |
| BD-FIX-CI10-M01 | The attribution corner's 5 s are timed in the page from the moment the map opens (test fix to the M02c2 AC-8 proof, round 10 M, PR #6, 2026-10-24) | `world-map.spec.ts:234` @world-attribution failed on chromium-webgl2 shard 4 on both tries with "corner collapsed after about 5 s" at 2923 and 2953 ms (run 40017655149, job 120634029251), and at 2574 and 2924 ms on the base run 39902246464 (job 120246753121). The check started its clock (`Date.now()` in Node) only after `toHaveAttribute('data-state', 'expanded')` and `toContainText('OpenStreetMap')` had returned. Each of those calls is a round trip that waits for the page's main thread, and SwiftShader keeps that thread busy with the synth world while world-look desktop fog-west runs on the other worker. The product counts from somewhere else: `MapApp` starts a 5000 ms `setTimeout` in its mount effect (wall clock; Preact runs effects after the next frame, or after its 35 ms fallback), which is the map opening that spec/domains/02-world-maps.md §6 rule 2 and M02c2 AC-8 time from. The CI trace of the first try shows the press of `row-phone-apps-map` performed at 1066466 ms (done at 1067342), the corner still expanded at 1072431 and collapsed at 1072787, so the product collapsed 5.1 to 6.3 s after the press. The test's clock started at 1070143, 2.8 s after the press, after the two checks took 906 and 1164 ms. A local shard-4 run with in-page stamps read: corner rendered 2.9 ms after the click event, collapsed 5515.6 ms after it appeared, while the Node clock read 3874 ms. So the test was wrong and the product is unchanged. Change: the init script's MutationObserver, which already timed the splash (BD-M02c2-12), now also watches `data-state` and stamps the corner's first appearance (that state must be `expanded`) and its turn to `collapsed`. The test requires 5000 ms ≤ collapsed − open ≤ 10000 ms, where it used to accept ≥ 3500 ms from a late clock; this tightens the gate. An annotation records both durations. The reopen and credits checks are unchanged. Proof: shard 4/4 of the DV_WORLD=synth half as ci.yml runs it (CI=1, 2 workers, `--grep-invert "$GRAYBOX_GREP"`, one DV_WORLD=synth QA build). chromium-webgl2 ran 5 times and @world-attribution passed on the first try each time, with the corner at 5642, 5745, 5454, 5925 and 5456 ms and the splash at 3126 to 4939 ms. Exits were 0, 1, 0, 0 and 0: run 2 failed and run 4 was flaky on world-look `mobile noon-street` (fractionWithin 0.986 to 0.989), a golden test this change does not touch, which also flaked on the pre-change baseline run here and passes in CI. webkit-webgl2 ran 2 times, exit 0, with the corner at 5096 and 5149 ms (24 passed, 11 skipped each). `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, and `tools/ci` unit-collect and workflows specs all passed |
| BD-FIX-CI10-P01 | Playwright's system packages install through `tools/ci/pw-install.ts`: only the engines a job launches, a download-only prefetch in time-boxed attempts with mirror rotation, and an apt cache saved under `always()` with a race-free key (CI, round 10 P, PR #6, 2026-10-24) | Run 40017655149 (ca4404f): perf was cancelled at its 45-minute limit inside `playwright install --with-deps chromium webkit`, and webkit-webgl2 shard 2 and xr-quest3 shard 2 spent 47 min in the same step: "Fetched 118 MB in 46min 13s (42.5 kB/s)" from azure.archive.ubuntu.com, while the same runners fetched the mirror's indexes at 9.9 MB/s and 14 of the 17 jobs fetched everything in 35 to 60 s. BD-FIX-CI9-T02's cache could not help: every candidate is a new branch, which restores only its own and the default branch's caches (spec/03 section 6), so all 17 jobs of a first run start together with nothing to restore (the perf log: "Cache not found for input keys: pw-apt-Linux-7a8ab9f5..., pw-apt-Linux-"); its save ran only after a successful install, and one key shared by 17 concurrent jobs. Change, in every job that installs Playwright with system packages (ci.yml e2e-shard and perf, nightly.yml perf, e2e-full and determinism, preview.yml preview): (1) a job-level `DV_PW_BROWSERS` with the engines the job launches (webkit-webgl2 legs `webkit`, other projects `chromium`, chromium-webgl2 shard 1 `chromium webkit` for check:coi, perf and preview `chromium webkit`); a Chromium-only leg needs 17 files and 25.9 MB instead of about 190 files and 128 MB. (2) `pw-install.ts`: apt points at the cache directory with `Acquire::Retries "5"` and `Acquire::http::Timeout "30"` (a stalled transfer fails, and the runner's mirror list moves on); `install-deps --dry-run` exit 0 installs the browsers only; otherwise up to two `install-deps` runs in apt download-only mode under `sudo timeout --kill-after=20 180`, the second on the mirror list rotated so archive.ubuntu.com is first (put back if the rotated mirror was cut too and slower); then the unchanged `playwright install --with-deps`, unbounded, whose exit status is the step's, so a missing package, a broken mirror or a failed browser download still fails the job (a prefetch never does). Cut attempts lose nothing: finished files stay and apt resumes a partial one (Builder-observed, spec/03 section 6). (3) The restore key is unique per run attempt and job (`pw-apt-<os>-<browsers>-<lock hash>-<run_id>-<run_attempt>-<job>-<job-index>`) and restores the newest cache of the same browser set through two restore-keys; an `if: always()` step counts the .deb files added since the restore (`--count-new`), and `actions/cache/save` runs under `always()` when that count is above 0, also on a failed or cancelled job (the perf log shows `always()` steps running after the 45-minute cancel). No gate, timeout or test changed; `timeout-minutes` stay. Proof: `pw-install.test.ts` (11 cases: the plan on a healthy runner, a slow mirror, a prefetch error, a stuck download-only switch, no mirror list, bad arguments) and `workflows.test.ts` (every install restores, installs through the script, counts and saves under `always()`; no raw `playwright install --with-deps` left; the browser set of all 16 ci.yml legs and the nightly legs evaluated against the projects' engines); `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`. Not provable here: a slow runner on GitHub (whether archive.ubuntu.com is faster there is untested; the log prints each attempt's rate). spec/08 section 3 still says browsers install with `--with-deps chromium webkit` "(not cached)"; the Architect may align it. Old .deb files of updated packages stay in the saved set until the lockfile part of the key changes |

The loop lives in `CatalogClient` (`retry` option, reusing `MAX_ATTEMPTS` and `backoffMs` of `fetch-retry.ts`), not in `FetchRetry`, whose lanes and pack-only fault hook the catalog does not use; `AssetService` always passes it, a bare `CatalogClient` tries once as before. | BD-FU-B02 | M05b2 (fix FU-B, 2026-10-24) | A sequence item that fails before its first frame under the previous clip uncovers the world for its in-engine beat; the covering element goes back to the pool only once it is off screen (follow-up B, review minor 3, 2026-10-24) | Back to back, `conclude` keeps the host up for the next clip, so the next item finds `host.visible` true, holds no poster, and on `start_stall`, `codec` or `media_error` (`failEarly`) hid nothing (`shown` and `postered` were both false): `showCues` returned early because the host was visible, the DOM host kept the loop stopped and the sim paused, and the sim could not run the DirectorScript the spec says runs in-engine (section D7 step 3). `failEarly` now also hides the host when it inherits the previous clip's cover. Taking that cover down calls `releaseDecoder` on the previous element, which `conclude` had already given back to the pool, so a third item pre-rolled meanwhile could be loading into it (pool order, first free) and would be blanked; the kept element now goes back to the pool (`Active.kept`) only when the next clip's `host.show` replaces it or `failEarly` hides it, while its budget slot and decoder are released at once as before (the pool has 3 elements and ping-pong uses 2). (2) A tap is now a press that began on the overlay during this showing and is let go there (`pointerdown` then `pointerup` of the same `pointerId`, primary button, trusted; `pointercancel` drops it; `reset()` at every showing forgets a press from before); Escape ignores key repeats and untrusted events; the skip button stops `pointerdown` from arming the overlay; the pad keeps its edge rule. Unit: `cutscene-overlay.test.tsx` (2 new cases). | Revert `SkipController.pointerDown/pointerUp` to a plain `tap()` on `pointerup`. |

| BD-FU-B05 | CI (fix FU-B, 2026-10-24) | The Playwright apt cache has one stable key per package set and ISO week, saved once per key (follow-up B to BD-FIX-CI10-P01, review minor 5, CI, 2026-10-24) | Round 10 keyed the cache per run attempt and job (`…-${run_id}-${run_attempt}-${job}-${job-index}`) and saved whenever new .deb files were downloaded, so a new branch's first run could save about 16 caches of 25 to 128 MB, and every later run of that branch saved again, toward the 10 GB repository limit that evicts the least recently used caches (spec/03 section 6). Now, in every job that installs Playwright with system packages (ci.yml e2e-shard and perf, nightly.yml perf, e2e-full and determinism, preview.yml preview): a `pw-key` step (`pw-install.ts --cache-key --week "$(date -u +%GW%V)" $DV_PW_BROWSERS`) writes `key=pw-apt-<RUNNER_OS>-<RUNNER_ARCH>-<os-release ID+VERSION_ID>-<engines, sorted, joined by +>-pw<installed @playwright/test version>-<ISO week>`, `version=` (the same without the week) and `base=` (the same without the version and the week); the week is the ISO 8601 week-numbering year and week, `date -u +%GW%V` (e.g. `2026W43`), computed in that step and passed as `--week`; the restore uses `key` and the restore-keys `<key>-` (partial caches of this key), `version` (the newest cache of the same set and Playwright, i.e. last week's) and `base` (the newest cache of the same engines under an older Playwright); the complete set is saved under `key` only when the install step succeeded, the restore was not an exact hit (`cache-hit != 'true'`; caches are immutable) and new archives were downloaded; a failed or cancelled install saves what it downloaded under `<key>-partial-<run_id>-<run_attempt>-<job>-<job-index>`, which the next restore takes by prefix, so round 10's resilience (time-boxed download-only prefetch, mirror rotation, archives kept from a cut or cancelled job) stays. Both saves are `continue-on-error: true`: when several jobs of one run save the same key, the docs do not say what the losers' saves do, and a refused save must not fail a job. Result: a set is saved once per week per branch scope (three sets: `chromium`, `webkit`, `chromium+webkit`), and once the default branch has saved a set for the week, which pushes to main do, branches restore it as an exact hit and save nothing. The runner image version is not in the key: GitHub documents no variable for it (spec/03 section 6), and the os-release pair plus the Playwright version decide the package list; archives that the image already has are not fetched. The week part (integration review note, 2026-10-24): without it the key changed only with Playwright, so every package Ubuntu updated after the save was downloaded again by every install until then; now the week's first install starts from last week's set, downloads only what changed, and saves the refreshed set under the new week's key, so the archive refreshes once a week with one save per key, and a week's caches age out under GitHub's 7-day unused rule (spec/03 section 6). Proof: `workflows.test.ts` (each install keyed with the step's ISO week, restored with the three prefixes, installed, counted, saved complete then partial, in that order; no run, attempt, job or lockfile part in the key step or the restore key), `pw-install.test.ts` (the key, prefix disjointness between sets, versions and weeks, the ISO week format, the CLI's `--week`, os-release parsing); `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`. Not provable here: the cache service on GitHub. | Drop `--week` and the `version` restore prefix (one key per set until Playwright changes), or restore the BD-FIX-CI10-P01 key and the single save step. |

The QA pursuit panel's own star readout also became heat bars (`▮`/`▯`), a QA-only presentation change with no test id or text dependency. |

Each regex is `\b(?:a\s+b|c)\b`, flag `i`; `\s+` keeps identifiers and usernames (normalized without separators) from matching. The guard test parses the alternatives from the config, so it holds no phrase itself. Single-word alternatives exist only in `trainer-menu` (the placement tool's name); the username-normalization case therefore covers multi-word alternatives only. |

A click needs several round trips, so a 2 s budget could never finish and the loop cancelled each attempt half way for the full 60 s. Reproduced here with `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate` 12 on a temporary copy: the old spec failed in `openTab`, the new one passed (5.1 min). | BD-M04b5-01 | M04b5 | The `ui.button({id, label, onClick})` registration API is rejected, not deferred: (1) §2.14 rule 5 makes the hooks read-only, for translators and accessibility tools, and a page-global registration would let any script in the app origin (an extension's main-world script) add controls that run with the app's privileges, outside the C14 permissions, provenance and moderation; (2) UI mods already render only in the C14 sandbox (`ModUiHost`, M13a5), so a launcher entry for them is an Architect decision for M13a7; (3) a callback button cannot be drawn by `CanvasScreen` on the XR panels, where every `ScreenDef` widget must work; (4) it would be a public API to version with no v1 consumer. | The milestone's Scope out states the rejection and asks for this row. | Revisit only through the Architect, with a C14-sandboxed design. |
| BD-M04b5-02 | M04b5 | `FpsFeed` emits when at least `periodMs` (1000) of fed time has passed since its last emission, starting with the first frame that has an interval; the shell's overlay hooks keep their own 1000 ms floor on `fps` signals (`createOverlayHooks`, by the injected clock), so the stream stays at one a second even if a port emits faster. `createPlatformPort` installs one frame listener for all frame-stat subscribers, with the first, and removes it with the last. | The spec gives the rate for the feed and for the stream but not which side enforces it; a floor on both keeps the stream bounded under a fake or a faster port. | Drop the hooks' floor and trust the port. |
| BD-M04b5-03 | M04b5 | `Widget` `choice.disabled` greys the whole row: `aria-disabled` on the radio group and on each radio, the reason as visible small text (`data-testid="reason-<id>"`) and as the radios' `title`; the radios stay focusable and a press does nothing. The canvas panel marks each option item disabled, so a ray press does nothing there either. `SettingsEnv.framePacer` and `.fullscreen`, and `PauseEnv.fullscreen`, are optional, so existing callers and fixtures need no change. | The spec names the field and the attributes; the optional env fields keep earlier specs unchanged. | Make the env fields required and update every fixture. |
| BD-M04b5-04 | M04b5 | `translate="no"` is written in JSX as `translate={false}`: Preact assigns the DOM property, which reflects as the attribute `translate="no"` (checked by the e2e on both WebGL2 projects). `data-ui-skip` is an empty attribute. The `#dv-engine` container gets both from `main.ts` with `setAttribute`. | Preact's JSX types only allow a boolean for `translate`; a string would assign the property a truthy value, which reads as `yes`. | Set the attributes through a ref callback. |
| BD-M04b5-05 | M04b5 | `normalizePrefs` spreads the raw record first, then the defaults, then the validated fields, so unknown top-level keys survive and every known key is still validated. | BD-FIX-SPAWN-01 | M05c1, M05c2 (fix S, 2026-10-25) | A new game's first auto mission no longer places the player itself. Root cause of the owner's report ("the character starts by falling", PR #9 preview, `?play=story&story=1`): `StoryController.placePlayer` wrote the Yard position (`config/autoplay/mission-starts.json` default, 5.5 km from the City Hall boot spawn) straight into `Transform` on the first trigger pass, with `y = groundY ?? resolver y`; the Yard's chunks were not resident then, so both were 0 and the player fell through the void while m.a1.01 started and its cast spawned at the Yard (observed on the preview: x 2718.3, z 4772.7, y −6 at tick 112, −1030 at tick 1710). Now the move is `ctx.world.teleport.request('respawn', {x, z, yaw})` (02-world-maps.md §5: pre-stream the destination tile and its ring, place on resident ground within 40 m, fade, ownership machine 700/701), and the mission starts on the next trigger pass after the teleport answers `ok`. A transient answer is asked again: `world.teleport.notLoaded`, `world.teleport.busy`, or no answer within 1,800 ticks (30 s; the teleport's own limits are 8 s of pre-stream and 15 s of move), which counts as `notLoaded`. Retries are spaced out (60 ticks after the first answer, the gap doubling to at most 600 ticks) and go on while the wait since the first request is under 10,800 ticks (3 min of sim time, so slow mobile streaming still gets there). The budget is checked when a retry is scheduled and again when it would be sent, so no request goes out once 10,800 ticks have passed since the first; the last one may leave just inside the budget, and its transient answer, or its 1,800-tick timeout, ends the wait: at worst 10,800 + 1,800 = 12,600 ticks (3.5 min) after the first request (amended by BD-FIX-SPAWN-04). While it waits, the non-blocking info toast `mission.startLoading` shows once the wait has lasted 300 ticks, then at most once every 1,200 ticks (20 s). Only a hard refusal (water, blocked, out of bounds, forbidden, heat, cooldown), a transient answer once the budget is spent, or a gray-box world leaves the mission unstarted (since BD-FIX-SPAWN-04 it stays `available` and Jobs offers a Start that tries again, DL-734), with the warning toast `mission.startUnreachable` and telemetry `mission.fail {reason: 'unresolved'}` (03-story.md: a start that does not resolve disables the mission, never a broken start); an `ok` answer, even from the last request, starts it. A gray-box world is detected the way `fallRecoverySystem` detects it, by the absence of a baked world: `ModeContext.world` is then the engine's stand-in, whose `resolver.bake` is `WORLD_STANDIN_BAKE` (`'graybox'`) and which refuses every teleport `not-loaded`, so the story treats it as a hard refusal at once (toast reason `no-world`) instead of waiting 3 minutes, and a gray-box `?story=1` never drops the player into the void. All limits are sim-tick counters (C02), never the wall clock. While the move runs, no other trigger fires. The same race exists on the `sf` bake (the Yard start is 17 m from La Salle Ave in the raw DataSF streets, inside the bake, and just as far outside the boot set), so the fix is not synth-only. | The player must start a story mission at its start, grounded, after the ground there is resident; the world's teleport is the specified single entry for such moves. | Put `placePlayer` back in `checkTriggers`. |
| BD-FIX-SPAWN-02 | M02c1 (fix S, 2026-10-25) | Out-of-the-world safety net (the specs are silent on a player under the ground; 02-world-maps.md §4.1 covers the water and the play-bounds wall only): `fallRecoverySystem` (`packages/engine/src/sim/control/fall.ts`, stage Control, order −9, registered by `registerControlSystems` next to swim) puts an on-foot player that has been in the air for 120 ticks (2 s, `CharacterBody.airTicks`) while it is under `play.minY` of `config/world.bounds.json` (−80 m), or more than 3 m under the lowest resident surface at its (x, z) (`WorldQuery.groundY(x, z, −1e6)`), back on the ground: the nearest walkable point within 60 m, else the top of the ground at its (x, z), else (nothing resident) a target at y 150 m whose ground the ownership machine's 701 probe (±200 m) finds once the colliders arrive. The move is the ownership machine's `requestTeleport` (not `TeleportApi.request`, so no mode rule, heat or cooldown can refuse a rescue, DL-735 a) with the teleport's own `world.teleport` out/in fade (DL-735 b; amended by BD-FIX-SPAWN-09), plus the SimEvent `control.recover` (`s`: `below-bounds` or `below-ground`). It does nothing without a baked world (`services.world.api` null), so the gray-box scenes and their hashes are unchanged, and it leaves a driver alone (the ownership machine teleports only the on-foot owner). | A net for any future placement or collision gap, so a player is never left falling for good. | Remove the system from `registerControlSystems`. |
| BD-FIX-SPAWN-03 | M05c2 (fix S, 2026-10-25) | e2e `tests/e2e/story-start.spec.ts`, tag `@story-start` (not matched by `GRAYBOX_GREP`'s `@story( \|$)`, so it runs in the `DV_WORLD=synth` half): `?play=story&story=1` must start m.a1.01 within 120 s with the player never more than 40 m (the teleport's search radius) from the Yard while the mission is active, grounded within 1 m of the resident ground and never under it over the next 300 ticks, and every spawned cast member within 60 m; a player pushed 25 m under the terrain by `debug.edit` must stand on the ground again within 3 s of sim time (snapshot ticks). | The owner's report as a test, on both WebGL2 projects. | Delete the spec. |

Each phase is now one `page.evaluate` that works in chunks of 32 with `Promise.all`; the spec still checks every resolvable ref with `has`, every world pack by SHA-256 with the network failing, and an unchanged network counter, and it reports the failing refs in one list. | BD-M06c-11 | M06c (repair of the refused merge, 2026-10-25) | Four causes, found by A/B builds of the merge candidate on `webkit-webgl2` (shard 4 of the synth half, 29 tests, three runs per variant; the base `claude/build` failed 0 of 87, the candidate 3 to 6 per run). (a) **WebKit crashes** (`Target page crashed` / `Target page, context or browser has been closed`, a `WPEWebProcess` segfault at the same address in `libWPEWebKit` each time, seen in `dmesg`): the page's first read of `GET /api/v1/roster` by the flags feed (`apps/web/src/npc-agency.ts`) runs in the busiest seconds of the world boot, beside the boot's own requests and the dialogue panel's poll of the same route. Variants: agency on, feed on 13 failures in 261 tests; sim module on, main-thread agency off 1 in 87; agency on with the feed off 0 in 87; feed on and its first read delayed 20 s after the age screen 0 in 87 (base 0 in 87). The feed now starts `FEED_DELAY_MS` = 20 s after the age screen (the 30 s poll and the visibility read are unchanged); nothing needs the kill flags in the first seconds, and the route is cached 60 s or more. The mechanism inside WebKit is not known; the fix removes the trigger, not the browser bug. (b) **Look goldens** (`chromium-webgl2` shard 4, `desktop noon-street` mean 30/255): the QA agency panel is a fixed overlay at the bottom left and `locator('#dv-engine-canvas').screenshot()` captures what covers the canvas, so the 256 x 144 golden shows the panel. The panel has the class `dv-npcagency` and the two style lists of `world-look.spec.ts` that hide the debug panels name it; it also assigns its text only when the text changed. (c) **`npc-barks` knock-down on `chromium-webgpu`**: the knock-down lasts 120 sim ticks, which is more than 2.6 s of wall time on the software runner, and a `qa.bump` while the pedestrian is down only moved its recovery later, so the repeated bump of the first repair never produced the up-down edge the bark watcher needs. `qa.bump` is now ignored while the filler is down, `debug().fillerSeen` is the anim state the watcher last saw, and the spec bumps only when it is 0, with no fixed wait. (d) the integrator's edits are kept: `npc-talk-request` uses an unrouted path, the world-hash page boots with `npc: false` (a baked world would add the NPC module's entities to the traffic-synth hashes the Node goldens fix), the barks spec allows `/api/v1/t`. The unused `PerformanceObserver` of the QA panel (`apiCalls()`) is removed. | The refused merge failed 3 jobs for these causes, none an acceptance criterion; the A/B figures are in this row so a later crash of the same signature can be compared. | Revert the delay (`FEED_DELAY_MS` = 0) and the QA changes; the fixes are independent. |
| BD-FIX-TMP-01 | CI (fix, 2026-10-25) | Test runs keep their temp folders in one per-run root, removed at teardown, and the containment never signals a process it did not start itself. `tools/ci/tmp-root.ts` makes `<os.tmpdir()>/dv-<label>-<pid>-XXXXXX`, writes the marker `.dv-tmp-root` into it (JSON `ns`, `pid`, `label`; `ns` is `linux:<boot_id>:<readlink /proc/self/ns/pid>`, `<platform>:<hostname>` elsewhere), points `TMPDIR`, `TEMP` and `TMP` at it and publishes it as `DV_TEST_TMP_ROOT`; the first runner to start owns it and removes it when it ends, pass or fail (plus a process-exit backstop), and a nested runner that finds a valid `DV_TEST_TMP_ROOT` reuses it and never removes it. Owners: `tools/ci/run.ts` (every `check:unit`, `check:e2e` and package `test` run), `check:workers` (`tools/ci/check-workers.ts`, which runs `services/edge/node_modules/.bin/vitest` directly, bin folder first on PATH, so no pnpm sits between it and Vitest), the root Vitest globalSetup `tools/ci/vitest-tmp-root.ts` (also in the `services/edge` and `services/pipeline` configs) and `playwright.config.ts`, which makes the root at config load because Playwright starts the web server before globalSetup; its globalSetup (`tests/support/tmp-root.setup.ts`) fails the run if the root is not in effect. `check:perf` is covered because `playwright.perf.config.ts` imports `playwright.config.ts`. run.ts and `check:workers` start their tool with an asynchronous spawn (`spawnInTmpRoot`): they forward SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP to that one child only, wait for it to exit, remove the root and exit with the child's code (128 + signal when a signal ended it); a run.ts on a reused root leaves it to the enclosing run. Whatever the child started is the child's tool's business: the forwarded signal reaches the child, and the tool stops its own workers; nothing scans `/proc` and nothing sends SIGTERM or SIGKILL to processes found there. A `DV_TEST_TMP_ROOT` is valid only when absolute, named like a per-run root, an existing real folder (not a symlink), carrying a marker of this PID namespace with the pid of its name, and that creator alive (`checkTmpRoot`); anything else (`/`, `/tmp`, a typo, a relative path, an unmarked folder, a foreign-namespace root, a root leaked by an earlier run and left exported) is ignored with a warning and a fresh root is made, next to a leaked root when TMPDIR points into it. Stale roots: a SIGKILLed run (or one ended by a hard timeout or the OOM killer) leaves its root, and the next run removes it: every creator first sweeps the folders directly in its temp folder that carry the per-run name, are real folders owned by this user and hold a marker with the same pid whose creator cannot be alive, either this PID namespace with the pid dead (`process.kill(pid, 0)` throws ESRCH) or another boot (both identities carry a Linux boot id, `linux:<uuid>:`, and they differ). A root of another PID namespace of this boot (a container or sandbox sharing /tmp), a live pid's root, an unmarked folder, a file, a symlink and a name that fails the pattern are skipped silently; nothing is swept when the namespace cannot be read; a failed removal warns once per process. `removeTmpRoot` removes only a real per-run-named folder whose real path is directly inside the expected parent. The specs that made the most folders (`tools/map/test` helpers, publish, bake.repro, fetch.mirrors, synth, places, fixture, attribution, landmarks.rest; `tools/ci/test` spec-files, traceability, milestone-format, check-api, check-c2pa, assets-gates, workflows, deploy) take theirs from `useTempDirs()` (`tools/ci/test-tmp.ts`, removed after the file's last test); `tests/e2e/ui/saves.spec.ts` removes its export folder. Tests (`tools/ci/test/tmp-root.spec.ts`, driving nested runs of `tmp-root.probe.spec.ts`): a spec's mkdtemp and a spawned process land under the root; the root is gone after a failing plain Vitest run and after a passing run.ts run; SIGINT to the process group of `tsx tools/ci/run.ts vitest run` (three rounds) and to run.ts alone remove the root, and a bystander process run.ts did not start, with its cwd and TMPDIR inside the root, keeps running; a SIGKILL of run.ts leaves the root and the next run's sweep removes it; a run.ts on an inherited root leaves the enclosing run's processes and the root alone; the sweep removes dead-pid and other-boot roots (also when the pid is alive now) and keeps live, foreign-namespace (also a live run in `unshare --pid`), unmarked, wrong-pid, hostname-fallback and badly named entries; invalid `DV_TEST_TMP_ROOT` values and the leaked-and-exported case. `tests/e2e/tmp-root.spec.ts` (`@tmp-root`) checks a Playwright worker. Known limit: a straggler the child's tool failed to stop can recreate folders in a removed root, which then has no marker and is not swept. | One afternoon of Builder runs left about 15,000 `/tmp/dv-*` folders (16 GB) and filled the disk twice; most specs never removed their mkdtemp folders. Vitest's forks and threads pools and Playwright's workers copy `process.env` when they start and `os.tmpdir()` reads `TMPDIR` on each call, so one variable set before the workers start contains every spec and subprocess. Ctrl-C leaked roots while run.ts used `spawnSync`: tsx relays SIGINT and SIGKILLs its child unless the child acknowledges within about 30 ms, which a blocked event loop cannot, hence the asynchronous spawn. An earlier draft also stopped the processes it found working in the root through `/proc` and ran a detached guard; a review found that this killed unrelated processes (a developer shell whose cwd was inside a root), and the Architect ruled (2026-10-25) that the containment never signals a process it did not start; a SIGKILLed run's root is left to the next run's sweep, which is accepted. A sweep keyed on pid liveness alone is unsafe wherever a temp folder is shared across PID namespaces, hence the marker; every namespace of one boot shares the boot id, so a marker from another boot is stale for certain. | Drop the globalSetup, the config-load call and the run.ts wrapper; specs then write to the system temp folder again. Dropping the marker checks needs another liveness test for the sweep. |
| BD-FIX-SPAWN-04 | M05c1, M05c2 (fix S2, 2026-10-25; DL-734) | Review of BD-FIX-SPAWN-01 and the Jobs Start action of the 03-story.md section 5 failure row. (a) The retry budget is checked when a retry is scheduled and again when it would be sent (`StoryController.stepPlacing`), so no `TeleportApi.request` goes out once 10,800 ticks have passed since the first; the worst case (last request just inside the budget, then its 1,800-tick answer time) is 12,600 ticks (3.5 min), and the code comment, the constants (now exported, with `placeRetryGap`: 60, 120, 240, 480, then 600 ticks) and BD-FIX-SPAWN-01 say the same. (b) The `mission:<id>:loading` info toast is taken off the screen when the mission starts or when `mission.startUnreachable` is shown. C06 `UiBridge` has no dismiss call and contracts change only through the Architect, so the story sends the hint's id again with an empty text and `ShellCore.onEngineToast` reads a sim toast with `text: ''` as `ToastStore.dismiss(id)` (an empty LocKey never shows). Proposal for the Architect: a `UiBridge.dismissToast(id)` (additive C06 minor) or this convention written into 10-ui.md section 3.3. **Superseded by DL-735 d and BD-FIX-SPAWN-09:** no dismiss convention; the hint is a short sim toast (5 s, at most 6 s) re-posted every 20 s while the wait lasts, and nothing takes it off the screen. (c) `content/ui/i18n/en.json` `mission.startUnreachable` formatting restored. (d) A far start that fails leaves the mission `available` and records it in `StoryController.startFailed` (cleared on attach); `MissionStore.rows` marks the row `retry: true`; the phone's Jobs row then shows the badge `phone.jobs.start` ("Start") and the detail `phone.jobs.retry` (our own words), and the press sends the existing `mission.start`. For a mission whose far start failed, while the player is more than 300 m from the new game's start (`CatalogPayload.autoStart`), `mission.start` runs the move again with a fresh budget (`beginPlacing`); a second press while it moves toasts `jobs.busy`. Other missions keep the M05c1 Jobs start where the player stands (superseded by BD-FIX-SPAWN-07: every Jobs Start more than 300 m from the mission's authored start is now a move): with the triggers on, the auto trigger makes the first move before the phone can open; marker missions have no move yet (the failure row names "auto or Jobs trigger", and moving every far Jobs start would refuse them on gray-box, where the `@story` CI half starts missions from Jobs with the triggers off; flagged for the Architect). Unit tests: `packages/modes/story/test/new-game-start.test.ts` (gaps double to 600 and stay, no request at or after the budget, end within budget plus one answer time, hint dismissed on start and on failure, Start from Jobs after a hard refusal, after the budget ran out, and on gray-box), `packages/ui/test/talk-choices.test.tsx` (the Start row), `packages/ui/test/modes-tiles.spec.ts` (the empty-text dismiss). | The review asked for these, and DL-734 gives Jobs a Start action that tries the move again. | Drop `startFailed` and `JobRow.retry`, the `mission.start` branch and the empty-text toast branch; put the budget check back to scheduling time only. |
| BD-FIX-SPAWN-05 | M03a1, M02c1 (fix S2, 2026-10-25; DL-734) | The below-the-world net for a vehicle (02-world-maps.md 4.1 d). `vehicleFallRecoverySystem` (`packages/engine/src/sim/control/fall.ts`, stage Control, order −8, registered by `registerVehicleSystems`) watches the local player's vehicle with the player at its wheel: once it has been in the air for 120 ticks (`VehicleRuntime.airTicks`, no wheel contact) while under `play.minY` or more than 3 m under the lowest resident surface at its (x, z), it pushes `control.recover` (`s` `below-bounds` or `below-ground`, `a` the driver) once per fall and asks `TeleportApi.request('respawn', pose)` (since BD-FIX-SPAWN-09 the ownership machine's `requestPlayerMove` instead, DL-735 a). The pose is the lane centre (lane 0) of the nearest drivable edge within 250 m, else the water wall's road-adjacent `ShoreNode` 3 m inland facing inland (`vehicleRespawnPose`). A refused or lost request is asked again every 60 ticks while the car is still out of the world (pacing, candidates and give-up amended by BD-FIX-SPAWN-07). The fade is the teleport's own (`world.teleport` out, then in; DL-735 b); the water wall keeps its longer stall fade. To let the teleport move a driver (02-world-maps.md 5 step 4: a driver keeps the vehicle only when the target lies on a drivable edge), the world module's `TeleportHost.begin` calls the new engine `requestDriverTeleport` when the player drives and the placed target is within 8 m of a drivable edge; it calls `EngineServices.vehicleMove` (set by `registerVehicleSystems`), which probes the static colliders under the target (refused `engine.control.groundNotLoaded` without one) and places the car with `placeVehicle`, the water wall's placement factored out of `respawnAtShore` (body pose, zero velocity, suspension reset), the driver following the seat in the same tick. The water wall keeps its own direct respawn of M03a1 (not through the teleport). With no baked world (`services.world.api` null) the system does nothing; it adds no hashed state (its pacing map is not sim state and `vehicleMove` is stateless), so the gray-box hashes and the C02 lint are unchanged. Side effect: any teleport kind requested while the player drives to a target on a drivable edge now moves the car with its driver instead of answering `busy`, as section 5 step 4 says. Limits: vehicles driven by NPCs are not covered (the teleport moves the local player only); the on-foot net (BD-FIX-SPAWN-02) still asks the ownership machine directly instead of `TeleportApi.request`, because the teleport answers `not-loaded` where nothing is resident and would drop the y 150 m fallback that 4.1 (d) keeps (flagged for the Architect). Unit tests: `packages/engine/test/control/vehicle-fall.spec.ts`. | DL-734 gives this follow-up the vehicle half of the net. | Remove the system and `vehicleMove` from `registerVehicleSystems` and the driver branch of `TeleportHost.begin`. |

Outside `Running` the engine net handles the race car. (f) A respawn is not heat-gated (02-world-maps.md 5 gates only fast travel and sandbox): escaping a pursuit through the net is now a test, not a decision. Open questions then (ruled by DL-735, built in BD-FIX-SPAWN-09): the on-foot net asks the ownership machine directly, not `TeleportApi.request`; the vehicle respawn uses the teleport's 12-tick fade, not the water wall's 30-tick one; NPC-driven cars and a player riding as a passenger are not covered; the empty-text toast as a dismiss (BD-FIX-SPAWN-04 b). C02: tick counters, comparisons and Map insertion order only. No golden changed: no ambient car goes under `play.minY` in a golden scenario, and with `dist/world` built the synth goldens (`traffic.determinism`, `peds.determinism`, `transit.synth`, `pursuit/*`) pass unchanged, so no regenerate command applies. A checkpoint that matches still continues from its stage. (b) **A car stalled in the water is the water rule's** (02-world-maps.md 4.1 a; DL-735 b keeps the longer fade with the stall respawn): `vehicleFallRecoverySystem` skips a vehicle whose `VehicleRuntime.wet.stalled` is set and drops its own rescue fade bookkeeping for it without an `in`, so the water rule's shore respawn and its `in` run as they would without the net (before, the net's move reset `rt.wet` and `respawnFade`, so the water rule never sent its `in`; the net's own `in` brought the screen back). Bookkeeping only: nothing new is hashed or saved. (c) **Left open:** a valid checkpoint still writes the saved player pose directly (`restoreCheckpoint`, `Actors.place`) before the tiles there are resident; the on-foot net recovers a fall after 2 s. Routing a resume through the teleport (with a cast vehicle the player was in) is a placement rule the spec does not give for resumes, so it is left for the Architect. | BD-FIX-SPAWN-11 | M05c1, M05c2, M02c1, M03b1 (fix S5, 2026-10-25; DL-736) | The checkpoint resume of DL-736 (03-story.md section 5, 'Resume from a saved checkpoint'). (a) **Pre-stream, then write as saved:** `StoryController.resumeSaved` (and a Jobs Start of a mission whose matching checkpoint is still saved) no longer calls `begin(…, {resume: true})` at once: `beginResume` asks the world for the pre-stream of 02-world-maps.md 5 step 2 alone at the saved player pose, through a new world-package port that is not a C08 member, `WorldServices.prestream(x, z)` (`TeleportService.prestream`: the `p0` chunk and its one-chunk ring that the bake has, as `needNow` and pinned, with no validation, placement, fade or move, so no mode rule, heat or cooldown applies; the ticket's `ready()` is true once the chunk under the point and every ring chunk are resident; `release()` unpins). While a ticket is held the streaming module feeds the static colliders around its point after the player's own, as it does for a teleport's destination (`TeleportService.prestreamFocus`, `WorldSim.prestreamDest`): without that the colliders at a pose kilometres from the player never came, since the feed follows the player's focus. `stepResume` (each trigger pass, 15 ticks) restores the checkpoint once the ticket is ready and `ctx.physics.groundY` has a static collider under the saved pose, within the DL-734 budget (`PLACE_BUDGET_TICKS`, 10,800 ticks) and with the `mission.startLoading` hint on the far start's ticks (5 s, then every 20 s; `hintWait` now serves both waits). A world without the port (the gray-box stand-in, a headless host) restores at once, as before: its ground is always there. The story mode reads the port structurally (`ctx.world` is the engine's proxy of `WorldServices`); no C01–C17 shape changed. (b) **Poses as saved, seated player included, no placement search:** `begin` passes `load: true` to `MissionRuntime.start` (new `StartOptions.load`; a retry never sets it). With it the cast is not spawned first at its authored spawns (before, `spawnStartCast` spawned it there and the deferred spawn left `restoreCheckpoint` no entity to move, so a resumed cast stood at its authored spawn), but by `restoreCheckpoint` at its saved poses; the cast's saved health, and a player saved in a cast vehicle (`cp.player.vehicle`), follow on the first tick those entities exist (`MissionRuntime.restorePending`): the player is put at the saved pose and seated at the wheel through a new engine extension `ctx.vehicleSeat` (`seatDriver`, like `ctx.vehicleAi` not part of C06); with no seat port, or no vehicle within `SEAT_WAIT_TICKS` (30), the player is set down on foot at the saved pose. A garage vehicle in `cp.player.vehicle` is not a cast entry and is set down on foot. (c) **Budget spent:** the checkpoint is kept (`activeMission` untouched), the row goes back to `available` (a `passed` row keeps its state and `bestSec`), `mission.startUnreachable` (`reason: not-loaded`) and telemetry `mission.fail {reason: 'unresolved'}` as for a far start, and the Jobs row gets its Start (`retry`); the trigger does not start the mission fresh meanwhile (it counts as tried by its trigger), the next load (`attach` resets the session) or a Jobs Start waits again with a fresh budget and resumes the checkpoint, not stage 0. A mode exit or a new attach releases a held ticket. (d) **i18n:** `mission.startLoading` names no first job any more ("The streets where this job picks up are still loading. Hold on, it gets going once the ground is there."), since every far start and every resume shows it. (e) **A fallen promoted car leaves the traffic in full:** `AmbientTraffic.sweepFallen` now also calls `TrafficSim.despawnPromoted(key)` (new; `PromotionBook.remove`) for a promoted car under `play.minY`, so its agent's promotion record goes with the car (the cap and the slot are free again, no wreck follows, `stats.despawns` counts it); before only the `vehicles` map entry and the entity went, and the record held a promotion slot until it ran out 8 s later. A wreck (its promotion already over) is despawned as before. C02: sim ticks, comparisons and insertion order only; no golden changed (the synth goldens pass with `dist/world` built: 207 files, 0 skipped). Tests: `packages/modes/story/test/resume-ground.test.ts` (tiles not resident: waits, then the exact saved pose of the player, the car and the ally, and their health; resume seated keeps the seat, seated once; no seat port sets the player down on foot; budget exhaustion keeps the checkpoint with one `mission.startUnreachable`, the hint ticks and a Jobs Start that resumes stage 1; the next attach tries again; no pre-stream restores at once; an exit releases the ticket), `packages/world/test/teleport.rules.spec.ts` (the ring as `needNow`, ready only when all are resident, pins and `prestreamFocus` until release, no teleport job; a point outside the bake is never ready), `packages/world/test/traffic.fallen.spec.ts` (the promotion record goes and the despawn is counted); e2e `tests/e2e/story-resume-ground.spec.ts`, tag `@story-resume-ground` (synth half: `GRAYBOX_GREP`'s `@story( \|$)` does not match it): m.a1.01 at the Yard, jump to its checkpoint stage, reload, and the player stands at the saved pose (0.000 m off on both browsers) and is never under the ground before the restore or over 300 ticks after it; with the wait switched off the same spec fails (the player ended 5.5 km from the saved pose). | DL-736 rules BD-FIX-SPAWN-10 (c), which had left the direct write of the saved pose open, and also asks for the hint text and the full traffic despawn. | Let `resumeSaved` call `begin(…, {resume: true})` at once again, drop `WorldServices.prestream`, `prestreamFocus` and `prestreamDest`, `StartOptions.load`, `restorePending` and `ctx.vehicleSeat`, and `TrafficSim.despawnPromoted`; restore the old hint text. |
| BD-FIX-SPAWN-12 | M05c1, M05c2, M03a1 (fix S6, 2026-10-25; DL-736 review minors) | The four minors of the DL-736 review, closed. (a) **The pre-stream ticket is held until the restore is written:** `StoryController.stepResume` no longer releases the ticket before `begin`; when the restored runtime still has `restorePending` (a seated player waits for the deferred cast-vehicle spawn, or a cast health waits for its entity) the ticket moves to `heldTicket` with `until = tick + SEAT_WAIT_TICKS` (30), and `advance` releases it after the runtime's tick once `restorePending` clears, once `until` is reached (the tick the runtime gives the seat up), or once the mission stops; a mode exit or a new attach releases it too. (b) **The current car:** `ctx.vehicleSeat.seat` is now `reseatDriver` (new, `enter-exit.ts`): a rider aboard another vehicle is taken out of it first (`unseatDriver`, which frees that car's `rt.driver` and logs the `unseat` transition), a rider walking up to another vehicle gives that booking up, then `seatDriver`; the car the rider already drives changes nothing. A taken seat answers false with the rider out of every car. (c) **A failed seat:** `applyRestorePending` reads the seat's answer; on false the player is set down on foot beside the car through the new port member `ctx.vehicleSeat.setDown` (`setDownBeside`: out of any vehicle, then the capsule at the normal exit's `findExitPoint` spot, driver side first at `trackM / 2 + 0.9` m from the centre line, with a clear ray and ground, else that driver-side point anyway; facing the car's way, colliding, shown), and the runtime notes `resume.seatFailed {vehicle, how}`. A port without `setDown` gets the driver-side point `SET_DOWN_SIDE_M` (2.2 m, the exit's first ring) to the car's left. `VehicleSeatPort` is an engine port, not a C06 member: no contract shape changed. (d) **e2e waits:** stage 0 writes a checkpoint too, so the confirmed `checkpoint` save the resume specs waited for could be that one (webkit failed `stage: 5` once in `story-resume.spec.ts` and once in the new seated case); they now also poll the saved checkpoint until it holds stage 5 before the same assertions. C02: sim ticks and detmath (`dsin`, `dcos`) only.
