| BD-FIX-SEC-01 | M00, M00a, M00d, M05d, M06b1 (security fix K1, 2026-10-25) | **Test runners never pass a live secret to a test.** **Incident (2026-10-24 15:54Z to 2026-10-25 11:13Z, no values recorded):** Builder sessions ran `pnpm check:workers` (and possibly other suites) in cloud shells that export the owner's production `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY`, `SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD`, `SUPABASE_PROJECT_ID_PRODUCTION`, `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `PLAY_TOKEN_HMAC`, `QUOTA_NET_SALT_SECRET`, `FAL_KEY` and, in new sessions, `R2_*`. Wrangler loads the names of `secrets.required` from the process environment by itself (the run prints "Using secrets defined in process.env") and the staging config of `services/edge/wrangler.jsonc` names the production `SUPABASE_URL` as a var, so non-harness Workers specs built a real Supabase client against production: 1,184 PostgREST requests (GET `/rest/v1/cutscene_catalog`, POST `/rest/v1/profiles`, GET `/rest/v1/roster_flag`), all 404 on the empty production schema, so nothing was written. A bare `unset` in one shell did not carry to the next; M06b1's `E2E_UNSET_SECRETS` covered the e2e web server only. **Fix:** (a) one module, `tools/ci/secret-env.ts`, is the single source of truth: exact names `FAL_KEY`, `QUOTA_NET_SALT_SECRET`, `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, `CLOUDFLARE_API_KEY`, `DV_NAMES_REAL_JSON`, and the prefixes `SUPABASE_` (every one: no spec reads a `SUPABASE_*` variable from the environment; the Worker's public URL and publishable key are wrangler vars), `PLAY_TOKEN_HMAC`, `ANTHROPIC_`, `OPENAI_`, `INTERNAL_HMAC`, `ID_SIGNING_JWK_`, `R2_`; it covers CLAUDE.md "Secrets", `tools/ci/deploy.ts` `WORKER_SECRETS` and both `secrets.required` lists (a unit test keeps that true). `scrubbedEnv(env)` returns a copy without them, `scrubProcessEnv()` removes them in place; either prints one stderr line naming (never valuing) what it removed. `WORKER_LOCAL_SECRETS` there replaces the old list, and `playwright.config.ts` re-exports it as `E2E_UNSET_SECRETS`. (b) Applied in `tools/ci/run.ts` for `vitest` and `playwright` only (`check:unit`, `check:e2e`, `check:e2e:xr`, every package `test`/`test:*` script), `tools/ci/check-workers.ts`, `tools/ci/perf-check.ts` (`check:perf`), `tools/ci/check-db.ts` (the local stack, psql and the DataPort contract run; `DV_DB_*` are not on the list) and `services/pipeline/scripts/check-pipeline.ts`; second layer for a runner started directly: the root `vitest.config.ts` at config load (before any worker forks) plus the setup file `tools/ci/vitest-secret-env.ts` in every worker before any spec is imported, `services/edge/vitest.config.ts`, `services/edge/vitest.db.config.ts`, `services/pipeline/vitest.config.ts`, and `playwright.config.ts` at config load (Playwright merges `process.env` into the web server's env, so the scrub happens in place; with the opt-in the Worker's own names are still passed on empty as BD-FIX-CI8-H02 says). `tests/e2e/npc-builder.spec.ts` uses the module with `force` (its Worker never gets a shell secret). The per-run temp root code (BD-FIX-TMP-01) is unchanged: the scrub runs before `spawnInTmpRoot`. (c) **Opt-in:** `DV_ALLOW_LIVE_SECRETS=1` (exactly `1`) passes every name through and prints a loud WARNING line once per process tree. No workflow sets it (a unit test checks). (d) **CI is unaffected:** the CI jobs that pass a secret on purpose run tools, not test runners, and are untouched: `names:lint` (job `ip`), `npc:lint` and `npc:eval` (job `content`) get `DV_NAMES_REAL_JSON` (a unit test checks that no step passing it runs a test runner); `db:push`, `deploy:prod` and `test:staging` (HTTP to the deployed staging Worker, `tests/staging/vitest.config.ts`, which has no scrub) keep their secrets. The `unit`, `db`, `e2e` and `perf` jobs pass no secret, so they see exactly what they saw before. (e) **Tests:** `tools/ci/test/secret-env.test.ts` spawns `tsx tools/ci/run.ts vitest run tools/ci/test/secret-env-probe` and a direct `vitest run` with `test-not-a-secret` for 26 denied names (the probe spec, skipped in a normal run, asserts it sees none), the same run with `DV_ALLOW_LIVE_SECRETS=1` (all 26 pass), and `tsx tools/ci/check-workers.ts secret-env-probe` (`services/edge/test/secret-env-probe.test.ts`: no Worker binding carries the fake value; with the opt-in the same probe fails, which proves it is not vacuous). | Production secrets reached test processes from the shell, and a per-shell `unset` does not persist; only the runners themselves can guarantee that a test never sees one. | Undo: drop the `scrubProcessEnv`/`scrubbedEnv` calls and the `setupFiles` entry, restore the literal `E2E_UNSET_SECRETS` list in `playwright.config.ts` and the `SECRET_NAMES` list of `tests/e2e/npc-builder.spec.ts`, delete `tools/ci/secret-env.ts`, `tools/ci/vitest-secret-env.ts`, `tools/ci/test/secret-env*.test.ts` and `services/edge/test/secret-env-probe.test.ts`. A single run that needs live values sets `DV_ALLOW_LIVE_SECRETS=1` instead. Rotating the exposed production values is an owner action outside this row. |
| BD-FIX-SEC-02 | M00, M00a, M00d, M05b2, M06b1 (security fix K1-R1, 2026-10-25; review of BD-FIX-SEC-01; final design K2-R1, 2026-10-26, Architect directive on 02ef164d) | **No Worker started by a test runner or by `pnpm dev` gets a live secret, whatever the env files beside its config hold; nothing is decided by parsing an env file.** (a) **Two ways in:** wrangler and @cloudflare/vitest-plugin load `secrets.required` from `services/edge/.dev.vars` (or `.dev.vars.<env>`), else `.env`, `.env.local`, `.env.<env>`, `.env.<env>.local`, beside the process environment the BD-FIX-SEC-01 scrub cleans; an env file may also override a config var (probed: `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` reach the Worker from `.dev.vars`; names that are neither required nor vars do not). (b) **No env file read:** the e2e web server, the second Worker of `npc-builder.spec.ts` and `check:pipeline`'s `getPlatformProxy` (and `gate.prompt-lint.test.ts`) pass the null device as their only env file (`--env-file` / `envFiles`, `NO_ENV_FILE`), as on the CI runner. (c) **Always blank:** `check:workers` (`services/edge/vitest.config.ts`, `miniflare.bindings`; the plugin has no env-file option) and `pnpm dev` / `pnpm dev:edge` (`tools/ci/dev.ts`, `--var NAME:`) bind every known Worker secret name to the empty string on every run (user Miniflare bindings win the merge; every route treats an empty secret as absent). The names come from `tools/ci/worker-secrets.ts`, computed on each run as the union of `WORKER_SECRETS` of `tools/ci/deploy.ts`, every `secrets.required` list of `services/edge/wrangler.jsonc` (top level, staging, any other environment) and the secret-like fields of the `Env` interface in `services/edge/src/env.ts`, less the names the config sets as vars (public by definition); no copy is kept by hand, and a new secret in any source is blanked from then on. Only the exact opt-in `DV_ALLOW_LIVE_SECRETS=1` skips the blanking, with the warning. (d) **Warning only:** the env files are still scanned, only to print one loud stderr line per file naming the deny-listed names it holds (never a value); the scan normalises `\r\n` and a bare `\r` as wrangler's dotenv does (K2-R1: the earlier `split(/\r?\n/)` read a bare-CR file as one line, found no name and so blanked nothing). (e) **Deny-list:** `OPS_TOKEN`, `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY` (env.ts Worker secrets), `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `AWS_SESSION_TOKEN` (S3 SDK defaults), and `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`; CI sets `GH_TOKEN` only in the env of the steps that run a deploy tool or `gh` (nightly `deploy:preview --sweep-closed`; preview.yml's PR comment step, moved there from the job level), none of which is a test runner, and `secret-env.test.ts` checks every workflow for it. (f) **Git and reuse:** `.env`, `.env.*` and `.dev.vars.*` are ignored (examples excepted); the e2e default port is 8790 (off the 8788 of `pnpm dev`), Playwright never reuses a server, `check:headers`/`check:coi --serve` refuse a busy port, and `npc-builder.spec.ts` refuses to start when `DV_E2E_PORT+1` is already listening, fails when its Worker exits before it is healthy, and kills the Worker's process group in `afterAll` (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL). (g) **Warning everywhere:** the opt-in warning prints in every process; the `DV_LIVE_SECRETS_WARNED` marker is gone. (h) **Load:** the heavy nested-runner probes (Vitest through `run.ts`, Vitest directly, `check:workers` with workerd, the temporary env files) live in `services/edge/test/node/secret-env-runners.test.ts`, which `check:workers` runs in Node after the workerd suites (`services/edge/vitest.node.config.ts`; `test/node` is excluded from the workerd pool, and the root config never collects `services/*/test/**`), so the `ci.yml` `unit` job's `pnpm check:workers` step runs them and `pnpm check:unit` keeps only the fast pure cases in `tools/ci/test/secret-env.test.ts`; nested runs start under `nice -n 10`; `tools/ci/tycoon-sim` is untouched (pinned by `verifiers.lock.json`). (i) **Temporary env files:** every file the probe writes into `services/edge` starts with a sentinel line; a file carrying it (a killed run's) is removed or overwritten, any other is never touched; with `CI` set a non-sentinel env file there fails the case, locally it skips. Proof: `check:workers -- secret-env-probe` passes with a temporary `.dev.vars` (`\n` and bare-`\r` endings) and `.env` holding fake values for every Worker secret and fails under the opt-in; `workerSecretBlanks` returns every name with no env file, with one, and with a file no scan understands; `unstable_getVarsForDev` loads the fake values from each env file by default and none with `NO_ENV_FILE`. | The first K1-R1 design blanked only the names it could parse out of an env file, so a file it read differently from wrangler (bare `\r` line endings) let every value through; the set of secrets the Worker can hold is known from the code and config, so blanking all of it needs no parsing. The nested probes each start a Vitest (often with workerd) beside the rest of `check:unit` on the 2-vCPU runner, where the load-sensitive specs (hash.fnv, lint.bans, the tycoon self-tests) share the CPU; the check:workers step already starts workerd. | Undo: drop the `miniflare.bindings` entry of `services/edge/vitest.config.ts` and the `--var NAME:` arguments of `tools/ci/dev.ts`, delete `tools/ci/worker-secrets.ts`, `services/edge/vitest.node.config.ts`, `services/edge/test/node/` and the second part of `tools/ci/check-workers.ts`, and remove `--env-file`/`envFiles` `NO_ENV_FILE` from `playwright.config.ts`, `tests/e2e/npc-builder.spec.ts`, `check-pipeline.ts` and `gate.prompt-lint.test.ts`; put `GH_TOKEN` back at the preview job level. A single local run that needs live values sets `DV_ALLOW_LIVE_SECRETS=1` instead. |
| BD-FIX-GLYPH-01 | M04b2, M04e5 (fix, 2026-10-26) | **HUD icons are the kit's inline SVGs, never font glyphs.** Owner screenshot of staging (production build of main 3a5eb603, macOS): the heat row read "▯▯▯▯▯" and the *Clear* chip had a box. **Root cause:** not a font fault on the wire: the app ships no font file (staging CSS has no `@font-face`; `--dv-font-*` are system stacks), so nothing is blocked by COEP, CORP or CSP. Every HUD icon was a symbol character (`'▯'`/`'▮'` U+25AF/U+25AE for the heat bars and the `heat.clear`/`heat.wanted` icons, `◎ ↻ ✦ ◔ ⛔ ⚒ ℹ ♥ ✓ ✖ ▲ ◆ ● …`) drawn from whatever system font the browser falls back to; SF Pro, Roboto and the Quest Browser stack lack several of them and paint .notdef, and U+25AF itself is a plain white rectangle, indistinguishable from .notdef where it does exist. 10-ui.md "Fonts and icons" already says icons are inline SVG. **Fix:** `packages/ui/src/kit/icons.tsx` holds `ICONS` (18 authored paths on a 16 x 16 grid, `currentColor`, fill even-odd or 2-unit round stroke), the `Icon` component (`aria-hidden`, `focusable="false"`, `data-icon`) and `HeatBar` (five bars rising like a signal meter; an unlit bar is a dim solid bar on a dark plate, never an outlined box, per 10-ui-hud-xr.md "five heat bars, no star glyphs"). `StatusDef.icon` is now an `IconId` (`heat.clear` shield, `heat.wanted` siren, `heat.searching` magnifier, `heat.cooldown` circular arrow, `heat.scripted` spark, and the rest); `StatusChip`, `MeterBar`, `HeatStars`, `ClockWeather`, the blocked prompt, the sandbox chip and the tip bar render it. Accessible names are unchanged (the heat group's `aria-label`, the chip text such as "Clear" stays an i18n key); `.dv-svg-icon` in `kit.css`. The wallet keeps its ASCII "$". **Tests:** `packages/ui/test/hud-icons.spec.ts` (every status names a kit icon; the heat row at every level and phase renders five SVG bars and the Clear chip an SVG shield with only printable ASCII or Latin-1 text; no HUD source holds a non-Latin-1 character outside comments; 5 of 6 cases fail on the old code); `tests/e2e/ui/hud.spec.ts` `@ui-hud` "the heat row and the Clear chip draw SVG icons" (each bar and the chip icon slot hold an SVG with a real box and no text; no HUD text node leaves ASCII/Latin-1; fails on the old code with `Received: "▯"`). **M04e5:** its glyph clause (`spec/07-milestones/M04e5-*.md` l.6, l.43 and AC l.90: `▮` lit, `▯` unlit, `STATUS['heat.clear'].icon` is `▯`) named the very characters that fail; its intent (five gauge bars, no star glyphs) holds, and `tools/ci/test/ip-trainer-phrases.test.ts` "heat bars replace the star glyphs" now checks the SVG icons and bans `▮ ▯ ★ ☆` in the HUD sources. Proposal to the Architect: amend those M04e5 lines to "five SVG gauge bars (BD-FIX-GLYPH-01)"; the milestone file is not edited here. Out of scope, same class, left as they are: phone app icons (`phone/registry.ts`), toast icons (`★`), the report flag (`⚑`), the creator's `∅` and the rotate hint's `↻` outside the HUD. | The screenshot's boxes are exactly what the code asked for on some browsers and .notdef on others; only shapes the app draws itself render the same on macOS and iOS Safari, Chrome and Quest Browser. No font subset is shipped yet, so "a character in the shipped font" was not an option. | Undo: restore the character icons in `hud/model.ts`, `hud/widgets.tsx` and `hud/tipbar.tsx`, delete `kit/icons.tsx`, `test/hud-icons.spec.ts`, the `.dv-svg-icon` rule and the e2e case; or keep the SVGs and ship the OFL font subset of 10-ui.md instead. |
| BD-FIX-CUTFLAKE-01 | M05b2 (fix, 2026-10-26, rewritten the same day on the proven cause; CI flake of `cutscene-player.spec.ts` "a press held from before the clip …" on webkit-webgl2) | **The e2e specs that play the fixture clip serve its renditions with `Range` honoured, as the production media route does; the local Worker's static-asset server ignored it, and WebKit failed the clip on that.** New `tests/e2e/media-ranges.ts`: `serveMediaRanges(page)` routes `*.mp4`/`*.webm` and answers a request that carries `Range` with `206`, `Content-Range` and those bytes (`416` when unsatisfiable; the parser follows `parseRange` of `services/edge/src/routes/media.ts`), from the upstream answer; a request without `Range` and an upstream `206` pass through; a request the media element drops while it is answered is aborted, not failed. `cutscene-player.spec.ts` and `cutscene-spike.spec.ts` register it in `beforeEach`, before each test's own routes (which take precedence and reach it through `route.fallback()`). No product code, wait, timeout, retry or assertion of an existing test changed. Kept from the first fix on this branch: `CutscenePlayerImpl.playClip` moves a clip whose pre-roll ends in `codec` or `media_error` to the next pooled element before the fallback (`nextHost`, `PlayerDeps.onNextHost`, logged `{k: 'next-host'}`), which section D7's trigger table requires ("next host, then fallback"), with its unit tests in `player-triggers.test.ts` and the e2e "a clip whose first element cannot decode it plays on the next pooled element"; it does not cure this failure, because the next element reads the same URL from the same server and fails the same way. New regression e2e "a clip that is still arriving plays: a ranged read of its tail gets those bytes, not the whole file" (both browsers): route 27 points at a second origin started by the test that serves the built fixture as the local Worker does (`200` and the whole file for every request, `Range` ignored) in 64 KB pieces 30 ms apart; it asserts `video`, no `fallback`, no `next-host`, a skip to `stage`, and that the browser did read in ranges. | **Proven cause** (CI evidence: PR #9 run 40368522267, job `e2e-shard (webkit-webgl2, 1)`, commit d2fb0e67, both tries of `:208`; their `cutscene-log` attachments and `1-trace.network`). The test never stalled on the Title: the mission gate released after the gesture (`after.gesture` 3439 and 3268 ms), the beat requested `cut.story.m.dev.sample.intro`, and the player answered `fallback` with trigger `codec` 0.19 s later (5953.7 → 6147.7 ms; 5739.2 → 5928.9 ms); the Title in the error-context snapshot is how every `?play=off&mission=` page looks while its mission runs (`title-start` stays in the DOM to the end of a passing run too). The network log of both tries shows the cause: `GET v768.mp4` answered `200` with `Content-Length: 1248222` and cancelled by WebKit, then `GET v768.mp4` with `Range: bytes=1248206-` cancelled with no response. `v768.mp4` is a fragmented MP4 (`ftyp`, `moov`, nine `moof`/`mdat`, then `mfra` at byte 1248003, its 16-byte `mfro` last): while the file is still arriving after the `moov` is parsed, WebKit's GStreamer demuxer reads the `mfro` with that ranged request. `wrangler dev` serves `/media-local/` from its static-asset server, which has no range support (`curl -H 'Range: bytes=1248206-'` on the local Worker: `HTTP/1.1 200 OK`, `Content-Length: 1248222`), and WebKit fails such a load with `MEDIA_ERR_SRC_NOT_SUPPORTED`, message "Received unexpected 200 HTTP status code for range request" (reproduced outside the game: the fixture from a Node server in 64 KB pieces 30 ms apart, `Range` ignored, 3 of 3 code 4 at about 100 ms after one `bytes=1248206-` request; the same server answering `206` 3 of 3 plays; the whole file at once never issues the ranged read, 3 of 3 plays). The player reads code 4 as `codec` and falls back, so `hasVideo()` stayed false for 60 s. On a quiet machine the download finishes before the demuxer asks, which is why the flake needs a busy runner; the held pointer is incidental (the other clip tests in the same shard passed). Players are not affected: they get clips from the media route (`/media/*` on R2, `routes/media.ts`, `206` for a range); `/media-local/` exists only in QA builds. The first hypothesis (a held press before the Title is ready, or the QA play bypass racing the Title) is refuted by the gate state and the request log above. **Proof:** the regression e2e without `serveMediaRanges` failed 6 of 6 tries on webkit-webgl2 (3 repeats × 2 tries; log `next-host codec`, then `fallback codec`; network log the same two cancelled requests as CI) and passed 3 of 3 on chromium-webgl2; with it 5 of 5 on each browser, every WebKit try reading `bytes=1248206-` (then `bytes=1248003-`, `bytes=131072-`) and every Chromium try `bytes=0-`. The CI test (now `:269`) 20 of 20 on webkit-webgl2 and 10 of 10 on chromium-webgl2, first try (DV_WORLD=synth QA build, CI=1, DV_E2E_PORT=9015); both cutscene spec files on both browsers 27 passed, 1 skipped (the Chromium-only MSE test on WebKit). Open, not done here: `pnpm dev` (the Vite middleware of `apps/web/vite/media-local.ts`) and `wrangler dev` still ignore `Range` on `/media-local/`, so a QA tester on Safari or WebKit can meet the same fallback locally. | Remove `tests/e2e/media-ranges.ts`, its two `beforeEach` lines and the regression e2e (with the `manifestUrl` option of `boot` and `slowClipHost`); the `nextHost` change reverts on its own (remove the `nextHost` branch in `playClip`, `onNextHost` and its e2e; the two unit tests go back to failing only `pool[0]`/`pool[1]`). |
| BD-M02f2-01 | M02f2 (2026-10-25) | **Store batch and quota stop.** `PackStore.prefetch` keeps a queue of packs that have not started (the deferred-priority map is the same queue plus those between their `has` check and their `get`). At most `prefetchWindow` `idle` packs start at once (option, default `IDLE_LANE` = 2, so `__dvBridge.assets.lanes().peak.idle` can reach 2); `soon` packs use a window of 16, the lanes cap them further. The most urgent queued pack starts first, so a pack raised by `promote` starts at once even when the idle window is full. A quota failure is recognised through a `quotaFailed` set that `fetchVerified` fills when `commit` fails for quota (the `commit-failed` event stays as it is); the pack's prefetch item reads it after its `get`, marks the batch stopped, drops the batch's packs that have not started and counts `stats.prefetchStops` once. `PackStore.promote(refs, to)` counts a ref once when its deferred priority was raised or the fetcher moved its queued request. `StatsResult`, `AssetsBridge.stats` and the service's reopen base carry `prefetchStops`. | AC-3 needs "`b` and `c` are never fetched once `a` fails", which only holds when the batch does not start everything at once; a window equal to the idle lane keeps the lane full, and a window option lets the spec test use 1. | Set the window to a very large number (all packs start at once, as before) and drop the stop. Files: `packages/engine/src/assets/store/store.ts`, `rpc.ts`, `service.ts`, `bridge.ts` |
| BD-FIX-CRON-01 | M05d, M06a1, M06b1 (fix, 2026-10-27; DL-OWN-08, DL-744, DL-746) | **One dispatcher Cron Trigger per Worker, now.** `services/edge/wrangler.jsonc` declared 4 daily expressions (`10 3`, `20 3`, `30 3`, `40 3 * * *`) for both `tha-bay-web` and `tha-bay-web-staging`: 8 against Workers Free's 5 per account (`spec/03-decisions-and-facts-2.md` §8 on the Free-plan amendment). DL-746 gave M13c1 the shape (`*/5 * * * *`, jobs in a table); it is brought forward for the four existing jobs. (a) **Config:** both Workers declare `triggers.crons: ["*/5 * * * *"]` (`DISPATCH_CRON`). (b) **Dispatch by time:** `src/ops/scheduled.ts` `SCHEDULE` maps a UTC `HH:MM` to jobs (`03:10` catalog-sync, ledger-export, transcripts; `03:20` memory; `03:30` stub-profiles; `03:40` builder-drafts, the old times and groupings); `slotOf(scheduledTime)` floors `ScheduledController.scheduledTime` to its UTC minute and `dueJobs` returns that minute's row, else nothing. A row matches one minute of the day, so a job runs once a day whatever expression ticks, and the handler never reads `controller.cron`: the four old expressions, live until the operator applies the new trigger, still run the same jobs at the same times. A tick with nothing due returns with no I/O and no log line; a due tick logs `cron.done` with `cron`, `slot` and the results. Each job is unchanged (same I/O, same order). DL-744 §1.4 rule 3 says "by stored timestamp"; matching the scheduled minute needs no stored state (KV is eventually consistent, a DO call per tick costs DO requests), and the jobs are idempotent, so a duplicate delivery of one tick costs a repeat, not a wrong result. (c) **Previews:** `POST /api/v1/_test/run-scheduled` keeps `?job=<name>` and the default (catalog-sync, ledger-export), and adds `?at=HH:MM` (the jobs of that tick, `{slot, ran}`; a minute with no row runs nothing; a malformed time or `job` with `at` is `bad_request`). (d) **Trigger deploy finding:** no CI step applies a cron change. `deploy.yml` runs `deploy:prod`, whose normal path is `wrangler versions upload` + `versions deploy`; in the pinned wrangler 4.151.1 only `wrangler deploy` and `wrangler triggers deploy` call `triggersDeploy` (a PUT that replaces the schedule list), and `versions upload` prints "Changes to triggers (routes, custom domains, cron schedules, etc) must be applied with the command wrangler triggers deploy". The repo runs `wrangler deploy` only in `deploy:bootstrap` (operator, first deploy) and in `deploy:prod`'s Durable Object lifecycle path. No live trigger deploy is added: the operator applies it once per Worker after this commit is deployed (`docs/runbooks/workers-free.md` §1, with the Q-45 dashboard check in §2; `wrangler triggers deploy --dry-run` passes for both environments locally). (e) **CPU (10 ms per cron invocation on Free):** every job is I/O-bound; the large-day risks are `ledger-export` (up to 20 × 500 rows serialised for the DO RPC and PostgREST) and `transcripts` sharing the 03:10 invocation, and `stub-profiles` (forget steps for up to 1,000 profiles); the runbook §3 names the remedy (a separate minute in `SCHEDULE`, smaller batches). **Tests:** `services/edge/test/scheduled-dispatch.test.ts` (one expression on the 5-minute grid; over a week of `*/5` ticks each job runs exactly at its time, once a day, keepalive never; per-minute ticks still run each job once a day; no job at neighbouring minutes or other hours; the `?at=` route); `retention.test.ts` (the table, a 03:20 tick runs memory, empty ticks run and log nothing, an old `10 3 * * *` tick runs the 03:10 row); `catalog-sync.test.ts` (the 03:10 tick); `boot.test.ts` (both configs hold `[DISPATCH_CRON]`). | 8 schedules against 5 can make the next `wrangler deploy` (bootstrap or a DO lifecycle change) refuse; one per Worker leaves room for M13c1…M14b1's jobs as rows, which DL-746 already decided. | Undo: restore the four expressions in both `triggers.crons`, `CRON_SCHEDULE` keyed by expression and `CRON_SCHEDULE[controller.cron] ?? CRON_JOBS` in `src/index.ts`, drop `?at=`, delete `test/scheduled-dispatch.test.ts` and `docs/runbooks/workers-free.md`; the operator then runs `wrangler triggers deploy` again. |
| BD-M02f2-02 | M02f2 (2026-10-25) | **Promotion plumbing.** `LaneHandle.promote` now returns whether the job moved (true only for a queued job and a higher lane), `FetchRetry.promote(url, to)` returns that, and the store's join path calls `fetcher.promote` only for a `now` or `soon` caller (an `idle` joiner has nothing to raise). The new RPC `promote` resolves targets like `prefetch`. `PackStore.fetchAndStore` was the only place that shared a pending fetch without reaching `FetchRetry`'s own promotion. | The milestone names the interfaces; the boolean return is the smallest way to give `promote` a result. | Make `LaneHandle.promote` return void again and drop `FetchRetry.promote`, the `promote` RPC and `AssetClient.promote`. Files: `packages/engine/src/assets/store/{lanes,fetch-retry,store}.ts`, `rpc.ts`, `service.ts`, `client.ts` |
| BD-M02f2-03 | M02f2 (2026-10-25) | **Reveal-gated start.** `prepareWorld` no longer sends `l1`, `l2` and `far` at `soon` before the reveal. `BootPrefetch.start()` runs from `view.whenRevealed()`. In a menu it measures first (resolves each planned ref, asks `has`, reads the Save-Data hint and `navigator.storage.estimate()`, the store kind and the tab mode), then applies `prefetchGate` and queues the missing packs at `idle`; the state is `waiting` until then and `running` after. Past the menu (`menu()` false at the reveal or after the figures were read) the whole plan goes at `soon` and the state is `direct`. A `promote()` that arrives while the state is `waiting` is remembered and runs as soon as the download is queued; `promote()` runs the `now` and `soon` calls only for the refs that were missing at start, once per page session, and returns the count moved by that call (0 after the first). `?prefetch=0` (QA builds) gives state `off`: nothing is requested at any point. The gate's order is read-only tab, memory fallback, Save-Data hint, quota. The report gains `sent` (refs handed to the asset worker) and the bridge gains `prefetchPlan()`. | The reveal gate is the milestone's rule; measuring before the gate is the only way to know `needBytes`; `sent` and `prefetchPlan()` let a spec prove the prefetch from its own report (see BD-M02f2-04). | Send the plan at `soon` from `prepareWorld` as before and delete `boot-prefetch.ts`. Files: `packages/world/src/client/{boot-prefetch,boot-world,world-bridge,index}.ts`, `apps/web/src/main.ts` |
| BD-M02f2-04 | M02f2 (2026-10-25) | **The e2e proves the prefetch from its report, not from the request log.** On the synth world the world streamer demands the same rings, `l1` and `l2` packs as soon as the sim starts, while the Title and the menu are up (the world is the backdrop): in the first runs 8 of the 14 planned packs were requested before the reveal with the prefetch skipped. So "no planned ref is requested before the reveal" and "no planned ref is requested before New Game" cannot hold for the request log. The specs assert instead that the controller queued its download after the reveal instant (`startedMs` against a `performance.now()` mark taken by a MutationObserver on `data-dv-world`), that `sent` is 0 when skipped and equals the missing packs otherwise, that `peak.idle` is at least 1 in the menu run, that each planned URL is requested at most once from page load to 10 s after the HUD, and the state names. On WebKit the pack store is the memory fallback, so the menu run reports `skipped` with `memory` there and the specs expect that. The unit spec covers every gate reason and the order. | AC-6 and AC-7 as written assume nothing else requests planned packs before play; the streamer does. | Tighten the specs once the streamer holds its requests until play, or once `sent` is replaced by a worker-side count of prefetch-originated requests. Files: `tests/e2e/world-prefetch.spec.ts` |

| BD-M01d3-01 | M01d3 | (a) `allowed` in `services/edge/src/ratelimit.ts` is exported and reused by the media throttle (the spec's optional export). (b) The throttle runs after the `OPTIONS` and method checks and before the key grammar, for `GET` and `HEAD`, as the milestone orders; a ranged `GET` is limited like any other. (c) A `Range` header on a request that `parseRangeHeader` rejects is answered from one `head` (404 for a missing key, else 416 with the size); a `Range` on `HEAD` is still ignored (BD-M01d2-01). (d) A start past the end: the local pool's R2 throws `(10039)` rather than returning a body, and the route also handles a returned object by computing `servedRange` against `obj.size` (dropping the body unread); both end in 416 with `bytes */<size>`. (e) The 10039 match is `/\(10039\)\s*$/` on `message`; the pool's text is `get: The requested range is not satisfiable (10039)`, which differs in words from the stub text of AC-4, so only the code is matched (spec/03 §7 row added). (f) `Env.MEDIA_ROUTE` is typed `'on' | 'throttle' | 'off'`; a deployed value outside the union still serves as `on`. | The spec leaves these open; each is the smallest reversible behaviour. | Edit `services/edge/src/routes/media.ts` and `services/edge/test/media.test.ts`. |

A build without the flag has no fighter components and an unchanged engine state hash. |

| BD-FIX-SBXWGPU-01 | M04e1–M04e4 (fix, 2026-10-27; CI flake on chromium-webgpu of `sandbox-actions.spec.ts:92`, `sandbox-teleport.spec.ts:95` and `sandbox-vehicles.spec.ts:63`) | **The Sandbox app specs act on the game only once what they act on has finished; the races were in the specs, the product does what its spec says.** New `tests/e2e/ui/sandbox-helpers.ts`, shared by the four sandbox specs (`sandbox-world` too, which had the same boot wait): `bootSandbox` waits, after the reveal, for the player to be on the ground **and out of the boot move** (control neither 700 nor 701) and for the sandbox to be running (its four view models `mode.sandbox.player/.teleport/.vehicles/.world` published); `openSandboxTab` and `closeSandboxPhone` are the specs' former copies, unchanged; `jumpEnded(page, fadesBefore)` waits for the jump's fade-in, and `sandbox-teleport` now waits for it after Spot 1 and after Back before it reads the pose (the 60 s position polls stay); `jumpPeak` samples the feet over 120 sim ticks (2 s of game time) instead of 2 s of wall time, and `sandbox-vehicles` holds W for 180 ticks instead of 3 s of wall time (`waitTicks`, which fails only when the tick stops moving for 30 s). Every threshold, timeout and assertion of the three specs is unchanged. New regression e2e `tests/e2e/ui/sandbox-slow.spec.ts` (@sandbox-slow, synth half): `slowFrameLoop(page, 1000)` runs every `requestAnimationFrame` callback 1 s late, so the sim (paid from the frame loop, 9 ticks a frame at most) falls behind the page; it asserts the placed player and the four view models after `bootSandbox`, Big jumps on, the Cars tab listing its cars, a jump of at least 3 m, then Spot 1 and Back landing within 3 m with no refusal toast. | **Proven cause** (CI evidence: run 40421958029, job `e2e-shard (chromium-webgpu, 3)` 121918551488, ad581dfe, both tries of each; run 40415315349, job 121896303969; runs 40413802167 (job 121891171705) and 40316584963 (job 121550818856) the flaky tries; Playwright traces of the `playwright-report-chromium-webgpu-3` artifacts). On chromium-webgpu the page is quick: the world revealed about 4 s after load, and the first `observe()` after the reveal read tick 16 to 27, `chunksLoaded` 0, the player at y 19.1 with control **701** (the boot move waiting for its colliders; the ground is 17.6) and `grounded: true` (701 sets it), while chromium-webgl2 runs the same files in about 5 min each (`Slow test file` lines of jobs 121918551523 and 121918551468), long after the sim is done. The old boot wait (`revealed`, then `grounded`) passed there. (1) `sandbox-actions`: `y0` was read at the unplaced height, Escape paused the sim in 701, and the Space press after Resume came while 701 still held the player; the on-foot system takes no input in 700/701 (`onfoot.ts`), so the feet only dropped to the ground and `jumpPeak` returned 0 (both tries, 8.4 s and 8.7 s). (2) `sandbox-vehicles`: the phone opened at tick 18 before the QA play bypass had started the sandbox, Pause held the sim, and the Cars tab had no cars (`btn-car-kestrel-quill-drive` not found, old l.67/l.72). (3) `sandbox-teleport`: the Spot 1 move writes the pose at "ground found" and keeps control 701 through the settle until the fade-in; the spec's position poll passed on the pose (tick 186, control 701), Escape paused the sim with the move unfinished, and Back was asked for while the Spot 1 request was still in flight, so `TeleportService.request` answered busy: every one of the four failing CI traces holds the toast "A teleport is already under way", and the player stayed at Spot 1, 2795.72 m from the target, for 60 s. **Not a lost action for a player:** a jump pressed during a teleport move is not taken by design (01-engine control 7xx), a second teleport while one is under way is refused with that toast (02-world-maps section 5), and outside the QA play bypass the HUD (and so the phone) appears only once the mode runs (shell `Loading` to `InMode` on `modeRunning`). **Deterministic repro:** copies of the old three specs with `slowFrameLoop` before the boot (temporary, not shipped): at 1000 ms 5 of 6 failed with the CI signatures (`big` 0 and `superJump` undefined; the Back distance 2795.722750930535 exactly as in CI, with the busy toast in the trace; the car button not found); at 250 ms 3 of 9 (actions, vehicles); unslowed, the old `sandbox-actions` failed 1 of 1 locally on chromium-webgpu. `sandbox-slow` with the old boot wait failed 3 of 3 (and, without its explicit readiness asserts, 3 of 3 on the behaviour: `superJump` undefined, or a jump of 0); with the new boot wait but the 2 s wall-clock jump window it failed 2 of 2 (peak 1.22 m, the feet still rising: 18 ticks in 2 s). Under heavier pressure (one busy loop per core) the 3 s wall-clock W hold gave a car at 1.91 m/s (`> 2` asserted) in 1 of 6 vehicles tries; the hold in ticks replaces it. **Proof:** the three target tests on chromium-webgpu, `--repeat-each=10`, 2 workers, no retries, pinned to 2 cores with a busy loop on them (`taskset -c 0,1`, on a host at load average about 30): 30 passed (18.0 min); on chromium-webgl2 the four sandbox specs and `sandbox-slow` 5 passed; `sandbox-slow` on chromium-webgpu 3 of 3 (with `sandbox-world` 3 of 3: 6 passed) and on webkit-webgl2 1 of 1 (1.7 min); `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck` clean. Seen once under the two-busy-loop pressure, not in CI, not changed here: the engine's watchdog (`sim worker stalled: no snapshot for 2033 ms`) restarted the sim mid-test. | Delete `tests/e2e/ui/sandbox-helpers.ts` and `sandbox-slow.spec.ts` and restore the four specs' own `bootSynth`/`openTab`/`closePhone` copies, the wall-clock `jumpPeak` and W hold, and the position-only waits after Spot 1 and Back (git revert of the commit). |
| BD-FIX-LOOKGOLD-01 | M02e (fix, 2026-10-27; CI flake of `world-look.spec.ts:214` "look goldens @look-golden › mobile noon-street is within the tolerance of its golden …" on chromium-webgl2, failing the first try of every run) | **A held look shot renders the sky dome's own clock at a fixed `SHOT_SKY_TIME_S` (0), not at the page's age.** `SkyView.freezeTime(true)` (called by `LookView.freezeClock`, which `__dvBridge.world.shot()` uses) stored `nodes.uTime.value`, that is the page time in seconds at the call (`uTime` is the rAF timestamp / 1000), and held it; the dome's painted clouds (`plane + uTime × (0.006, 0.002)`) and the star twinkle (`sin(uTime × 2 + …)`) were therefore drawn at whatever page age the boot reached. It now holds `SHOT_SKY_TIME_S = 0` (new export of `sky-view.ts`), the clock's origin as for `LUMA_SKY_TIME_S` (BD-FIX-CI2-02) and the physical sky's clouds at zero speed; the look clock was already fixed (`SHOT_LOOK_CLOCK_S`, BD-M02e-11). No test, tolerance, timeout or wait changed. **Golden-update:** five goldens regenerated with `DV_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 pnpm check:e2e -- --project=chromium-webgl2 --grep @look-golden` after the fix (PR needs the `golden-update` label); every change is the sky drawn at clock 0 instead of the page age of the old capture, all in the sky rows: `mobile.noon-street` (1671 px differ, 480 over 6/255, max 82, rows 0–47; the dome's clouds through the street canyon), `mobile.night-street` (321 px, 16 over 6, max 13, rows 20–41; star twinkle), `desktop.night-street` (328 px, 24 over 6, max 13, rows 0–41; stars of the dome blend), `mobile.golden-water` (66 px, max 2, rows 0–57) and `desktop.golden-water` (64 px, max 2, rows 0–56). Each new golden equals, pixel for pixel (one pixel off by 1 in `mobile.golden-water`), the old build's capture with the held clock set to 0. The other five (`desktop.noon-street`, `desktop.fog-west`, three `mobile-low`) and the manifest came out unchanged: their capture does not depend on the clock. | **Proven cause.** CI evidence (run 40415315349, job 121896303886, commit 6b87675; both tries' `test.trace` in `playwright-report-chromium-webgl2-4`): `Navigate` to the start of `shot()` took 47.3 s on the first try and 42.7 s on the retry, so the dome clock was held at about 48 and 44 s; results 0.98736 and 0.98888 within. Sweep on the old build (synth QA build, chromium-webgl2, one page per shot, the held clock set to t and the canvas compared with the committed golden): `mobile.noon-street` within 0.99 only for t of about 5 to 42 s (best 0.99992 at 25 s, so the golden was written at a page age near 25 s), 0.98904 at 45 s, 0.98728 at 50 s, 0.98589 at 60 s, 0.97900 at 150 s; the CI first-try values 0.98923–0.98980 and the 0.98736/0.98888 pair sit on this curve at 44–50 s. The out-of-tolerance pixels are all in rows 0–43, the sky between the buildings (columns 128–191 and 208–239 of 256). The same sweep for the other nine shots stays within (worst 0.99935, `desktop.night-street` stars), which is why only this shot failed. CI boots reach `shot()` at 40–50 s, right at the window's upper edge, so a few seconds of boot time decide the result: in the traced run the first try was 4.6 s slower than its retry, and in the runs where the retry passed it presumably landed under the edge (only failed runs keep traces). Injected delay, old build, the sim held at the shot's hour and weather first so only the page age varies: shot at page age 34 s 0.99609 (pass), 62 s 0.98584, 91 s 0.98408, 151 s 0.97892 (fail), four different images; same probe on the fixed build at 39, 92 and 152 s: three byte-identical images, held `uTime` 0. Old build under CPU pressure (`taskset -c 0,1` with two busy loops on cores 0 and 1), target `--repeat-each=4 --retries=0`: 4 of 4 failed (0.97651–0.98421). **Proof (fixed build, new goldens):** under the same CPU pressure the target `--repeat-each=10 --retries=0` 10 passed; alone as the first test of a fresh run (one worker, pressure) 1 passed; the whole `@look-golden` group `--retries=0` on a fresh DV_WORLD=synth QA build (DV_E2E_PORT=9130): 11 passed, 1 skipped (the `DV_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1`-only manifest writer), 29.6 min. The hypotheses of shader warm-up, late textures, LOD, fade, traffic or the golden being taken from a retry-like state are refuted: the picture after `shot()` is a function of the held sky clock alone (two captures 2 frames apart were byte-identical at every page age). | Restore `this.frozenTime = on ? this.nodes.uTime.value : null` in `SkyView.freezeTime`, drop `SHOT_SKY_TIME_S`, and restore the five goldens from the parent commit. |
| BD-FIX-WATERRESPAWN-01 | M02c1 (fix, 2026-10-27; CI flake of `world-bounds.spec.ts` "a player walked into the water respawns on the shore within 3 s" on chromium-webgl2) | **The e2e walks the player into the water in bot steps of 45 sim ticks, so the stick is never held into the respawn; no product code changed.** The walk was one 40 s bot script (`debug.bot`, forward at 4.2 m/s), stopped by `stopWalking` only after four page round trips that followed the poll that first saw Swim (`observe`, `query`, `shore`, `act`). The swim respawn (`swim.ts`) waits `SWIM_RESPAWN_TICKS` (90), then the teleport places the player at the shore pose and settles 15 ticks later; from then on the on-foot controller reads the stick again, as it must. When the stop reached the sim after that, the player ran from the pose back toward the sea, and the test measured that run. New in `tests/e2e/world-bounds.spec.ts`: `walkIntoWater(page, camYaw)` installs a 45-tick step (`STEP_TICKS`, half of the swim's 90) and starts the next one only when the page sees the step is over (on foot, idle, horizontal speed under 0.01 m/s, more than 0.5 m from where it began) or sees Swim. A step starts only while the player stands still on land, so the stick is held at most 45 ticks after the player enters deep water, counted by the sim: a slow page adds only standing time between steps. The swim tick and the `into` position now come from the observation that saw Swim. Before, `into` came from a second `observe`, which on a slow page could fall after the respawn; that happened in 1 of the 3 delayed-stop runs below (`wet.isWater` false). `toTheCoast(page)` holds the old set-up so the two water tests share it. The assertions, the 3 s, the 3 m and the timeouts are unchanged. New regression e2e (same file, `@world-bounds`) "a player respawned from the water stands still at the shore pose, however late the page looks": it walks in with `walkIntoWater`, then looks only once the sim has run 4 s past the swim, and again 60 ticks later. Both looks must show on foot, idle, within 0.5 m of the pose, the two less than 1 cm apart, on dry ground. New unit test in `packages/engine/test/control/swim.spec.ts` for the two product properties the walk relies on: a stick held while swimming moves nothing, and once the stick is free the respawned player stays exactly at the shore point (32, −90) for 120 ticks with zero horizontal velocity. It passes on the old code: the product was already right. Spec check: 02-world-maps.md §4.1 (a) and 01-engine.md §2.4 say only that an on-foot player in deep water swims and respawns at the nearest shore node. Velocity is cleared at placement (`ownership.ts`, "ground found"), and nothing in the spec says a held stick is to be ignored after a respawn. For the Architect, not done here: a person who holds forward with the camera toward the sea runs straight back in after the fade, because the on-foot swim respawn drops the inland yaw of `shoreRespawnPose` and the camera is not turned. A stick latch or an inland camera after a water respawn would be a design change (a new hashed state field), so it is a proposal, not this fix. | **Proven cause** (CI evidence: run 40406569016, job 121866032949 `e2e-shard (chromium-webgl2, 3)`, both tries; the `1-trace.trace` of the first try in artifact `playwright-report-chromium-webgl2-3`). Shore node `sn.11800.81980` at (1180, 8198), normal (0, 1), so `wanted.pose` is (1180, 8196). Before the swim the player ran at 4.2 m/s, control 102 (z 8196.49, then 8199.64). The poll saw Swim at stats tick 866, at (1180.00002, 8200.06). The stop command went out 1.6 s of wall time after that observation (911.70 s to 913.39 s in the trace). The arrival observation, stats tick 996 (log: "swam at tick 866, back on land at tick 996 (2.17 s of sim time)"), shows the player at (1180, 8199.0975), velocity 0, control 100. Its x is exactly 1180, the teleport target, and its z is 3.0975 m seaward of the pose: the player had been placed on the pose and had run back toward the sea until the stop arrived. The retry shows the same at 3.2375 m (928 to 1046). **Reproduced:** the old test under CPU pressure (`taskset -c 0,1` plus two busy loops on the same cores, CI=1, `--retries=0`) failed 3 of 5. One failure is the CI signature, 3.1675 m. In the other two (283 and 290 ticks) the held stick took the player back into the sea and the poll caught the second respawn. **Deterministic:** the old test with its stop sent only once the sim tick reached the swim tick plus 160 failed 3 of 3 (250 and 243 ticks, then `wet.isWater` false). The new regression e2e with the old walk put back (the 40 s script, no stop before the late look) failed 3 of 3: a second after the late look the player was at z 8198.888, 8200.008 and 8200.008, control 701, 102 and 102. With `walkIntoWater` both looks show (1180.000, 2.000, 8196.000), the pose. **Proof:** the target (now `:104`) 20 of 20 on chromium-webgl2 under CPU pressure (`taskset -c 0,1` plus two busy loops on the same cores, on a machine at load average about 19 from other sessions; CI=1, `--retries=0`, DV_WORLD=synth QA build, DV_E2E_PORT=9150; 38.0 min; swim to arrival 1.10 to 2.52 s of sim time); the regression e2e 3 of 3 under the same pressure; all four `@world-bounds` tests once on chromium-webgl2 (the AC-12 proof form) and once on webkit-webgl2; `pnpm lint` and `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm check:unit -- packages/engine` 118 files, 1072 tests passed (a first run under that load exited 1, output not kept; the rerun passed whole) | Revert the commit. In the first water test, put back `walk(page, camYawFor(s.nx, s.nz), 40)`, the swim poll, the second `observe` for `into` and `stopWalking` before the arrival poll, with the inline set-up. Remove `walkIntoWater`, `toTheCoast`, `STEP_TICKS` and `CONTROL_STATE_IDLE`, the regression e2e, and the unit test (with its `LocalPlayer`, `Velocity` and `YAW` imports) from `swim.spec.ts`. |

| BD-FIX-PERF-CHASE-01 | M05c2 (fix, 2026-10-27; CI flake of `tests/perf/perf-budget.spec.ts:25` "story-chase at desktop writes its counts report @perf" on webkit-webgl2) | **The perf scenario `story-chase` now waits for each `mission.start` to be answered, instead of giving it a fixed 20 s. No product code changed.** On the synth world the chase is m.a1.03. Its start marker at the Yard is more than 300 m from the boot spawn, so `mission.start` first moves the player there (DL-734: `StoryController.beginPlacing`, a pre-streamed `teleport.request('respawn')` retried for `PLACE_BUDGET_TICKS`, the last request given `PLACE_ANSWER_TICKS`). The mission runs only once the Yard tiles are resident and the player stands on them. The old setup treated "no debug state after 20 s" as "does not resolve here". That fits m.a1.12, which is refused at once (`mission:m.a1.12:disabled` toast), but not a far start that is still streaming. A real player's start is not lost: the move keeps retrying with the `mission.startLoading` hint, and then starts the mission or refuses it with `mission.startUnreachable` (Jobs row `retry`). New in `tests/perf/scenarios/story-chase.ts`: a MutationObserver records `data-toast-id`s from the setup on. `page.waitForFunction` (250 ms polling, in the page) ends on the first answer: the debug state names the mission (`started`), or one of `refusedToasts(id)` appears (`mission:<id>:disabled`, `mission:<id>:start`, `mission:start:<id>`). The bound is `START_ANSWER_MS` (240 s), above the move's 12,600 ticks (3.5 min at 60 Hz). The setup logs `story-chase: m.a1.12 refused after N s …; m.a1.03 started after N s …`. A failure names each start's outcome, timing and toasts, plus any `sim worker …` console line (watchdog restart, see below). The Jobs-list wait now fails with its own message. The 240 s Jobs wait and the 480 s test timeout are unchanged. m.a1.12 no longer burns 20 s. | **Proven cause.** CI evidence: run 40453163476, job 122020327917 (head f33348f8). The failing test took 49.4 s: boot, then both 20 s windows, then the throw at `story-chase.ts:59`, so the Jobs list had been published and neither start was seen. From the `perf-reports` `createdAt` against the job log: webkit desktop-ultra spent about 75 s before its 8 s run (about 55 s on boot plus the m.a1.03 start once m.a1.12's 20 s is taken off). Webkit mobile spent about 29 s (about 9 s). The base run 40451286297 shows the same at desktop (1.5 min) against mobile (42.7 s). So on WebKit at desktop tiers the Yard start runs in the tens of seconds, near or above 20 s. Locally (DV_WORLD=synth QA build, port 9410, webkit-webgl2, desktop): unloaded, m.a1.03 started 11.1 s after its send. Under `taskset -c 0,1` plus two busy loops on those cores it took about 24 s and passed only on the check after the deadline. **Reproduced (old code):** with an injected delay on the Yard's `world_pack/p0-*` fetches (a `page.route` installed just before the setup, in an uncommitted copy of perf-budget.spec.ts), it failed 3 of 3 with the CI error (6 s delay, busy loops) and 3 of 3 (25 s delay, `taskset -c 0,1` only). **Proof (new code), same 25 s delay under `taskset -c 0,1`:** 10 of 10 passed. m.a1.12 was refused after 0.7–10.8 s; m.a1.03 started after 33.9–48.5 s. Without the injection, `pnpm check:perf -- --scenario story-chase` under `taskset -c 0,1` passed: 8 of 8 tests and "check:perf passed (8 reports)". m.a1.03 started after 57.8 s and 60.8 s on chromium desktop-ultra and desktop, and after 2.5–11.3 s on WebKit. **Separate mechanism, not fixed here (for the Architect):** under heavy CPU starvation the sim-host stall watchdog (`sim-host.ts` `STALL_MS` 2000; 01-engine.md §5 "missed snapshots 2 s") restarted a slow but live worker. The page showed "Recovering…" and returned to the main menu, which dropped the story mode and any start in flight. Seen 1 of 10 runs with busy loops and a 6 s delay (right after the m.a1.12 send), and 1 of 8 old-code runs under `taskset` only (at world reveal, then a 240 s Jobs-wait timeout). The CI log cannot rule this out for run 40453163476, because console output and traces are not uploaded. The new failure message prints the `sim worker stalled` line when it happens. Whether 2 s of missed snapshots should count as a crash on a starved device is a spec question (proposal: a longer window, or a liveness ping, before "Recovering…"). | Revert the commit: `tests/perf/scenarios/story-chase.ts` goes back to the fixed 20 s per-mission loop with `state()` polling. |
| BD-FIX-OFFLINEDL-01 | M04b4 (fix, 2026-10-27; CI flake of `packages/engine/test/assets/offline-download.spec.ts` "PackStore city download (M04b4) > a second downloadAll fetches only what is still missing, and clear() empties the store" in the `unit` job) | **The spec now waits for the detached commits of the aborted first download before it counts what is stored; no product code changed.** New `until(ok, ms = 5000)` helper in the spec (real timers, polls every 5 ms, as in `store.promote.spec.ts`, commit a8e69216), here with an async predicate. After the first download is aborted and every held request is released, the test waits until the number of stored packs equals `fetcher.calls.length` (every started fetch serves a valid pack, so each one commits), then `settled()`, and only then reads `before` and `stored`. The assertions (`stored >= 3`, new fetches = refs − stored, 6 packs, `clear()` to 0) and every number are unchanged. **Product check:** `downloadAll` has no race. A get whose caller aborted keeps its shared fetch running (`fetchAndStore`: "a caller that aborts stops waiting, and the fetch still caches the pack"); that fetch hashes the bytes (`crypto.subtle.digest`, off the JS thread) and only then queues its commit on the store chain. `settled()` is documented as waiting for mutations already queued, so a fetch still hashing is not covered. A second `downloadAll` that meets such a pack joins the in-flight fetch or finds it committed, and correctly fetches nothing for it. | **Proven cause.** CI evidence: run 40440975992, job 121980977380 `unit`, and run 40500401386, job 122176365238 `unit`: both `AssertionError: expected +0 to be 1` at `offline-download.spec.ts:148:45` (`calls.length - before` against `refs.length - stored`), so `stored` was read as 5 while the sixth pack (released in the post-abort drain) was still between its fetch and its commit; the second run then fetched 0. The test waited for that commit only with the `release()` macrotask and `settled()`. **Reproduced:** the old spec under CPU pressure (`taskset -c 0,1` with one busy loop on each of cores 0 and 1), 30 separate `vitest run` of the target: 29 passed, 1 failed with the CI signature `expected +0 to be 1`. Forced interleaving (a temporary copy of the old spec, not shipped, whose `hash` option delays only the sixth hash by 15 ms): fails 1 of 1 with `expected +0 to be 1` at the same line; the same copy of the new spec passes. **Proof:** the new spec under the same CPU pressure, 50 separate runs of the whole file: 50 of 50 passed (7 tests each); `pnpm lint` and `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm --filter @dv/engine test` 120 files, 1087 tests passed. | Revert the commit (drop `until` and the wait before `settled()` in that test). |
| BD-FIX-WATERLATCH-01 | M02c1, M03a1 (fix, 2026-10-27; Architect decision DL-753, 02-world-maps.md §4.1 (a)) | **A water respawn now turns the player (or the car) and the camera inland and latches the movement input until it is centred.** New `packages/engine/src/sim/control/latch.ts`: `ControlShared` gains `moveLatch`, `respawnSeq` and `respawnYaw`. The swim respawn (`swim.ts`), when its teleport is accepted, sets the player's facing to the inland yaw `datan2(nx, nz)` (forward is minus the shore node's normal, which points to the water) and arms the latch; when the fade-in starts it records the respawn (`respawnSeq` + 1, `respawnYaw`). The vehicle water wall (`vehicle-system.ts`) arms the latch at the stall and records the respawn (the yaw `placeVehicle` already used) when the car is put down, both only while a player is aboard (`vehicleHasPlayer`), so an ambient car changes nothing. The owner that reads the stick asks `moveLatched(control, frame)` once the player is back under its control: the on-foot controller after the teleport states (a swimmer and a teleporting player never reach it), the vehicle input system once the car is no longer stalled. A frame with `moveX`, `moveY`, `throttle`, `brake` and `steer` all 0 lets go (the stick in its dead zone, every movement key and touch button up: the router quantizes them all to 0, and each context binds only its own axes); until then the movement axes read as centred (Jump, the handbrake and the look still work). A release during the fade does not count, so a key pressed again before the player is back stays latched. No wall clock: the latch is sim state, cleared only by the input the sim reads. **Hash and checkpoint:** the `control` resource hashes and saves the three fields only once they are in use (`moveLatch` or `respawnSeq` not 0), as a trailing block that `load` reads only when bytes remain, so every run without a water respawn hashes and checkpoints byte for byte as before and an older checkpoint loads with the latch off. No golden or hash changed: no recorded scenario respawns from water (the unit and determinism suites passed unchanged). **Page:** `runtime.ts` adds `respawn: { seq, yaw }` (from the first respawn on) and `latched: true` (while latched) to the player side block `pl` (`PlayerSide`, optional fields). `boot-engine.ts` turns the view when it sees a new `respawn.seq`: on foot `look.yaw` takes the yaw and the rig snaps (`CameraRigImpl.snap()`, new; the veil is down), in a vehicle the chase camera snaps behind the car (the existing `snapBehind`). The QA observation's `camera` gains `yaw` (the page's look yaw). No contract change (`ControlShared`, `PlayerSide` and the observation are engine-internal). | **Proven cause** (the gap DL-753 names; CI evidence: run 40406569016, job 121866032949 `e2e-shard (chromium-webgl2, 3)`, log re-read 2026-10-27: "swam at tick 866, back on land at tick 996", then `Expected: <= 3, Received: 3.0975000113248825`, and on the retry 3.2375): the swim respawn placed the player on the shore pose (1180, 8196) but kept its seaward facing and the page's camera, and from the first settled tick the on-foot controller read the forward stick that was still held, so the player ran 3.1 m back toward the sea. In code: `swim.ts` passed only a position to `requestTeleport` and dropped the inland yaw that `shoreRespawnPose` computes, nothing turned the page's `look`, and `onfoot.ts` had no latch. **Reproduced:** with the latch switched off (`moveLatched` returning false) the new e2e failed on chromium-webgl2 (QA build, DV_WORLD=synth, `--retries=0`): when the respawn was recorded the player was already 3.8675 m from the pose; 4 of the new unit tests failed the same way. **Proof:** new unit tests in `packages/engine/test/control/swim.spec.ts` (forward held into the water and through the respawn: the player faces +X inland, stands at (32, −90) for 120 more ticks with `moveLatch` true, one centred tick lets go, then it walks inland; a sideways and backward key pressed again during the swim is still latched; a dry walk never latches; hash and checkpoint: the default fields change neither, a checkpoint restores the latch; determinism with the stick held) and `packages/engine/test/vehicle.water.spec.ts` (full throttle and steer held through the stall and the fade: the car is put down facing −X, stays within 0.3 m for 120 ticks at under 0.2 m/s with throttle 0, one released tick lets go, then it drives inland past 3 m/s; an empty car stalling in the water latches nothing). New e2e in `tests/e2e/world-bounds.spec.ts` (`@world-bounds`) "forward held through a water respawn: the player waits on the shore pose facing inland until released, then walks inland": one 40 s bot script holds forward toward the sea through the respawn; the published yaw matches the shore node's normal and the pose is 2 m inland of that node; 2 s and 3 s of sim time later the player is on foot, idle, latched, within 0.5 m of the pose, still (under 1 cm), and the page's camera yaw is the inland yaw; after the bot lets go the latch clears, and the real keyboard W (along the page's camera) walks the player more than 2 m inland, straight, on dry ground, with no second respawn. All five `@world-bounds` tests passed once on chromium-webgl2 and once on webkit-webgl2 (QA build, DV_WORLD=synth, DV_E2E_PORT=9470, one worker, `--retries=0`); the three water tests twice each on chromium-webgl2 under CPU pressure (`taskset -c 0,1` with a busy loop on the same cores; the same `--grep water` also ran the three `golden-water` look goldens once each, which passed, so the page's change leaves the look untouched). `pnpm lint` and `pnpm typecheck` clean; `pnpm check:unit -- packages/engine packages/world` 1770 passed, with one timeout of `lint.bans.spec.ts` at 30 s while `pnpm lint` and a world bake ran beside it (it passed alone, 4 of 4, 17.5 s) | Revert the commit. By hand: delete `control/latch.ts` and its export in `control/index.ts`; remove `moveLatch`, `respawnSeq` and `respawnYaw` from `ControlShared`, `createSimServices` and the `control` resource (hash, save, load); in `swim.ts` remove `inlandYaw`, the facing, `armMoveLatch` and `noteWaterRespawn`; in `onfoot.ts` remove `latched` (and `control` from its services type); in `vehicle-system.ts` restore the latch-free input branch and `respawnAtShore`'s old signature; remove `respawn` and `latched` from `PlayerSide` and `runtime.ts`, `respawnSeen` and its block from `boot-engine.ts`, `camera.yaw` from the observation and `snap()` from the rig; remove the new tests. |
| BD-M05c3-01 | M05c3 act1-prewarm | **The cache check runs before the sheet gate.** The plan walks each G1 ref as: HEAD `cutscenes/<key>/manifest.json` (`ready`, $0), then route 27 on a miss (`pending`, $0), then the sheet gate (`sheet_missing`, $0), then the endpoint check and the estimate against the line. The milestone lists the sheet gate (step 5) before the cache check (step 6). | A published manifest is done whatever the sheets say, and the report must show it as a clip, not as in-engine for a missing sheet. Offline runs skip both network steps, so their order does not matter. A ref with a missing sheet costs one HEAD and one route 27 read online; nothing is spent either way. | Swap the two blocks in `buildPlan` (`tools/assets/gen/prewarm.ts`) and the test "a ready key is never re-planned even when its sheet is missing". |
| BD-M05c3-02 | M05c3 act1-prewarm | **`--live` stops at two file-only gates, then at the Workers Free refusal, before any port is touched.** Order: flags, provider gate, C2PA guard (prod only, reads `vars` of `services/edge/wrangler.jsonc`), `blocked: no transcoder on Workers Free; lands in M05c4`. The ledger, the HEAD and route 27 are never reached, so the fetch spy of AC-1, AC-7 and AC-9 sees 0 calls. A test-only `--wrangler <file>` reads another Worker configuration (the AC-7 fixture `wrangler-resign-off.jsonc`) instead of the repository one. | AC-7 needs a Worker build with `C2PA_RESIGN` off without editing the real file. The flag follows `--requests`, `--registry` and `--sheets` as a test input. | Remove `--wrangler` and the `wranglerFile` parameters in `tools/assets/gen/cli.ts`; the AC-7 unit tests then use the probe port only. |
| BD-M05c3-03 | M05c3 act1-prewarm | **`C2PA_RESIGN` is only a `vars` entry (`"1"` production, `"0"` staging and previews); `Env` and `PipelineEnv` do not type it yet.** Only the CLI guard reads it. The report has no ledger-delta line, and the CLI has no `OPS_TOKEN`, route 28, polling or index-rebuild code. | The milestone limits `services/edge` to the var, and live submission is M05c4's (DL-750). The archived Container build had the `Env` field, the report line and the submission path; they come back with M05c4 where something reads or writes them. | Add `C2PA_RESIGN?: string` to `services/edge/src/env.ts` and `services/pipeline/src/env.ts` when the Worker starts reading it. |
| BD-M05c3-04 | M05c3 act1-prewarm | **The `prewarm-act1` scenario plays the operator's part.** The CLI cannot submit on Workers Free, so `services/pipeline/test/prewarm-act1.test.ts` runs the plan (`runPrewarm`, not live), submits exactly the planned keys through an in-process stand-in for route 28 (the M05b1 mock path: single-flight object, stages, `MockFalPort`, `MockSignPort`, `MockTranscodePort`), then plans again: every row is `ready`, so nothing is submitted and the `FalPort` call count does not change. A last step runs `--live` and asserts it prints only the transcoder refusal. It is picked up by `check:pipeline` through the existing `vitest run --root services/pipeline`. | The AC-5 wording ("submits the planned keys through route 28") describes the path M05c4 turns on; this is the same proof over the part that exists. | Replace the stand-in with the real hand-off when M05c4 lands. |
| BD-M05c4-01 | M05c4 offedge-finish | **The hand validators and the one manifest builder live in `@dv/shared` (`genai/cutscene-offedge.ts`).** It holds the key names (`handoffKeyOf`, `finishKeyOf`, `refusedKeyOf`, `finishOutKeyOf`), `HandoffRecord`, `FinishRecord`, `RefusedRecord`, `parseHandoff`, `parseFinish` (64 KiB cap, no schema library) and `buildCutsceneManifest`, which the in-Worker mock path, the operator's `--finish` and the publish phase's checks all call. C09 is only read; no contract shape changed. | The Worker (services/pipeline) and the operator tools (tools/assets) may not import each other, but both may import `@dv/shared`; one builder keeps the two halves from drifting. |
| BD-M05c4-02 | M05c4 offedge-finish | **`JobSinkPort` gained `handoff()` and an optional second argument of `fail(err, code?)`.** A refused publish of a handed-off key calls `fail` with the gate code: `KILLED` blocks the key, any other code leaves it `handed_off` with the error recorded. `fail` on a `handed_off` key never moves it to `failed`, so the re-arm cannot return it to `absent`. | The milestone names only `handoff()`; the publish phase has to tell the object whether a refusal was a kill (AC-6), and the port is internal (not a contract). |
| BD-M05c4-03 | M05c4 offedge-finish | **`FalPort.fetchStream` and `BlobPort.putStream`/`copy` were added; `put` takes `cacheControl`.** `LiveFalPort.fetchStream` returns the fetch body and its Content-Length. `r2Blob.putStream` pipes through a `FixedLengthStream` of that size (R2 refuses a stream of unknown length, and the stream then fails on a wrong byte count); `copy` reads an R2 object body into another bucket and compares the size to `finish.json`. A fal output without a Content-Length fails the `copy-orig` step with a clear message instead of buffering it. | Workers Free allows 10 ms of CPU per step, so the original is never buffered or hashed in the Worker (AC-1); the stream path was proven in Node over Miniflare (a test double reads the stream whole) and in workerd (`services/edge/test/cutscene-handoff.test.ts`). |
| BD-M05c4-04 | M05c4 offedge-finish | **A handed-off key has no timer.** `handoff()` deletes the alarm; the 2 h watchdog and the re-arm only act on `pending` and `failed`. The publish instance is remembered in the object (`publishInstanceId`, `publishSince`) and counts as running for 30 minutes; the check is lazy, at the next route 28 call. `reset()` also ends a `handed_off` key (the operator's way out). `ensure` on a handed-off key starts the publish phase with or without `allowGenerate`, so route 28 now always calls `ensure` (a kill switch or governor level 3 still holds back new generation, never a publish). | AC-1 needs the state to survive the watchdog and re-arm; a publish is not generation and the spend was settled at the hand-off. |
| BD-M05c4-05 | M05c4 offedge-finish | **The pairing rule is enforced three times and an absent `TRANSCODE_MODE` follows `FAL_MODE`.** `modePairingError` (env.ts) is checked by `CutsceneJobDO.ensure` (answers `failed`, claims nothing, no Workflow), by `GenJobWorkflow.run` (calls `fail`, no ports built) and by `runCutsceneJob` (before the gate, so no budget is reserved). `TRANSCODE_MODE=container` is not a mode on Workers Free and counts as a pairing error. `RunContext.transcodeMode` is optional: the shim tests that omit it get `mock` for FAL_MODE=mock and `offedge` for live. | AC-2 requires 0 reservations and 0 FalPort calls; the old tests build the context without the new field. |
| BD-M05c4-06 | M05c4 offedge-finish | **A job that finds `handoff.json` in PRIVATE hands off again at $0 instead of submitting.** In `offedge` mode the step `handoff-check` follows the cache check. The state can be lost (an operator `reset`) while the original is still there; the prewarm CLI skips such a key for the same reason. | The milestone says a key is generated at most once and the hand-off is stored state; the file is the second copy of that fact. An operator who wants a regeneration deletes `handoff.json` too (docs/releases/README.md). |
| BD-M05c4-07 | M05c4 offedge-finish | **`handoff.json` stores the whole job; `finish.json` stores the manifest body and a run id.** `handoff.json` = `{schema, key, jobKey, job, plan, requestId, providerKey, endpoint, pinnedUpdatedAt, characters, licence, subs, costUsdMicros, at}`. The publish phase re-runs `gate('resign')` over `handoff.job` (so a character added by the real request is checked, not the mock-grade job that route 28 builds). `finish.json` = `{schema, key, runId, files[{file, bytes, sha256, contentType, cacheClass}], manifest, at}`; `runId` is `r<base36 ms>-<orig sha 8>`; files sit under `out/<runId>/`; `cacheClass` `immutable` becomes `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` and the manifest gets `public, max-age=300`. `refused.json` carries the run id, and `--finish` ignores a refusal of an older run. | The milestone lists the contents loosely; these are the fields the three readers (publish phase, `--finish`, the CLI poll) need. |
| BD-M05c4-08 | M05c4 offedge-finish | **`--finish` does all local work for every key before its first upload.** Order: signing credentials (prod refuses with exit 1, other envs print SKIP and exit 0), private pair and `OPS_TOKEN` (SKIP), list `cutscenes-orig/`, skip keys with a `finish.json`, a ready manifest or a blocked state; then per key download, transcode, qa-gate, re-sign and read back; only then upload (files, `finish.json`, route 28 for that key) and poll route 27 and `refused.json`. A key that fails the qa-gate or the read-back is not uploaded and makes the run exit 1. | AC-5: a refusal must leave the bucket as it was; one failing key must not keep the others from finishing. |
| BD-M05c4-09 | M05c4 offedge-finish | **qa-gate thresholds (not in the spec):** frame count within 17 of the plan (spec), black frames (`blackdetect d=0.5:pix_th=0.10`) or frozen frames (`freezedetect n=-60dB:d=2`) covering half the clip or more fail it, and when the original has an audio track an integrated loudness (`ebur128`) outside -40 to -9 LUFS fails it. The original must be H.264 at 24 fps, as the first build's container required. | §6 step 8 names black, frozen frames and loudness without numbers; these are conservative and live in one function (`qaVerdict`) for the Architect to tune. |
| BD-M05c4-10 | M05c4 offedge-finish | **`@contentauth/c2pa-node` 0.9.10 is a root devDependency and its postinstall is switched off** (`pnpm-workspace.yaml` `allowBuilds`: false). | BD-FIX-TOUCH-01 | M04b2 (fix, 2026-10-27; CI flake of `tests/e2e/ui/touch.spec.ts:36` "stick, look and a button register together and a pointercancel releases everything @ui-touch" on chromium-webgl2, `expect(states.has(110)).toBe(true)` received false) | **The spec reads the jump from every snapshot the page draws, keyed by sim tick, not from runner-side polls; no product code changed.** State 110 is `CONTROL_STATE.jump` (`packages/engine/src/sim/control/states.ts`). `onfoot.ts` shows it only while the feet rise (`vy > 0`): v0 = √(2 × 9.81 × 1.0) = 4.43 m/s, so 4.43 / 9.81 × 60 ≈ 27 ticks (0.45 s of game time). After that the player is in 111 (fall), then 112 (land) and back to 102 (run). The old spec added `observe().player.control` to `states` once per pass of its 40-pass look loop (one `contact` round trip, one `observe` round trip, a 40 ms wait). On a loaded runner a pass takes 500–650 ms of wall time, 30–45 ticks, so the first sample after the press could already be past the apex. New in the spec: `startControlLog` installs a `requestAnimationFrame` loop in the page that logs `(tick, control)` for every new snapshot the page reads, and `stopControlLog` ends it. The page reads the newest snapshot once a frame and a frame pays for at most 9 ticks, so log entries are one or two frame groups apart (largest gap seen under CPU pressure: 18 ticks), always under the 27-tick jump. The log starts before the contacts. After the look loop the spec waits with `waitTicks` (from `sandbox-helpers.ts`) until the sim has run `JUMP_WATCH_TICKS` (120) past the press, then asserts that the logged states contain `CONTROL_JUMP` (110); the failure message gives the tick range, the snapshot count, the largest gap and the states seen. The contacts, the 40-pass look loop, the 1 m move, the three-contact count, the pointercancel stop and the 300 s timeout are unchanged. | **Proven cause** (CI evidence: run 40444522436, job 121994326540 `e2e-shard (chromium-webgl2, 3)`, branch `claude/integ-spec-look` at b3b7fac, both tries; the retry's `1-trace.trace` in artifact `playwright-report-chromium-webgl2-3`). Jump `down` at 13.82 s into the trace. The first `observe` after it (14.38 s, tick 172) shows control **111** at y 0.979 with `grounded` false: the player was already at the apex, so the jump had happened. Every later sample was 102 (run), about 30–45 ticks apart (ticks 204, 249, 288, …). The product works: the 1 m move passed and the feet reached the 1.0 m apex. The sampler missed the 27-tick rising phase. **Reproduced** with the old spec on a DV_WORLD=synth QA build (DV_E2E_PORT=9430), chromium-webgl2, one worker, `--retries=0`, pinned with `taskset -c 0,1` plus busy loops on the same cores: 2 of 5 failed at l.60 with two busy loops. With four busy loops 3 of 5 failed at l.60, and a fourth failed earlier in `waitForEngine` (90 s boot). A local failing trace has the same signature: the first sample after the press was 111 at y 0.999. **Deterministic:** temporary copies of the spec (deleted afterwards) with a 600 ms wait right after the Jump press, which is the CI press-to-first-sample gap. The old sampler failed 3 of 3; the new log passed 3 of 3, and under two busy loops it passed 3 of 3 with 2–5 snapshots in state 110 and a largest gap of 16–18 ticks. **Proof:** the fixed target under `taskset -c 0,1` with two busy loops, `--repeat-each=10 --retries=0`, one worker: 10 passed (17.4 min). All four `@ui-touch` tests of the file on webkit-webgl2 and chromium-webgpu, once each: 8 passed. `pnpm lint` and `pnpm typecheck` clean. No package was touched, so there are no unit specs to run. | Revert the commit: restore the runner-side `states` set filled in the look loop and `expect(states.has(110)).toBe(true)`, and remove `startControlLog`, `stopControlLog`, `CONTROL_JUMP`, `JUMP_WATCH_TICKS` and the `waitTicks` import. |

One loop over the frame's `poses` (the same array those renderers read, called from `WorldView.frame` right after them) covers cars, pedestrians, the player's avatar and any gray-box vehicle (the engine's `vehicleFootprint(key)`), so the 200-odd traffic instances are walked once more instead of two renderers growing a second output each, and a car and its blob can never disagree. `traffic/instances.ts` gained only the caster switch (`setShadows`) and the paint reflection. | BD-FIX-ODBL-01 | M02c2 world release (fix, 2026-10-28; Architect decision DL-754, ODbL 4.6, DL-695) | **`assets:publish --world` accepts the ODbL offer already published for a bake id instead of re-archiving HEAD.** `pnpm assets:publish -- --env staging --world assets/world/sf.lock.json --pointer-out assets/world/pointer.staging.json` failed with `ODbL offer: odbl/bf9c29c4…/bake-scripts.tar.gz: size 357723, expected 361534`: the offer of 2026-10-26 is immutable (`rclone --immutable --ignore-existing`), and `git archive HEAD tools/map config/world content/world` had changed since (M07a2 `tools/map/src/race/*`, M02g1 `config/world/look*.json`) while the bake still reproduces to the same id with byte-identical packs and an unchanged `sf.lock.json`. `uploadOffer` (`tools/assets/src/publish.ts`) now reads `odbl/<bakeId>/index.json` first (`Uploader.readJson`, `rclone cat`). An existing index is accepted, with nothing uploaded, archived or overwritten, when it is an `odbl-offer/1` index naming this bake id and either the same sorted world-pack list or the same SHA-256 for the world lock file, and every file it lists is present at its recorded size (`Uploader.verify`, `rclone lsjson`). Otherwise the run fails, uploads nothing more and writes no pointer: another bake id, other packs and another lock, an index without `bake-scripts.tar.gz`, a listed object missing or at another size (partial offer), or objects under `odbl/<bakeId>/` with no `index.json` (`Uploader.list`, `rclone lsf`; partial offer). A prefix with nothing in it still gets a fresh offer archived from HEAD. The publish output prints `ODbL offer tarball: <key>, <bytes> bytes, sha256 <hex>` and `ODbL offer URL: …` for both, `ODbL offer accepted: …; nothing uploaded or replaced` for an accepted one; `PublishResult.offer` and an additive `odblOffer {bakeId, url, index, tarball {key, bytes, sha256}}` field in `pointer.<env>.meta.json` carry the same values for both, so a re-run writes a byte-identical meta file (`world-release.ts` reads only the lock and world-index fields). `readJson` and `list` are now declared on `Uploader` (`RemoteFiles` picks them, so `assets:gen prewarm --finish` is unchanged); the `dir:` uploader implements both. Cases in `tools/assets/test/publish.world.spec.ts`: fresh upload, accept (dir and the r2 fake runner, HEAD archive never called), accept on the lock hash, mismatched packs and another bake, missing index, partial offer. Reversible: drop the read and the offer step re-archives HEAD as before. | `tools/assets/src/publish.ts`, `tools/assets/src/uploader-rclone.ts`, `tools/assets/src/uploader-dir.ts`, `tools/assets/test/publish.world.spec.ts`, `docs/runbooks/r2-media.md` §7 |
